1a  N=4 (and deformed) spectrum from Feynman graphs / supergraphs in DR-type schemes
====================================================================================================

[S1a.01_FSSZ2007] F. Fiamberti, A. Santambrogio, C. Sieg, D. Zanon -- Wrapping at four loops in N=4 SYM (2007)
  arXiv: 0712.3522   Journal: Phys. Lett. B666 (2008) 100
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: First field-theory computation of the planar four-loop anomalous dimension of the length-4 Konishi descendant tr(phi[Z,phi]Z) in the SU(2) sector, the first order at which wrapping interactions contribute; wrapping produces zeta(5). Letter version; details in 0806.2095 (S1a.02).
  Loop order / theory: 4 (planar) / planar N=4 SYM, SU(N), SU(2) scalar sector, length L=4 operator
  Scheme: N=1 superspace Feynman supergraphs; D-algebra completed in 4 dimensions, remaining momentum integrals regulated dimensionally (1/eps poles), MS subtraction; non-wrapping part not computed from graphs but taken from the asymptotic four-loop dilatation operator (see S1a.02).
  Symmetry argument: Manifest N=1 superspace (only N=1 of the N=4 SUSY is manifest); SUSY of the regulator is implicit in the use of supersymmetric dimensional regularization/reduction after D-algebra; no explicit SUSY Ward identity check.
  Proved: -
  Assumed:
      - supersymmetric DR (D-algebra in 4d, integrals in D dims) preserves the N=4 structure at four loops for the operator renormalization constant
      - the asymptotic four-loop SU(2) dilatation operator (integrability-based, dressing coefficient beta=4 zeta(3)) is correct for the range-1..4 part
  Checked partially:
      - final number later agreed with the Luscher/integrability prediction (S1b.01) after corrections; see notes
  Evidence:
      - Abstract: wrapping first contributes at four loops for this operator and gives zeta(5)
      - arXiv comments field: v2 corrected the sign of one diagram, which changed the zeta(3) coefficient of the final result
  Red flags:
      - v1 result was wrong (sign of one diagram); corrected value only in v2/v3
      - the non-wrapping part is imported from an integrability-derived asymptotic dilatation operator rather than computed, so this is a hybrid field-theory/integrability result
      - only N=1 of the N=4 supersymmetry is manifest
  Cross-checks:
      - agrees after correction with Bajnok-Janik 0807.0399 (S1b.01) and with Velizhanin 0808.3832 (S1a.06)
      - disagrees with component computation of Keeler-Mann 0801.1661 (S1a.03)
  Access / confidence: abstract only (details verified in companion paper S1a.02, full text via PDF) / high
  Notes: Final number: gamma_4 = -2496 + 576 zeta(3) - 1440 zeta(5) in units lambda^4, lambda = g^2 N/(16 pi^2); see S1a.02 eq. (5.3)-(5.4).

[S1a.02_FSSZ2008] F. Fiamberti, A. Santambrogio, C. Sieg, D. Zanon -- Anomalous dimension with wrapping at four loops in N=4 SYM (2008)
  arXiv: 0806.2095   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B805 (2008) 231
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: Full details of the four-loop Konishi-descendant computation: gamma = 4 + 12 lambda - 48 lambda^2 + 336 lambda^3 + lambda^4 (-2496 + 576 zeta(3) - 1440 zeta(5)), lambda = g^2 N/(16 pi^2). Non-wrapping (range 1-4) contributions are taken from the asymptotic four-loop dilatation operator D_4; range-5 terms are subtracted at the level of chiral functions; all wrapping supergraphs are computed explicitly.
  Loop order / theory: 4 (planar) / planar N=4 SYM written in N=1 superspace, SU(N) gauge group (tr T_a T_b = delta_ab), SU(2) sector
  Scheme: N=1 superspace (conventions of Superspace, Gates-Grisaru-Rocek-Siegel), supersymmetric Fermi-Feynman gauge (<VV> = -1/p^2). D-algebra on supergraphs; resulting integrals in dimensional regularization (poles in 1/eps), MS scheme. Pole parts of four-loop propagator-type integrals by the Gegenbauer polynomial x-space technique (GPXT), checked by uniqueness and by MINCER for three-loop subintegrals. IR: external momentum set to zero and an IR cutoff R on the radial x-space integration, also used for the subdivergences (an IR-rearrangement argument: pole parts of log-divergent subtracted integrals are momentum independent). Anomalous dimension from the 1/eps pole: gamma = lim eps g d/dg log Z.
  Symmetry argument: Manifest N=1 superspace; a power-counting/Euler-formula lemma (Sec. 2.1) shows only supergraphs whose spinor derivatives stay inside loops can diverge, which removes most graphs. Supersymmetry of the regulator itself is not discussed beyond working in superspace with D-algebra in 4d. Multiplicative renormalizability of tr(phi Z phi Z - phi phi Z Z) is stated to hold to all orders (exact eigenvector of the mixing).
  Proved:
      - Sec. 2.1 lemma (eqs. (2.14)-(2.17)): divergent contributions require all spinor derivatives to remain in loops (combinatorial, regulator independent)
      - Appendix A: non-maximal range-5 interactions do not contribute, so the range-5 subtraction can be read off from D_4 (eq. (3.7))
  Assumed:
      - the asymptotic SU(2) four-loop dilatation operator D_4 of eq. (3.1), taken from the literature, with dressing-phase coefficient beta = 4 zeta(3) (integrability input, not a Feynman-graph result)
      - DR applied after 4d D-algebra preserves supersymmetry at four loops for this quantity
      - IR cutoff prescription does not change pole parts after subtraction of subdivergences (argued, Sec. 4.2)
      - GPXT applicability: authors state they cannot prove it in general at higher loops (Sec. 4.2)
  Checked partially:
      - range-5 part of D_4 recomputed by explicit supergraphs (Appendix B); yields MS values eps_3a=-4, eps_3b=-4i/3, eps_3c=4i/3 (eq. (3.8))
      - rational part corrected after mismatch with an independent result (footnote to eq. (4.8))
  Evidence:
      - eq. (1.1): lambda = g^2 N/(16 pi^2)
      - eq. (1.2)-(1.3): gamma(O) = lim_{eps->0}[eps g d/dg log Z_O], O_ren = Z_O O_bare
      - eq. (2.1): N=4 action in N=1 superspace; eq. (2.5): superfield propagators
      - Sec. 2.1, eqs. (2.14)-(2.17): spinor-derivative lemma
      - Sec. 3, eq. (3.1): asymptotic D_4 with similarity-transformation parameters eps_3a..eps_3d and beta = 4 zeta(3) from the dressing phase
      - eq. (3.8): MS values of eps_3a, eps_3b, eps_3c from explicit range-5 graphs (Appendix B)
      - eq. (3.14): subtracted asymptotic part 4(121 + 12 zeta(3)) M on the L=4 basis
      - Sec. 4.2: GPXT, IR cutoff R, MINCER and uniqueness checks
      - eq. (4.12)-(4.13): wrapping dilatation operator, -8(17/2 + 18 zeta(3) - 30 zeta(5)) M
      - eq. (5.3)-(5.4): gamma_4 = -2496 + 576 zeta(3) - 1440 zeta(5)
      - Table C.8: pole parts of the twelve four-loop integrals I_1..I_12
  Red flags:
      - hybrid result: about half of the four-loop number comes from an integrability-based asymptotic dilatation operator; a complete Feynman-graph evaluation was not done
      - two corrections between versions: missing symmetry factor 2 found only after comparison with an independent result (footnote 1, p. 15) and a sign change in the chi(1) wrapping term (footnote 2, p. 18)
      - scheme statement is only implicit (MS with 1/eps poles after 4d D-algebra); no discussion of eps-scalars/evanescent issues or of the Siegel inconsistency
      - only the 1/eps pole of log Z is used; consistency of higher poles is a check but not reported in full
      - GPXT applicability at higher loops not proven (Sec. 4.2)
  Cross-checks:
      - agrees with Bajnok-Janik Luscher computation 0807.0399 (S1b.01)
      - agrees with Velizhanin component computation 0808.3832 (S1a.06)
      - disagrees with Keeler-Mann 0801.1661 (S1a.03), whose component calculation the authors say still differs after fixing a trivial error
  Access / confidence: full text via PDF (pp. 1-18 and Table C.8 read) / high
  Notes: Reference [31] for D_4 not opened; it is presumably Beisert-McLoughlin-Roiban (four-loop dressing phase), which in turn fixed beta using the four-loop cusp anomalous dimension computed in DR (BCDKS hep-th/0610248). The superspace D-algebra is done in 4d before continuing integrals, i.e. Siegel-type supersymmetric DR; the paper does not discuss its consistency.

[S1a.03_KeelerMann2008] C. A. Keeler, N. Mann -- Wrapping interactions and the Konishi operator (2008)
  arXiv: 0801.1661   Journal: unpublished (arXiv only, single version)
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: Four-loop Konishi (SU(2) sector) anomalous dimension from the known asymptotic four-loop dilatation operator plus recomputation, in component fields, of the diagrams that change from L>4 to L=4; result contains zeta(5) but differs from other proposals and calculations.
  Loop order / theory: 4 (planar) / planar N=4 SYM, SU(2) sector
  Scheme: component-field Feynman diagrams with dimensional regularization (details not verified); same hybrid strategy as S1a.02 (asymptotic D_4 + wrapping diagrams)
  Symmetry argument: none beyond the asymptotic dilatation operator input; supersymmetry not manifest in components
  Proved: -
  Assumed:
      - asymptotic four-loop dilatation operator
      - that the component regularization preserves the needed supersymmetry
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Abstract: result differs from previous proposals and calculations
      - arXiv record: only v1, no journal reference
  Red flags:
      - result disagrees with the later consensus value (S1a.02, S1a.06, S1b.01) and was never corrected or published
      - FSSZ (S1a.02, Introduction) state that even after correcting a trivial final-step error the result differs, and point to the size of the component calculation and non-manifest supersymmetry
  Cross-checks:
      - disagrees with S1a.02 and S1b.01
  Access / confidence: abstract only (plus discussion in 0806.2095 Introduction) / medium
  Notes: Included as a documented example of a component-field four-loop computation in N=4 that failed; the scheme used inside it was not verified.

[S1a.04_FSSZ2008beta] F. Fiamberti, A. Santambrogio, C. Sieg, D. Zanon -- Single impurity operators at critical wrapping order in the beta-deformed N=4 SYM (2008)
  arXiv: 0811.4594   Journal: JHEP 0908 (2009) 034
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: Anomalous dimensions of the one-impurity operators O_{1,L} = tr(phi Z^{L-1}) in the planar superconformal (real beta, h hbar = g^2) beta-deformed theory, including wrapping at the critical order K = L, obtained explicitly up to L = 11 loops (v2). Wrapping plus range-(L+1) subtraction give purely transcendental terms; the highest is zeta(2L-3) (Table 1).
  Loop order / theory: L loops for length L, explicit for L <= 11 (this is the highest loop order reached by a DR-type Feynman-graph spectral computation in this family) / planar beta-deformed N=4 SYM (N=1 superconformal, real beta, h hbar = g^2), SU(N), SU(2) sector, single-impurity operators
  Scheme: N=1 superspace, Fermi-Feynman gauge, D-algebra in 4d, integrals in D = 4 - 2 eps (dimensional regularization of the scalar integrals), gamma from the 1/eps pole of log Z (eq. (1.3)). The surviving L-loop integrals P_L and I_L^(j) are free of subdivergences, so only simple poles enter (eq. (2.15)); they are evaluated with D-dimensional IBP triangle rules generalized to numerators (Appendix A.1, eqs. (A.1)-(A.5)) and GPXT for L up to 11 (Appendix A.3).
  Symmetry argument: Manifest N=1 superspace; cancellations among wrapping supergraphs derived from the D-algebra lemma; asymptotic (non-wrapping) part taken from an all-loop formula for long single-impurity states derived earlier in superspace (eq. (1.5)); conformality of the deformed theory taken from the literature (real beta, h hbar = g^2).
  Proved:
      - for this operator class only a few supergraph classes survive (Sec. 2, Fig. 10), reducing the L-loop computation to P_L and I_L^(j) (eqs. (2.12)-(2.15))
  Assumed:
      - asymptotic all-loop dimension gamma_as = -1 + sqrt(1 + 16 lambda sin^2(pi beta)) for long single-impurity states (eq. (1.5)), taken from Mauri-Penati-Santambrogio-Zanon
      - planar superconformal invariance of the real-beta deformed theory with h hbar = g^2 to all orders (eq. (1.2), cited)
      - supersymmetric DR (4d D-algebra + D-dim integrals) is adequate; no discussion of regulator-induced SUSY breaking (here harmless at the level of simple poles of primitive integrals)
  Checked partially:
      - closed-form guesses for I_L^(1), I_L^(2) (eqs. (3.2)-(3.3)) verified up to L = 11
      - Luscher/twisted-S-matrix integrability computations reproduce critical-wrapping single-impurity results (reported in the integrability review 1012.3998; individual comparison not opened here)
  Evidence:
      - eq. (1.1)-(1.2): deformed superpotential and conformality condition h hbar = g^2
      - eq. (1.3): gamma = lim eps g d/dg log Z (dimensional regularization)
      - eq. (1.5) and (2.6): asymptotic all-loop result gamma_L^as = alpha_L lambda^L sin^{2L}(pi beta)
      - eqs. (2.7)-(2.8): range-(L+1) subtraction diagram S_L
      - eq. (2.15): delta gamma_L in terms of simple poles of P_L and I_L^(j)
      - eq. (3.1): P_L pole ~ (2/L) binom(2L-3, L-1) zeta(2L-3)
      - Sec. 4 and Table 1: all I_L^(j) computed up to L = 11; transcendental content up to zeta(19) at L = 11
      - arXiv comment: v2 completed results up to 11 loops
  Red flags:
      - gauge group SU(N) used in eq. (2.2); later work (Fokken-Sieg-Wilhelm, S1a.08/S1a.09) showed that in deformed theories the U(N) vs SU(N) choice and induced double-trace couplings matter for short operators (L = 2), though L >= 3 single-trace results in the planar limit are not affected in the same way
      - hybrid: non-wrapping part from an all-loop asymptotic formula, not recomputed
      - the 11-loop statement concerns only the critical-wrapping correction of a one-magnon state, built from subdivergence-free integrals; it is not a test of DR beyond simple poles
  Cross-checks:
      - integrability (twisted S-matrix Luscher formula) reproductions reported in the integrability review arXiv:1012.3998; Bajnok-el Deeb 1010.5606 extend to six loops for L=... beyond critical order (not opened)
  Access / confidence: full text via PDF (pp. 1-5, 9-13) / high
  Notes: Companion earlier paper 0806.2103 (JHEP 0808:057) computed one- and two-impurity beta-deformed operators to four loops (abstract only, no separate record). Candidate source of high-loop-order claims ('11 loops') in a DR-type Feynman graph computation.

[S1a.06_Velizhanin2008] V. N. Velizhanin -- The four-loop Konishi in N=4 SYM (2008)
  arXiv: 0808.3832   Journal: JETP Lett. 89 (2009) 6
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: Full direct component-field computation (about 131,000 planar four-loop diagrams) of the four-loop anomalous dimension of the Konishi operator, using its lowest component tr(phibar_i phi^i); result gamma = 12g^2 - 48g^4 + 336g^6 + (-2496 + 576 zeta(3) - 1440 zeta(5)) g^8 (eq. (5)), in full agreement with the superspace result (S1a.02) and the Luscher computation (S1b.01). By-product: four-loop planar scalar-field anomalous dimension (eq. (6)).
  Loop order / theory: 4 (planar) / planar N=4 SYM, component fields, Konishi primary (scalar bilinear)
  Scheme: dimensional reduction (Siegel) in components, Feynman gauge; operator inserted in vertex-type diagrams with momentum through the operator; infrared rearrangement with R* (one external momentum set to zero, propagators expanded, a common mass given to all lines, i.e. massive tadpoles) with added counterterms for the induced gluon and scalar 'masses' (eqs. (3)-(4)); DIANA/QGRAF, FORM + COLOR, Laporta reduction with BAMBA, four-loop masters from Czakon; scalar field anomalous dimension subtracted.
  Symmetry argument: None proved: the paper relies on DR being SUSY preserving; it states that DR is expected to fail at some higher order, notes that DR was found to work through three loops for three-point vertices, and hopes it holds at four loops for propagator-type diagrams.
  Proved: -
  Assumed:
      - component dimensional reduction (with Feynman-gauge eps-scalar sector, details not given) preserves supersymmetry for this four-loop operator renormalization
      - common-mass IRR with mass counterterms does not spoil gauge invariance of the pole parts (standard R* argument)
  Checked partially:
      - agreement with the independent superspace (S1a.02) and integrability (S1b.01) results at four loops
      - absence of zeta(2), zeta(4) and non-reciprocity constants in eq. (5) used as a consistency check
      - part of the four-loop pure-YM gluon anomalous dimension reproduced
  Evidence:
      - eq. (1)-(2): Konishi superfield and its lowest component tr(A_i A^i + B_i B^i)
      - eqs. (3)-(4): mass counterterms needed by the IRR with a common mass
      - eq. (5): four-loop Konishi anomalous dimension
      - eq. (6): four-loop planar scalar anomalous dimension
      - text: 131015 four-loop diagrams in Feynman gauge
  Red flags:
      - no SUSY Ward identity or beta = 0 check inside this paper (beta = 0 at four loops was checked later, S1a.11)
      - eps-scalar sector not described explicitly in this paper
      - author stresses that DR should be violated at higher loops, so the four-loop agreement is an empirical check, not a theorem
  Cross-checks:
      - agrees with Fiamberti-Santambrogio-Sieg-Zanon 0712.3522/0806.2095 (S1a.01, S1a.02)
      - agrees with Bajnok-Janik 0807.0399 (S1b.01)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (summarised) / high
  Notes: This is the only completely diagrammatic four-loop Konishi computation (no integrability input); together with S1a.02 (hybrid superspace) and S1b.01 (Luscher) it makes four loops the order at which a DR-type scheme and integrability agree on a wrapping-sensitive quantity.

[S1a.07_Sieg2010] C. Sieg -- Superspace calculation of the three-loop dilatation operator of N=4 SYM theory (2010)
  arXiv: 1008.3351   Journal: Phys. Rev. D84 (2011) 045014
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: Direct N=1 superspace Feynman-graph computation of the full planar three-loop dilatation operator in the flavour SU(2) subsector; transcendental (zeta(3)) pieces cancel; the result fixes the coefficients h_2, h_3 of the magnon dispersion and the magnon-scattering deformation s to the integrability values, i.e. proves three-loop integrability in this subsector from field theory.
  Loop order / theory: 3 (planar), full operator in the SU(2) subsector / planar N=4 SYM with SU(N), N=1 superfield formulation, Fermi-Feynman gauge
  Scheme: dimensional reduction in D = 4 - 2 eps (explicitly stated, Sec. 2, ref. [62]); N=1 superspace D-algebra; Z defined by O_ren = Z O_bare (eq. (2.1)); D = mu d/dmu ln Z = lim 2 eps lambda d/dlambda ln Z (eq. (2.4)), i.e. 2K times the 1/eps pole at K loops; KR subtraction of subdivergences (K extracts poles, R subtracts subdivergences, Sec. 5); higher poles of ln Z shown to cancel (Appendix D).
  Symmetry argument: Uses N=4 finiteness of the elementary chiral self-energy (to three loops, in N=1 Fermi-Feynman gauge; cited) and power-counting 'finiteness conditions' derived from the N=1 superspace Feynman rules (Appendix B) to discard whole classes of graphs. Sec. 4 relates the protection of tr(Z^L), tr(phi Z^{L-1}) to the preservation of conformal invariance at the quantum level (only the finite chiral self-energy could contribute chi()).
  Proved:
      - finiteness conditions for supergraphs in the SU(2) sector (Appendix B; power counting from the D-algebra) valid for N=4, beta-deformed and N=6 CS
      - cancellation of higher-order poles in ln Z at three loops (Appendix D) = internal consistency of the local renormalization
      - all coefficients of D_3 determined; h_2 = h_3 = s = 0 (Sec. 3, eq. (3.6)-(3.7) and Sec. 6)
  Assumed:
      - dimensional reduction preserves the N=1 superspace structure at three loops (no evanescent/eps-scalar analysis)
      - finiteness of the chiral self-energy up to three loops in Fermi-Feynman gauge, taken from earlier superspace literature
  Checked partially:
      - one- and two-loop dilatation operator rederived (Sec. 5, eqs. (5.1)-(5.3))
      - Konishi three-loop eigenvalue agrees with earlier field-theory results
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2, p. 4: eps is the regulator of dimensional reduction in D = 4 - 2 eps
      - eq. (2.1): O_ren = Z O_bare; eq. (2.2): chiral functions; eq. (2.4): D = lim 2 eps lambda d ln Z/d lambda
      - eq. (3.1): integrability prediction for D_1..D_3 (with unphysical similarity parameter eps_2)
      - eq. (3.6)-(3.7): deformed dilatation operator with h_2, h_3, s and Konishi eigenvalue 12g^2 - (48 - 12 h_2) g^4 + ...
      - Sec. 4: finiteness conditions; protection of BPS states tied to conformal invariance
      - Sec. 5, eq. (5.3): Z_2 with KR(I_2)
      - Sec. 6, eqs. (6.1)-(6.2): maximum-range diagrams
      - Appendix D: cancellation of higher poles
  Red flags:
      - only the SU(2) subsector (scalars), so fermion/derivative operators and the full psu(2,2|4) structure are not tested
      - supersymmetry of the regulator is not analysed; dimensional reduction is invoked by citation only
      - finiteness of the elementary self-energy is imported, not recomputed
  Cross-checks:
      - reproduces three-loop integrability predictions (Beisert-Kristjansen-Staudacher, hep-th/0303060) and Konishi three-loop value (Kotikov-Lipatov-Onishchenko-Velizhanin hep-th/0404092; Eden-Jarczak-Sokatchev hep-th/0409009)
  Access / confidence: full text via PDF (pp. 2-10) / high
  Notes: Genuinely scheme-level DR result (not using integrability input) at three loops in a closed scalar subsector; this is the strongest pure-Feynman-graph spectral confirmation found in this survey beyond two loops.

[S1a.08_FSW2013nonconf] J. Fokken, C. Sieg, M. Wilhelm -- Non-conformality of gamma_i-deformed N=4 SYM theory (2013)
  arXiv: 1308.4420   Journal: J. Phys. A47 (2014) 455401
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: The non-supersymmetric three-parameter gamma_i-deformation of N=4 SYM is not conformal: a double-trace coupling is induced and runs, also in the 't Hooft limit; adding tree-level multi-trace couplings with minimal consistency requirements does not restore conformality. Suggested link to a divergence of integrability-based two-loop finite-size corrections for tr(Z^2).
  Loop order / theory: one-loop running of induced double-trace coupling (from abstract; loop order of the explicit computation not verified) / gamma_i-deformed N=4 SYM (non-supersymmetric), planar limit
  Scheme: Feynman diagrams with dimensional regularization (details not verified from full text)
  Symmetry argument: Shows that conformal invariance assumed in integrability-based treatments of deformed theories fails because of double-trace running; a DR-based computation is used to detect symmetry breaking rather than to assume protection.
  Proved:
      - running of an induced double-trace coupling in the gamma_i-deformed theory, surviving the planar limit (per abstract)
  Assumed: -
  Checked partially:
      - proposed (not completed in this paper) test of the link to the divergent two-loop finite-size integrability result
  Evidence:
      - Abstract (paraphrased): running double-trace coupling breaks conformal invariance even at large N; multi-trace completion does not cure it
  Red flags:
      - shows that 'planar = single-trace only' reasoning can miss double-trace couplings that contribute to planar two-point functions of short operators (L=2)
      - integrability (Y-system/TBA) predictions for deformed theories rest on conformality assumed, not derived
  Cross-checks:
      - continued in Fokken-Sieg-Wilhelm 1405.6712 (S1a.10) and Sieg-Wilhelm 1602.05817 (fishnet-type limit also non-conformal; complex double-trace fixed points at one loop)
  Access / confidence: abstract only / medium
  Notes: Sieg-Wilhelm, PLB 756 (2016) 118, arXiv:1602.05817 (abstract read): the integrable non-unitary limit of the gamma_i-deformation is incomplete and not conformal even in the planar limit; completing it with double-trace couplings gives conformal one-loop fixed points only for complex couplings. Parent check (abstract, arXiv v3 31 Mar 2015): non-conformality holds even in the t Hooft limit; tree-level multi-trace couplings satisfying minimal consistency requirements do not cure it; abstract suggests a link to the two-loop finite-size divergence seen in integrability for a two-site chiral operator.

[S1a.11_Velizhanin2010beta] V. N. Velizhanin -- Vanishing of the four-loop charge renormalization function in N=4 SYM theory (2010)
  arXiv: 1008.2198   Journal: Phys. Lett. B696 (2011) 560
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: Four-loop renormalization constants of N=4 SYM in dimensional reduction: field anomalous dimensions of gluon, ghost, scalar, fermion and the ghost-ghost-gluon and fermion-fermion-scalar vertex anomalous dimensions (eqs. (5)-(10)); the beta function extracted from either vertex vanishes at four loops (eq. (11)), i.e. gauge and Yukawa couplings renormalize identically through four loops.
  Loop order / theory: 4 (full colour as far as SU(N) Casimirs; planarity not restricted in the summary) / N=4 SYM, component fields
  Scheme: dimensional reduction: Dirac algebra in four dimensions, 4 - 2 eps gauge-field components and 3 + eps scalars plus 3 + eps pseudoscalars (6 + 2 eps real scalar components including the eps-scalars) so that the field count matches SUSY; IRR to massless propagator integrals; Laporta reduction (BAMBA); FORM, COLOR, DIANA/QGRAF; about 726,000 four-loop diagrams (Table 1).
  Symmetry argument: SUSY of DR tested a posteriori: beta extracted from the gauge vertex (ghost-ghost-gluon) and from the Yukawa vertex (fermion-fermion-scalar) must coincide (and vanish) if SUSY holds; eq. (4): beta_jjk = g^2 (2 gamma_jjk - 2 gamma_j - gamma_k).
  Proved:
      - beta = 0 at four loops from both vertices in this DR implementation (eq. (11)) - an explicit four-loop check of the gauge/Yukawa coupling equality required by SUSY
  Assumed:
      - absence of evanescent eps-scalar coupling splitting (the eps-scalar couplings are set equal to the gauge coupling)
  Checked partially:
      - only two vertex functions; no check of quartic scalar vertex, of composite operators or of full SUSY Ward identities
      - gauge-parameter independence of the printed results not discussed
  Evidence:
      - eq. (3): coupling renormalization from vertex and field constants
      - eq. (4): beta from a vertex
      - eqs. (5)-(10): four-loop gamma functions
      - eq. (11): beta^(4-loop) = 0
      - Table 1: diagram counts (about 726,000)
      - Introduction: cites Siegel PLB 94 (1980) 37 for internal contradictions; Avdeev PLB 117 (1982) 317 for gauge and Yukawa beta functions that 'do not coincide starting from three loops' in N=1 and N=2 SYM; Avdeev-Chochia-Vladimirov PLB 105 (1981) 272 and Avdeev-Vladimirov NPB 219 (1983) 262 for the estimate that DR should work for N=4 up to five loops for propagator-type diagrams (Table 1 of Avdeev 1982)
  Red flags:
      - the author's own summary of the earlier literature says DRED fails for the gauge/Yukawa equality at three loops in N=1/N=2 SYM (Avdeev 1982) - this should be cross-checked against later N=1 three-loop DRED results (Jack-Jones et al., covered in S1e/S2)
      - the five-loop 'safe' estimate for N=4 is a counting estimate of where Levi-Civita/Fierz ambiguities can first enter propagator-type graphs, not a proof
      - a vanishing beta function is necessary, not sufficient, for SUSY of the scheme
  Cross-checks:
      - consistent with the classic three-loop results: Avdeev-Tarasov-Vladimirov 1980 (S1a.15, components DR) and Grisaru-Rocek-Siegel 1980 (S1a.16, superspace)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (summarised; Introduction sentences checked) / high
  Notes: Correct arXiv id is 1008.2198 (the lead 1008.2911 was wrong). Highest-loop explicit check of DRED SUSY consistency for N=4 found in this section.

  Notes: Highest-spin four-loop non-planar DR-type computation in N=4 found; shows the component DRED toolchain is still used for N=4 operator renormalization at four loops in 2024-2025.

[S1a.15_ATV1980] L. V. Avdeev, O. V. Tarasov, A. A. Vladimirov (detailed version: O. V. Tarasov, A. A. Vladimirov, JINR E2-80-483) -- Vanishing of the three-loop charge renormalization function in a supersymmetric gauge theory (1980)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv); detailed JINR preprint E2-80-483 posted as 1301.5645   Journal: Phys. Lett. B96 (1980) 94
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: Three-loop beta function of the SU(4)-supersymmetric (N=4) gauge theory vanishes, as at one and two loops; the standard (non-supersymmetric) dimensional regularization would instead give a non-zero three-loop value (8 N^3 h^4 in their normalization).
  Loop order / theory: 3 / N=4 SYM, component fields
  Scheme: 'supersymmetric dimensional regularization' in components (preprint 1301.5645): Dirac algebra written in 4 - 2 eps with defining relations kept in four-dimensional form; numbers of scalars and pseudoscalars continued to 3 + eps each so that bosonic and fermionic components match (counting argument: 8 spinor components = (2 - 2 eps) vector + (3 + eps) + (3 + eps)); internal alpha/beta matrices adjusted accordingly; beta from ghost-ghost-gluon vertex and propagators, beta = h[2 gamma~_1 - gamma_3 - 2 gamma~_3].
  Symmetry argument: a posteriori: at two loops two methods of computing beta agree only in the supersymmetric scheme, the standard scheme gives a different value by one method and is judged non-invariant; authors state there is no rigorous proof of validity of the regularization and call for a symmetry-based argument.
  Proved:
      - beta = 0 at three loops in this scheme (preprint eq. (57))
  Assumed:
      - the component supersymmetric dimensional regularization preserves SUSY at three loops
  Checked partially:
      - two-loop comparison of two ways of computing beta (scheme consistency check)
  Evidence:
      - 1301.5645 Appendix: Dirac algebra in 4 - 2 eps with 4d defining relations
      - 1301.5645: delta^{rr} = 3 + eps for scalars and pseudoscalars
      - 1301.5645 eq. (57): beta(h) = 0 at three loops; standard regularization gives 8 N^3 h^4
  Red flags:
      - explicitly no proof that the regularization is supersymmetric (stated by the authors)
      - only vertex-based beta; Yukawa-vertex check came later (Velizhanin, S1a.19)
  Cross-checks:
      - Grisaru-Rocek-Siegel PRL 45 (1980) 1063 and Caswell-Zanon NPB 182 (1981) 125 in superspace (S1a.16)
      - Velizhanin 0809.2509 (S1a.19) recomputed three loops in arbitrary gauge
  Access / confidence: secondary: PLB abstract via INSPIRE (search snippet); detailed preprint 1301.5645 full text via ar5iv (summarised) / medium
  Notes: Author list of the PLB article per INSPIRE: Avdeev, Tarasov, Vladimirov; the preprint note mentions additional co-authors (Zharkov) for related publications - check the PLB title page before citing.

[S1a.17_KLV2003] A. V. Kotikov, L. N. Lipatov, V. N. Velizhanin -- Anomalous dimensions of Wilson operators in N=4 SYM theory (2003)
  arXiv: hep-ph/0301021   Journal: Phys. Lett. B557 (2003) 114
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: Two-loop anomalous-dimension matrix of twist-two operators (unpolarized and polarized) in N=4 SYM, triangularized by the leading-order similarity transformation; eigenvalues are a universal function with shifted arguments (eq. (18) with Q-hat of eq. (8)).
  Loop order / theory: 2 / N=4 SYM, twist-two operators (quark/gluino, gluon, scalar)
  Scheme: Diagrams computed in MS-bar (conventional DREG) with DIANA/QGRAF, MINCER, FORM, following the QCD calculations of Hamberg-van Neerven and Mertig-van Neerven; polarized case with a 'reading point' gamma5 prescription plus a Larin-type finite renormalization restoring anticommutativity; then converted to DR-bar (Siegel) using the one-loop finite-part differences (Antoniadis-Floratos), plus an additional coupling redefinition alpha_s -> alpha_s(1 - alpha_s N_c/(12 pi)) to reach the universal form.
  Symmetry argument: MS-bar is stated to violate SUSY; DR-bar is described as preserving SUSY at least at two loops (cited, not proved); superconformal invariance is said to break at two loops, requiring a slightly modified operator basis for diagonalization.
  Proved:
      - two-loop matrix triangular in the LO similarity basis; eigenvalue relations eqs. (19)-(27) (stated to hold to all orders after replacing the two-loop function by the exact gamma)
  Assumed:
      - DR-bar preserves SUSY at two loops
      - MS-bar -> DR-bar conversion fully captured by one-loop finite differences (Antoniadis-Floratos argument)
      - reading-point gamma5 + Larin-type finite renormalization is consistent for the polarized operators
  Checked partially:
      - agreement with earlier Born-level and partial results
  Evidence:
      - eq. (8): definition of Q-hat(j) in harmonic sums
      - eq. (13)-(17): eigenvalues Q-hat(j-2), Q-hat(j), Q-hat(j+2) (unpolarized), Q-hat(j-1), Q-hat(j+1) (polarized)
      - eq. (18): gamma(j) = -(alpha_s N_c/pi) S_1(j-2) + (alpha_s N_c/4 pi)^2 Q-hat(j-2)
      - text: MS-bar to DR-bar conversion via one-loop finite parts; coupling redefinition removing the S_1 term
  Red flags:
      - the N=4 result is obtained in a non-supersymmetric scheme (MS-bar) and converted; this conversion assumes that the scheme difference is a finite renormalization of coupling/operators determined at one loop
      - the polarized case relies on a reading-point gamma5 prescription (KKS-type) - a gamma5 subtlety inside an N=4 computation
      - statement that superconformal invariance breaks down at two loops in the computation means the conformal (similarity) basis has to be corrected - an instance of DR-induced conformal-symmetry breaking (beta-like term from d != 4)
  Cross-checks:
      - used as the two-loop check for KLOV (S1a.18) and for the ABA predictions
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (summarised) / medium
  Notes: Relevant for the gamma5 synthesis: even inside an N=4 two-loop computation a reading-point prescription was used for the polarized operators.

[S1a.20_ChakrabortyMoch2026] M. Chakraborty, S.-O. Moch -- Dimensional reduction is supersymmetric at three loops (2026)
  arXiv: 2603.02892   Journal: preprint (PUBDB-2026-00811)
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: The long-standing claim that DR breaks SUSY at three loops (mismatch of Yukawa and ghost-gluon beta functions in N=2 SYM; Avdeev-Tarasov 1982, Velizhanin 2008) stems from the treatment of Levi-Civita terms in flavour and spinor traces, not from DR itself; with these terms kept, beta_ffs = beta_ccg for N=1, 2, 4 at three loops. A non-zero flavour Levi-Civita trace requires six flavour matrices, which for N=4 first occurs at five loops; four-loop results are consistent. New four-loop N=1 Yukawa beta function.
  Loop order / theory: 3 (N=1,2,4); 4 (N=1 gauge and Yukawa, N=4; QCD check) / N=1, N=2, N=4 SYM from a generalized Lagrangian with n_s scalars and n_f fermions
  Scheme: DR realised as analytic continuation n_s -> n_s + 2 eps of a Lagrangian with Euclidean flavour Clifford algebra Z^I Zbar^J + Z^J Zbar^I = 2 delta^{IJ} (eq. (4)); Levi-Civita (chiral) parts regularised by a 'simplified regularisation' with integer-dimensional indices and an O(eps) remainder (eq. (10)); spinor traces with Wick-rotated Pauli matrices and a four-index epsilon tensor (eq. (11)); MS-bar, IRR for vertices, general covariant gauge; Qgraf, FORM, FORCER.
  Symmetry argument: a posteriori equality of beta functions from different vertices; gauge-parameter independence; the 'five-loop' statement for N=4 is a diagram-structure argument (first appearance of a non-vanishing flavour epsilon-trace).
  Proved:
      - the N=2 three-loop mismatch is removed once the Levi-Civita contribution of the fermion-scalar Yukawa triangle is kept (eq. (15)); beta_ffs = beta_ccg for N=1, 2, 4 at three loops
      - Table 1 of the earlier literature reproduced by dropping Levi-Civita terms (diagnosis)
  Assumed:
      - the 'simplified regularisation' of Levi-Civita terms is consistent (it is a prescription, not a theorem)
      - that no Levi-Civita ambiguity enters N=4 below five loops (argued from flavour-trace structure)
  Checked partially:
      - four-loop QCD (N=0) reproduced; four-loop N=1 beta_ccg agrees with literature; four-loop N=4 matches Velizhanin (S1a.11); N=2 four loops deferred
      - Weyl consistency conditions proposed for future checks
  Evidence:
      - eq. (4): flavour Clifford algebra
      - eq. (9): Tr[Z^I Zbar^J] for n_s = 2 contains i eps^{IJ}
      - eq. (10)-(11): regularisation of Levi-Civita terms and Pauli traces
      - eq. (13): Levi-Civita terms survive only with chiral parts in both traces
      - eq. (15): Yukawa-triangle Levi-Civita contribution cancelling the N=2 error
      - Fig. 3: five-loop N=4 topology with six flavour matrices
      - Table 1: earlier beta_ccg vs beta_ffs values
  Red flags:
      - the claimed SUSY preservation depends on a specific prescription for Levi-Civita tensors in d dimensions - structurally the same issue as gamma5 in the SM (KKS reading point / Larin / BMHV)
      - five-loop N=4 statement is a prediction of where ambiguity can enter, not a demonstration of consistency or of failure
      - evanescent couplings are dismissed by citation ('SYM has no evanescent interaction terms')
  Cross-checks:
      - resolves the N=2 discrepancy of Velizhanin 0809.2509 (S1a.19) and Avdeev-Tarasov 1982
      - consistent with four-loop N=4 beta = 0 (S1a.11)
  Access / confidence: full text via arxiv.org/html (summarised) / high
  Notes: Most recent (2026) primary statement on the loop order at which DRED can fail in N=4: five loops, via flavour Levi-Civita traces.
  ALSO RECORDED AS S2.13_ChakrabortyMoch2026 (section 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars); that record, verbatim:
    [S2.13_ChakrabortyMoch2026] M. Chakraborty, S.-O. Moch -- Dimensional reduction is supersymmetric at three loops (2026)

      Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
      Claim: The three-loop mismatch between the Yukawa (ffs) and ghost-gluon (ccg) vertex beta functions in N=2 SYM in DR (Avdeev 1982; Velizhanin 2008, Table 1) is not intrinsic to DR: it arises from Levi-Civita terms in flavour traces (eqs. 8-9) and Pauli traces (eq. 11) that survive only when both the spinor and the flavour trace have chiral parts (eq. 13); keeping them (eq. 15) cancels the O(a^4) mismatch.       - eq. (17)-(19): point-split definition and multiplicative renormalization
      - eq. (21)-(22): gamma_1, gamma_2
      - Sect. 6: 20' operator at Delta_0 = 4 unrenormalized
  Red flags:
      - the regularization defining the anomaly is not specified; mixing of the anomaly term with the classical term is scheme dependent beyond one loop (see S1a.22)
      - normalization of g differs by a factor 2 from some earlier papers (footnote 3)
  Cross-checks:
      - EJSS hep-th/0501077 (S1a.22) reinterpret the Konishi anomaly as operator mixing in SSDR
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (summarised) / medium
  Notes: A one-loop N=4 composite-operator datum (Konishi anomaly coefficient) whose scheme dependence beyond one loop is exactly the kind of quantity a BV/QME treatment of composite operators should fix.

[S1a.22_EJSS2005] B. Eden, C. Jarczak, E. Sokatchev, Y. S. Stanev -- Operator mixing in N=4 SYM: the Konishi anomaly revisited (2005)
  arXiv: hep-th/0501077   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B722 (2005) 119
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: In N=4 SYM the Konishi anomaly is the superconformal descendant K_10 of the Konishi multiplet; it is a mixture of a bare trilinear operator B and a bilinear 'quantum' operator F whose renormalized combination must carry the Konishi anomalous dimension; superconformal covariance truncates the anomaly equation to K_10 = a(g)[B]_R (eq. (3)); the protected combination is O_10 = -1/2 (F - 4B); the coefficient one would call the anomaly becomes divergent beyond one loop, so 'one-loop exactness of the Konishi anomaly' is not a meaningful question in a superconformal scheme. Explicit two-loop SSDR computation.
  Loop order / theory: 2 (explicit), structure of Z_O through g^6 (eq. (65)) / N=4 SYM in N=1 superspace
  Scheme: N=1 superspace Feynman rules with supersymmetric dimensional reduction (SSDR), coupling g-hat = g mu^eps (Sects. 1, 3.1); minimal subtraction (Sect. 3.2, eq. (45))
  Symmetry argument: superconformal symmetry of the renormalized operators fixes which operator can appear on the right-hand side of the anomaly equation; the anomalous dimension is scheme independent in a conformal theory, while mixing coefficients (finite part of Z_O) are not (Sect. 3.3)
  Proved:
      - explicit two-loop SSDR computation: the protected combination O_10 is not renormalized; the Konishi mixing coefficient becomes divergent (Sect. 3.3)
      - one-loop anomaly coefficient N/(32 pi^2) reproduced as first term of Z_O
  Assumed:
      - SSDR preserves the superconformal structure needed for the truncation argument
      - conformal covariance of renormalized operators (used to exclude F from the right-hand side)
  Checked partially:
      - protected two-point functions (O_20' and descendants) checked to O(g^4) (Sects. 2.2, 4.1)
  Evidence:
      - Introduction / eq. (3): truncated anomaly equation
      - eq. (1), (8): N=1 form of Dbar^2 K with one-loop coefficient
      - Sect. 3.2, eq. (45): MS renormalization in SSDR
      - Sect. 3.3: scheme (in)dependence statements; eq. (65): structure of Z_O to g^6
      - footnote 13: corrections to classical equations may be scheme dependent
  Red flags:
      - explicitly states that mixing coefficients and whether classical equations of motion receive quantum corrections are scheme dependent - a DR-type scheme does not by itself define the 'anomaly' beyond one loop
      - superconformal covariance of the renormalized operators in SSDR is assumed, not derived from a Ward identity of the regulated theory
  Cross-checks:
      - consistent with BKRS hep-th/0104016 (S1a.21) at one loop
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (first 100k characters, through Sect. 3.4) / medium
  Notes: Direct precedent for a one/two-loop N=4 composite-operator test of how a DR-type scheme realises superconformal symmetry on operators (Konishi descendant mixing).

[S1a.23_EJS2004] B. Eden, C. Jarczak, E. Sokatchev -- A three-loop test of the dilatation operator in N=4 SYM (2004)
  arXiv: hep-th/0409009   Journal: not shown in the fetched text (not verified)
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: Three-loop anomalous dimensions of the first two BMN two-impurity multiplets (Konishi, J=0, twist two; and the J=1, twist-three multiplet) from two-point functions of chiral descendants in N=1 superspace; Konishi reproduces the earlier three-loop value, J=1 is new; both equal the predictions of the dilatation operator compatible with BMN scaling.
  Loop order / theory: 3 (planar) / N=4 SYM in N=1 superspace
  Scheme: supersymmetric dimensional reduction (Sec. 3.1), MS-bar (mass-scale conventions in Appendix 6.3); Anselmi's trick (superspace differentiation) lowers the effective loop order by one (Sec. 2.1, eqs. (21)-(23)); ratio of two-point functions of two descendants (eqs. (8), (64)) to avoid normalization and quantum-anomaly problems; contact terms discarded as O(eps) at separated points (Sec. 2.3).
  Symmetry argument: Superconformal multiplet structure (descendants share gamma); protectedness of half-BPS operators used to drop graph sums that are O(eps) (eq. (110), Sec. 3.3.4); a coefficient f_21 fixed by requiring the ratio to be logarithm free (eq. (119)).
  Proved:
      - one-loop gamma_1 = 3N/(4 pi^2) reproduced (eq. (72))
      - three-loop Konishi and J=1 values in SSDR
  Assumed:
      - SSDR preserves the superconformal multiplet structure used in the ratio method
      - half-BPS protection used as input to discard contributions
      - some coefficients fixed by requiring logarithm-free ratios rather than computed
  Checked partially:
      - agreement with BMN-scaling-compatible dilatation operator (Beisert su(2|3), S1a.25)
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2.1, eqs. (21)-(23): Anselmi's trick
      - eqs. (8), (64): ratio of descendant two-point functions
      - Sec. 3.1: SSDR; Appendix 6.3: MS-bar conventions
      - eq. (72): gamma_1
      - eq. (110): use of half-BPS protection
      - eq. (119): coefficient fixed by absence of logarithms
      - footnote 12: two-loop propagator correction possibly scheme dependent
  Red flags:
      - protected-operator and superconformal-multiplet properties are used as inputs, so this is not an independent test of whether SSDR preserves superconformal symmetry
      - the 'quantum anomaly' of the Konishi multiplet is circumvented by the choice of ratio, i.e. the scheme-dependent anomaly issue (S1a.22) is avoided rather than resolved
  Cross-checks:
      - KLOV hep-th/0404092 (S1a.18) three-loop Konishi; Sieg 1008.3351 (S1a.07)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (first 100k characters) / medium
  Notes:

[S1a.25_Beisert2003su23] N. Beisert -- The su(2|3) dynamic spin chain (2003)
  arXiv: hep-th/0310252   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B682 (2004) 487
  Section: 1a N=4 spectrum via Feynman/supergraphs
  Claim: In the su(2|3) subsector the requirement that the perturbatively deformed su(2|3) symmetry algebra be respected fixes the planar energy shifts uniquely through three loops (confirming the BKS conjecture); the higher-loop Hamiltonian changes the number of sites ('dynamic' chain) and still appears integrable.
  Loop order / theory: 3 (planar) / N=4 SYM su(2|3) subsector
  Scheme: no regularization: algebraic construction of the deformed generators (interacting supercharges at O(g)) constrained by closure of the symmetry algebra; remaining freedom fixed by additional physical input (per EJS: a two-parameter freedom at three loops fixed by BMN scaling)
  Symmetry argument: Assumes that the quantum (renormalized) theory realizes su(2|3) on composite operators through coupling-dependent generators whose algebra closes order by order; this replaces a scheme-level derivation of the dilatation operator.
  Proved:
      - Within the algebraic ansatz: the deformed generators are constructed order by order by solving the closure constraints (eq. 1.5), and the planar energy shifts are fixed through three loops (abstract; Secs. 3-5).
  Assumed:
      - Identification of the deformed su(2|3) representation with the action on renormalized N=4 operators, H = D (eq. 2.30), up to similarity transformations (eq. 3.18) and a coupling redefinition (footnote 3); argued from field-theory structure and BMN scaling, not derived from a regulated theory.
      - Parity (eq. 2.12), planarity, Feynman-diagram leg counting (eq. 3.7) and iterated-one-loop structure of maximal-leg diagrams (Sec. 5).
      - BMN scaling (eqs. 6.3-6.4) for the coupling redefinition; integrability is not assumed (stated in Sec. 1).
  Checked partially:
      - agreement with three-loop field theory (S1a.23, S1a.07)
  Evidence:
      - Eq. (3.1): J(g) = sum_k g^k J_k, J_0 free representation; eq. (3.7): leg count n + m = k + 2 (planar interaction symbols).
      - Eq. (3.32): O(g) length-changing supercharges (Q_1)^a_alpha = c_9 eps_{alpha beta} eps^{abc} {beta; bc} (one fermion -> two bosons) and (S_1)^alpha_a = c_10 eps_{abc} eps^{alpha beta} {bc; beta}.
      - Eqs. (3.29)-(3.30): [Q_0, H_2] = 0 fixes the form of the one-loop Hamiltonian H_2 (c_1 = c_2 = c_3, c_4 = c_5 = -c_6); eq. (3.35): O(g^3) closure gives c_1 = -c_4.
      - Eq. (3.36) and text after (3.37): closure of {Q, S} at O(g^2) relates normalizations, c_9 c_10 = (1/2) c_1 with c_1 = alpha_1^2; eqs. (3.41)-(3.42) classical anticommutator and second-order relation.
      - Sec. 5, eqs. (5.3)-(5.4): maximal-leg diagrams = iterated one-loop diagrams used to set sigma_3 = sigma_4 = 0, zeta_1 = xi_1 = 0; eqs. (4.13)-(4.15): BPS energy integrality gives eps = 6 alpha_1^4; eqs. (6.3)-(6.4): BMN scaling fixes the coupling redefinition.
  Red flags:
      - the existence of a perturbatively deformed, closing su(2|3) algebra acting on renormalized operators is exactly what a regularization (DR/DRED) must deliver and is not derived here
  Cross-checks:
      - S1a.23 (EJS three-loop SSDR test), S1a.07
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (Secs. 1-5 and start of Sec. 6; updated by parent) / medium
  Notes: Directly relevant for a BV/QME programme: the O(g) correction to the supercharges acting on operators (length-changing) is the object whose existence and closure the QME/Ward identities should establish. Parent update: the g^2 closure ties the one-loop dilatation normalization c_1 to the product c_9 c_10 of the classical O(g) supercharge coefficients (eq. 3.36); in a BV formulation with composite-operator sources c_9, c_10 are tree-level antifield data and c_1 is the one-loop QME output.

[S1c.01_EHKS2011] B. Eden, P. Heslop, G.P. Korchemsky, E. Sokatchev -- Hidden symmetry of four-point correlation functions and amplitudes in N=4 SYM (2011)
  arXiv: 1108.3557   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B862 (2012) 193 (DOI 10.1016/j.nuclphysb.2012.04.007)
  Section: 1c correlator integrands
  Claim: The l-loop integrand f^(l) of the four-point function of stress-tensor multiplets, defined through l Lagrangian insertions into a Born-level (4+l)-point correlator, is totally symmetric under S_{4+l} (external and integration points exchanged). With this symmetry the three-loop integrand is fixed up to four constants for any gauge group; in the planar limit the correlator/amplitude duality fixes them; at four loops 32 S_8-invariant topologies remain and are fixed by the planar four-loop amplitude.
  Loop order / theory: integrand constructed to 4 loops; OPE (Konishi) check at 3 loops only / N=4 SYM, SU(Nc); four-point function of the 20' stress-tensor multiplet; planar limit used for coefficient fixing
  Scheme: No regulator for the integrand: f^(l) is a rational function on Euclidean 4d space, obtained from a Lagrangian-insertion formula written with d^4x. Integrals are used only where finite (Euclidean OPE coincidence limit, Sec. 4.2.1). D=4-2eps appears only in App. C as a device inside a Mellin-Barnes representation of a finite one-loop integral, followed by eps->0. Light-cone-limit integrals are divergent and are never evaluated; the duality with amplitudes is stated at integrand level only (Sec. 3.2).
  Symmetry argument: N=4 superconformal symmetry of the Born-level (4+l)-point correlator with l chiral Lagrangian insertions: the maximal nilpotent chiral superconformal invariant I_n (Grassmann degree 4(n-4), weight -2 at each point) is claimed unique and S_n symmetric (App. B, eq. B.4); together with crossing this forces S_{4+l} symmetry of f^(l) (eq. 2.20).
  Proved:
      - Given the Lagrangian-insertion representation and the uniqueness of the maximal nilpotent invariant: total S_{4+l} symmetry of f^(l) (eq. 2.20, App. B).
      - Two-loop integrand f^(2) uniquely fixed by the symmetry (eq. 2.21).
  Assumed:
      - Validity of the unregulated 4d Lagrangian-insertion formula a dG4/da = int d^4x5 <O...O L(x5)> (eq. 2.12) and its iteration (eqs. 2.13-2.14); contact terms only discussed in footnote 4.
      - Uniqueness of the maximal invariant is asserted (App. B), not proved in a separate step.
      - Denominator ansatz prod x_ij^2 to first power justified by OPE pole arguments (Sec. 3.1).
      - Planar correlator/amplitude duality (conjectural at that time) used to fix the remaining coefficients (Secs. 3.2, 5).
  Checked partially:
      - OPE check at three loops reproduces the three-loop Konishi anomalous dimension (Sec. 4).
      - Four-loop result (eq. 5.3) not tested against the OPE (stated in Sec. 5).
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2.2, eq. (2.12): Lagrangian insertion with respect to a = g^2 Nc/(4 pi^2), written with d^4x5.
      - Eqs. (2.13)-(2.14): l-loop correction as Born-level (4+l)-point function with l insertions and 1/l!.
      - Eq. (2.20): total symmetry of f^(l); footnote 3: a generic N=2 theory lacks it at two loops.
      - Sec. 3.1-3.2: three-loop ansatz; topologies 3(a),(c),(d) set to zero by the planar duality; footnote 7: all four can contribute at finite Nc.
      - Sec. 5, eqs. (5.2)-(5.3): 32 S_8-invariant cubic topologies at four loops, fixed by the four-loop amplitude.
      - Sec. 6: authors note the amplitude-based fixing leaves more unfixed coefficients at higher loops.
      - App. B, eq. (B.4): nilpotent invariant as a Grassmann integral in analytic superspace; App. C: D=4-2eps used only inside an MB representation of a finite integral.
  Red flags:
      - The integrated-insertion formula is used without a regulator; finiteness of the integrated correlator and harmlessness of contact terms are assumed, not derived in a regularized framework.
      - Coefficient fixing rests on the conjectured planar correlator/amplitude duality.
      - At finite Nc the three-loop integrand keeps four unknown constants.
      - Nothing is said about whether a d-dimensional continuation of the integrand obeys d-dimensional SUSY Ward identities; the construction never needs a regulated integrand.
  Cross-checks:
      - Three-loop Konishi anomalous dimension reproduced via the OPE (Sec. 4).
      - Light-cone limit matches two-, three-, four-loop four-gluon amplitude integrands (hep-th/0505205, hep-th/0610248).

[S1c.02_EHKS2012] B. Eden, P. Heslop, G.P. Korchemsky, E. Sokatchev -- Constructing the correlation function of four stress-tensor multiplets and the four-particle amplitude in N=4 SYM (2012)
  arXiv: 1201.5329   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B (2012), DOI 10.1016/j.nuclphysb.2012.04.013 (volume/page not verified)
  Section: 1c correlator integrands
  Claim: Using the S_{4+l} permutation symmetry and 4d conformal weights, the integrand is written as a sum over f-graphs with unknown coefficients; logarithmic-singularity criteria in the Euclidean double short-distance (OPE) limit and, more strongly, in the Minkowski light-cone limit fix the planar integrand completely through six loops. Non-planar corrections are absent at three loops; at four loops the non-planar part is reduced to four undetermined constants.
  Loop order / theory: planar integrand through 6 loops; non-planar through 4 loops (4 constants left) / N=4 SYM, SU(Nc); four-point function of 20' stress-tensor multiplets; planar amplitude obtained via the correlator/amplitude duality
  Scheme: No regulator for the integrand: a rational 4d function (f-graphs with conformal weight 4 at each vertex). Individual integrals diverge in the light-cone limit and would need regularization (e.g. DR in D=4-2eps), but the constraints are imposed on the integrand / on the logarithmic asymptotics, not on regulated integrals. Gram-determinant identities valid only in exactly 4d are used (one three-loop structure drops out because a 4d Gram determinant vanishes; three conformal Gram conditions at four loops non-planar, eq. 5.28).
  Symmetry argument: Hidden S_{4+l} symmetry of the Lagrangian-insertion integrand (from S1c.01_EHKS2011) for any gauge group plus 4d conformal covariance; dynamical input from the required logarithmic asymptotics of log G4 in the light-cone limit (eq. 2.19) and in the Euclidean OPE limit (eq. 2.17), which encode exponentiation / single-log behaviour of the OPE with anomalous dimensions.
  Proved:
      - Within the ansatz (S_{4+l}-symmetric f-graphs, planar, weight 4 per point), the light-cone criterion (2.19) plus Euclidean criterion (2.17) admit a unique planar solution through six loops (Sec. 7, eqs. 7.2-7.3, footnote 24).
  Assumed:
      - The asymptotic logarithmic forms (2.17) and (2.19) of log G4 are imposed as requirements, not derived from a regulated computation; the authors note the softened singularity is not guaranteed sufficient and is justified a posteriori by uniqueness (Sec. 4.2).
      - Disconnected f-graphs carry zero coefficient (stated as a belief in a footnote); genus-ordering of 1/Nc^2 coefficients; planar f-graphs stay planar.
      - Use of exactly-4d Gram determinant identities (integrand is intrinsically 4d).
      - Rung rule justified up to six loops only (footnote 23).
  Checked partially:
      - Six-loop planar amplitude extracted from the correlator stated to agree with earlier amplitude integrand constructions (refs. [15],[16] of the paper).
      - Konishi anomalous dimension: one-loop value extracted; two-loop value used as input; five-loop value deferred to S1c.03_EHKSS2012.
      - Non-planar four-loop sector left with four free constants (eq. 5.28).
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2.1, eq. (2.17): Euclidean double short-distance (OPE) logarithmic criterion.
      - Eq. (2.19) with limit (2.14): Minkowski light-cone logarithmic criterion, the stronger constraint.
      - Sec. 5.1, eqs. (5.10)-(5.14): at four loops the Euclidean condition leaves one planar coefficient free, the light-cone condition fixes all; rung rule (Sec. 5.2) independently agrees.
      - Sec. 5.4, eq. (5.28): non-planar four-loop part reduced to four constants after three conformal Gram conditions.
      - Sec. 6, eqs. (6.1)-(6.2): seven planar five-loop graphs, one non-rung-rule coefficient.
      - Sec. 7, eqs. (7.2)-(7.3), footnote 24: 36 planar six-loop topologies (23 rung-rule, 13 non-rung-rule); all fixed; two coefficients vanish without explanation; footnote 22: sign-alternation heuristic fails at six loops.
  Red flags:
      - The fixing criteria are physical asymptotic requirements on unregulated integrands; their sufficiency at higher loops is not guaranteed (Sec. 4.2).
      - The integrand is intrinsically 4d (4d Gram identities used), so it cannot be read as the 4d limit of a d-dimensional regulated integrand without further work; any DR evaluation of quantities extracted from it (e.g. Konishi) applies DR to a 4d-defined object.
      - Non-planar sector undetermined at four loops.
      - Some six-loop numerators not uniquely fixed by the graph rules (Fig. 12 discussion).
  Cross-checks:
      - Six-loop planar four-gluon amplitude consistent with earlier constructions (amplitude references of the paper; see S1d records).
      - Five-loop Konishi in S1c.03_EHKSS2012 agrees with the TBA/Luscher prediction (0906.4062).

[S1c.03_EHKSS2012] B. Eden, P. Heslop, G.P. Korchemsky, V.A. Smirnov, E. Sokatchev -- Five-loop Konishi in N=4 SYM (2012)
  arXiv: 1202.5733   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B (2012), DOI 10.1016/j.nuclphysb.2012.04.015 (volume/page not verified)
  Section: 1c correlator integrands
  Claim: The planar five-loop Konishi anomalous dimension is extracted from the known 4d integrand of the stress-tensor four-point correlator (S1c.02_EHKS2012) by taking the double coincidence (OPE) limit of the logarithm of the correlator and reading off the single-log coefficient; the result 237/16 + 27/4 z3 - 81/16 z3^2 - 135/16 z5 + 945/32 z7 (coefficient of the five-loop term, eq. 1.1) agrees with the integrability (Luscher/TBA) prediction.
  Loop order / theory: 5 loops (planar); four-loop non-planar Konishi reproduced only after fixing one integrand constant from a direct Feynman-graph result / planar N=4 SYM, SU(Nc); Konishi operator via OPE of four 20' operators
  Scheme: The 4d integrand is kept unchanged (scalar 1/x^2 propagators) and only the integration measure is continued, d^4x -> mu^{2eps} d^{4-2eps}x (Sec. 3.1, footnote 6); the paper notes this differs from conventional coordinate-space DR and requires eps<0, and that only the singular terms are meaningful (finite parts differ from the true ones). The coincidence limit reduces five-loop quantities to four-loop two-point integrals (x3 -> infinity, eqs. 5.5-5.6); >17000 integrals reduced by IBP (FIRE) to 22 masters (eq. 5.8); 20 planar masters from known epsilon expansions, two non-planar masters computed in App. B and checked numerically with FIESTA; infrared rearrangement / R* reasoning in App. A.
  Symmetry argument: None at the level of the regulator: SUSY and superconformal symmetry enter only through the 4d integrand (hidden permutation symmetry, Lagrangian insertion) and through the OPE assumption that Konishi dominates the leading log singularity; the measure-only regulator is a mathematical device to isolate the log coefficient, not a symmetric regularization of the theory.
  Proved:
      - Given the 4d integrand and the OPE assumptions, the planar five-loop log coefficient is computed analytically (eq. 5.12); cancellation of 1/eps^2 and 1/eps poles in C_4 (eq. B.2, B.23) used as internal consistency check.
  Assumed:
      - Correctness of the six-loop-constructed 4d integrand (S1c.02_EHKS2012) at five loops.
      - Konishi is the leading contribution to the relevant singularity of log G4 (Sec. 2.2); leading singularity arises only from integration points approaching external points (Sec. 3.2).
      - IRR argument assumes the divergence is a pure constant counterterm, R* needed only if IR divergences arise (App. A, footnote 14).
      - Measure-only DR isolates the correct log coefficient (justified by the structure of the argument, not by a symmetry-preserving regularization of N=4).
  Checked partially:
      - Four-loop non-planar check: only the combination r of the four free non-planar constants enters; r = -135/2 is fixed by comparison with the direct four-loop Feynman-graph result of ref. [26] (eq. 4.38), not derived.
      - Non-planar masters checked numerically (FIESTA, six digits).
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 3.1 and footnote 6: measure-only dimensional continuation, eps<0, finite parts not meaningful.
      - Sec. 3.4, eqs. (3.27)-(3.33): algorithm extracting gamma_K from the simple pole of the regulated coincidence-limit integral.
      - Eq. (4.2): five-loop combination; eq. (4.38): non-planar constant r fixed from direct computation.
      - Sec. 5, eqs. (5.5)-(5.12): reduction to four-loop two-point integrals, IBP to 22 masters, planar five-loop result eq. (5.12).
      - App. B, eqs. (B.10)-(B.25): non-planar masters via Fourier transform and gluing; numerical FIESTA check.
      - Sec. 6: six loops possible in principle (five-loop two-point integrals) but left open.
  Red flags:
      - The only regulator ever applied is a measure-only continuation of a strictly 4d integrand: it is not DRED, not DREG, and not tied to any regularized N=4 action; its legitimacy rests on extracting only log coefficients.
      - Konishi five-loop result therefore tests the integrand + OPE assumptions + integrability, not any DR-type scheme for N=4.
      - Non-planar four-loop agreement is not independent: one integrand constant was fitted to a direct computation (eq. 4.38).
  Cross-checks:
      - Agrees with the integrability five-loop Konishi prediction (refs. [5-8] of the paper; Bajnok-Hegedus-Janik-Lukowski 0906.4062 per S1b records).
      - Four-loop non-planar: matched to direct Feynman-graph computation cited as ref. [26] (likely Velizhanin 0811.0607; attribution not verified here).

  Section: 1c correlator integrands

New at eight loops: integrals finite even on-shell, and 'pseudoconformal' integrals that are conformal as integrands but divergent even off-shell, so manifest finiteness/conformality of individual terms is lost; their divergences cancel only in combination.

  Scheme: No regulator: integrand-level construction in 4d (coordinates in R^{3,1}); the correlator is finite, the amplitude IR divergent; pseudoconformal integrals must be regularized but no scheme is specified; the Higgs-branch (Alday-Henn-Plefka-Schuster) mass regulator cannot be used for them because they diverge on the Higgs branch.
  Symmetry argument: Hidden S_{4+l} symmetry and dual conformal invariance of f^(l), planarity, locality, weight -4, simple poles; dynamics from the soft-collinear bootstrap (vanishing residue of log A in the collinear limit, eq. 6).
  Proved:

  Assumed:
      - Hidden symmetry, amplitude/correlator duality, locality, planarity, simple poles (Sec. II).
      - Expected but unproven graph property: every f-graph has a triangle adjacent to a square face (Sec. III).
      - The authors state it is unclear why the collinear criterion should suffice (Sec. III).
  Checked partially:

      - Alternative coincident-limit + rung-rule route claimed to fix 8 loops, left for future work (Sec. IV).
  Evidence:

      - Sec. II: f-graph enumeration, Table 1 through 10 loops.
      - Sec. III, eq. (6): soft-collinear bootstrap criterion; sufficiency through 8 loops by linear independence of residues.
      - Sec. IV, Table 2: new coefficients -2, -5, half-integers.
  Red flags:

      - Uniqueness of the bootstrap is empirical, with no explanation of why the criterion suffices.
      - Planar only.
  Cross-checks:

  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / high

[S1d.01_BDS2005] Z. Bern, L.J. Dixon, V.A. Smirnov -- Iteration of planar amplitudes in maximally supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory at three loops and beyond (2005)
  arXiv: hep-th/0505205   Journal: Phys. Rev. D72 (2005) 085001 (from memory)
  Section: 1d amplitudes / UV divergences
  Claim: The planar three-loop four-gluon amplitude of N=4 SYM, built from the Bern-Rozowsky-Yan rung-rule integrand and evaluated in DR through O(eps^0), satisfies an iterative relation in terms of one- and two-loop amplitudes; this motivates the all-loop exponentiation ansatz (BDS) for planar MHV amplitudes.
  Loop order / theory: 3 loops computed (planar, four-point); all-loop conjecture / planar N=4 SYM, four-gluon MHV amplitude
  Scheme: Dimensional regularization d=4-2eps for IR divergences; the unitarity method implicitly assumes a supersymmetric regulator such as the four-dimensional helicity (FDH) scheme, described as a variant of dimensional reduction (Sec. II); coupling carries eps-dependent factors and is dimensionful in d dims.
  Symmetry argument: Supersymmetry assumed preserved by FDH/DRED-type regulator (no proof given); integrand from rung rule / iterated two-particle cuts in 4d.
  Proved:
      - Two-loop iterative relation (eq. 18) verified through O(eps^0); three-loop relation (eq. 21) obtained by explicit computation through O(eps^0).
  Assumed:
      - SUSY-preserving regulator (FDH) implicit in unitarity method (Sec. II).
      - Rung-rule (BRY) integrand called an ansatz; terms from (-2eps)-dimensional loop-momentum components (mu-terms) dropped in three-particle and MHV cut analysis; a rigorous proof would need them (Sec. III).
      - All-loop exponentiation (eq. 24) is a conjecture.
  Checked partially:

      - Constants c1, c2 and f_2^(l) not fixed by four-point data.
  Evidence:

      - Sec. III: rung-rule integrand; mu-term contributions dropped in cut analysis.
      - Eq. (18): two-loop iteration; eq. (21): three-loop iteration with O(eps) error term; eq. (24): all-loop ansatz.
      - Text: DR explicitly breaks conformal invariance although MSYM is conformal in 4d.
  Red flags:

      - SUSY of the regulator assumed, never tested here; O(eps) pieces of lower loops enter higher-loop iteration so evanescent errors could feed in.
      - DR breaks conformal symmetry explicitly (dimensionful coupling); conformal/iterative structure only up to O(eps).

[S1d.12_DHKS2007] J.M. Drummond, J. Henn, G.P. Korchemsky, E. Sokatchev -- Conformal Ward identities for Wilson loops and a test of the duality with gluon amplitudes (2007)
  arXiv: 0712.1223   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B826 (2010) 337 (from memory)
  Section: 1d amplitudes / UV divergences
  Claim: Light-like polygonal Wilson loops in N=4 SYM, regulated dimensionally, obey anomalous conformal Ward identities to all loops: the special-conformal variation of the finite part ln F_n equals (1/2) Gamma_cusp(a) times a fixed kinematic function (eq. 50). The anomaly arises because the regularized action has a dimensionful coupling: conformal variations produce eps times integrated Lagrangian insertions, which hit 1/eps cusp poles and leave a finite remainder. For n=4,5 the solution is unique up to a constant (eq. 51); for n>=6 an arbitrary function of cross-ratios remains.
  Loop order / theory: all loops (given stated inputs); explicit two-loop check for n=5 / planar N=4 SYM, light-like polygonal Wilson loops (dual to MHV amplitudes)
  Scheme: DR with D=4-2eps in the dimensional-reduction (DRED) form to preserve SUSY (footnote 3), scalars counted as 6+2eps (footnote 9); regularized action S_eps = (1/(g^2 mu^{2eps})) int d^Dx L(x) with fields rescaled so g is out of L (eq. 23).
  Symmetry argument: Exact 4d conformal symmetry of the classical action; in D dims the Lagrangian keeps weight 4 while the measure has weight -D, so dilatation gives delta_D S_eps = -(2eps/(g^2 mu^{2eps})) int d^Dx L (eq. 25) and special conformal transformations give K^nu <W_n> = -(4i eps/(g^2 mu^{2eps})) int d^Dx x^nu <L(x) W_n> (eq. 27). The insertion <L W_n> has 1/eps poles localized at cusps with residue governed by Gamma_cusp (eq. 41), producing the finite anomaly.
  Proved:
      - Given the all-loop cusp-divergence structure and exponentiation: the anomalous dilatation and special-conformal Ward identities for ln F_n (eqs. 43-50); Upsilon^nu cancels in the sum over cusps (eq. 49).
  Assumed:
      - All-loop structure of divergent factor Z_n from the cusp evolution equation (eqs. 14-15); non-abelian exponentiation (eq. 20, cited).
      - Structure of the Lagrangian insertion near cusps (eq. 41) argued from webs and conformal weights, not computed to all orders.
      - DRED assumed to preserve SUSY (footnote 3); SUSY plays no explicit role in the Ward identity derivation, which uses only conformal weights.
      - Normalization alpha = a + O(a^2) (eq. 48) fixed only at one loop.
  Checked partially:
      - Two-loop pentagon (Sec. 4): divergences agree with eq. (67), finite part agrees up to a constant r not fixed by the identity (eq. 73).
  Evidence:
      - Eq. (22)-(23): functional integral and regularized action with mu^{2eps}.
      - Eq. (25)-(27): eps-proportional conformal variations as integrated Lagrangian insertions.
      - Eq. (38): int d^Dx x^nu Upsilon^(1) formula; eq. (41): all-loop form of the insertion with Gamma_cusp/(l eps) residue.
      - Eq. (50): final anomalous special-conformal Ward identity; eq. (51): unique n=4,5 solutions; Sec. 3.6: n>=6 freedom.
      - Footnotes 3, 9: DRED, 6+2eps scalars.
  Red flags:
      - Cleanest DR statement of conformal breaking: breaking = eps x integrated Lagrangian insertion (exact in D dims), anomaly = finite remainder from 1/eps poles; but the all-loop status depends on assumed divergence structure (eqs. 14, 41).
      - The Ward identity tests dual conformal symmetry of Wilson loops; mapping to amplitudes relies on the (then conjectural) amplitude/Wilson-loop duality.
      - The derivation does not check that DRED preserves SUSY; only the conformal weight counting enters.
  Cross-checks:
      - BDS ansatz (S1d.01_BDS2005) satisfies the identity for n=4,5; earlier four-gluon conformal analysis 0707.0243 (Drummond-Henn-Korchemsky-Sokatchev, 'Conformal properties of four-gluon planar amplitudes and Wilson loops', not fetched here).
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / high
  Notes: Lead correct. The brief's guessed form of delta_K S was not literally in the paper; the actual equations are (25) and (27). Journal from memory.

[S1d.13_BHY2017] R.H. Boels, T. Huber, G. Yang -- Four-loop nonplanar cusp anomalous dimension in N=4 supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory (2017)

  Section: 1d amplitudes / UV divergences
  Claim: From the four-loop Sudakov form factor of the stress-tensor multiplet (integrand from colour-kinematics duality, 1211.7028) re-expressed in a uniformly transcendental basis and integrated numerically in DR, the non-planar four-loop cusp coefficient is gamma_cusp,NP^(4) = -3072 (1.56 +- 0.21)/Nc^2, nonzero at 7.4 sigma: quadratic Casimir scaling breaks at four loops.

  Scheme: DR with D=4-2eps for IR divergences (Sec. II); no FDH/DRED scheme named and no SUSY-scheme statement; the form factor has no UV divergences since the operator is protected; numerical integration (FIESTA sector decomposition, some MB).
  Symmetry argument: None stated for the regulator; integrand from colour-kinematics duality (not re-derived here); UT basis supported by residue checks in 4d loop-momentum parametrization (eqs. 9-11).

[S1d.14_HKM2019] J.M. Henn, G.P. Korchemsky, B. Mistlberger -- The full four-loop cusp anomalous dimension in N=4 super Yang-Mills and QCD (2019)
  arXiv: 1911.10174   Journal: JHEP 04 (2020) 018 (from memory; no journal listed on fetched page)

  Loop order / theory: 4 loops (full colour) / N=4 SYM and QCD
  Scheme: The key object (Wilson loop with Lagrangian insertion) is finite in N=4 and computed without regulator; DR presumably enters only in the final integration over the insertion point (not stated in the abstract).
  Symmetry argument: Finiteness and dual conformal covariance of the Lagrangian-insertion ratio in N=4; supersymmetric decomposition of QCD (gluon + gluino + scalar contributions).
  Proved:
      - Analytic full-colour four-loop cusp (abstract).
  Assumed:

[S1e.01_JJN1996a] I. Jack, D.R.T. Jones, C.G. North -- N=1 supersymmetry and the three loop anomalous dimension for the chiral superfield (1996)

  Section: 1e N=1 beta functions in DRED
  Claim: The three-loop anomalous dimension of the chiral superfield in a general N=1 supersymmetric gauge theory is computed and used to probe renormalization-invariant relations between Yukawa and gauge couplings (v2 adds the Wess-Zumino special case).
  Loop order / theory: 3 loops (chiral superfield anomalous dimension) / general N=1 supersymmetric gauge theory with chiral matter
  Scheme: Not stated in the abstract; in this programme (cf. S1e.03_JJN1996c) calculations use superspace DRED with minimal subtraction (DRED-bar).
  Symmetry argument: Manifest N=1 superspace (supergraphs) with DRED: SUSY assumed preserved by the superspace D-algebra performed in 4d followed by d-dimensional momentum integrals.
  Proved:
      - Three-loop gamma for general N=1 theories (abstract).
  Assumed:

  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:

  Red flags:

[S1e.02_JJN1996b] I. Jack, D.R.T. Jones, C.G. North -- N=1 supersymmetry and the three loop gauge beta function (1996)
  arXiv: hep-ph/9606323   Journal: Phys. Lett. B386 (1996) 138
  Section: 1e N=1 beta functions in DRED
  Claim: The three-loop gauge beta-function of an abelian N=1 gauge theory is computed in DRED; a coupling redefinition relating it to the NSVZ form is constructed and generalized to the non-abelian case, yielding the non-abelian three-loop DRED gauge beta-function.
  Loop order / theory: 3 loops (gauge beta function) / N=1 SQED-type abelian theory with Yukawa couplings; inferred non-abelian result
  Scheme: DRED (superspace), minimal subtraction.
  Symmetry argument: SUSY-covariant superspace DRED; the non-abelian result follows from the redefinition ansatz, not from direct non-abelian graphs.
  Proved:
      - Abelian three-loop DRED gauge beta (direct computation).
  Assumed:
      - Non-abelian three-loop DRED beta inferred by extending the redefinition (not computed directly).
      - SUSY of DRED not discussed in the abstract.
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Abstract.
  Red flags:
      - The non-abelian three-loop DRED result is an inference based on the existence of a DRED->NSVZ redefinition, i.e. it presupposes that DRED and NSVZ are SUSY schemes related by a coupling redefinition.
  Cross-checks:
