[S1e.03_JJN1996c] I. Jack, D.R.T. Jones, C.G. North -- Scheme dependence and the NSVZ beta-function (1996)
  arXiv: hep-ph/9609325   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B486 (1997) 479
  Section: 1e N=1 beta functions in DRED
  Claim: A coupling redefinition delta g (starting at two loops, delta g = -(1/4) beta_g^(2)) relates DRED and NSVZ gauge beta-functions; abelian three-loop DRED graphs plus four structural requirements fix the full non-abelian three-loop DRED beta (agreeing with Avdeev-Tarasov for pure SYM); the four-loop DRED beta is obtained up to one undetermined parameter alpha_1 (later fixed in S1e.04_JJP1998). Conclusion: there exists a scheme in which NSVZ holds, perturbatively related to DRED, up to four loops.
  Loop order / theory: 3 loops (gauge beta, non-abelian inferred), 4 loops (up to one parameter) / general N=1 supersymmetric gauge theories
  Scheme: Superspace DRED with minimal subtraction in super-Feynman gauge (vector two-point function, D^2 Dbar^2 shortcut, Sec. 2); four-loop: partial abelian O(g^3 Y^6) computation (Sec. 3).
  Symmetry argument: SUSY manifest in superspace; the DRED vs NSVZ comparison is a scheme-equivalence statement (existence of a coupling redefinition), not a test of SUSY preservation by DRED; the paper makes no claim on DRED SUSY preservation.
  Proved:
      - Abelian three-loop DRED vector self-energy (Sec. 2); with stated requirements, uniqueness of delta g up to a constant fixed by the abelian result; agreement with Avdeev-Tarasov pure-SYM three-loop result.
  Assumed:
      - Four structural requirements on delta g used to extend from abelian to non-abelian (Sec. 2).
      - Four-loop: coefficient of tr[M C(R)] deduced from a Yukawa redefinition making gamma^(3) vanish in one-loop-finite theories (Sec. 3).
  Checked partially:
      - Four-loop: six terms matched with two parameters (alpha_2=-2/3, alpha_3=1/6) - a non-trivial consistency; alpha_1 undetermined.
      - Remarks that epsilon-scalar contributions to background-field superspace counterterms beyond one loop may obstruct an all-orders construction.
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2: abelian three-loop computation, Figs. 1-2, Table 1; delta g = -(1/4) beta_g^(2); 'quite non-trivial' check.
      - Sec. 3: four-loop partial computation, Fig. 3, Table 2; parameters alpha_1, alpha_2, alpha_3.
      - Secs. 1, 4: existence of an NSVZ scheme related to DRED.
  Red flags:
      - Consistency with NSVZ up to a coupling redefinition is a scheme statement: it does not test whether DRED preserves SUSY Ward identities; non-abelian three-loop DRED result was inferred, not computed (later confirmed directly, S1e.05_HJKMS2006).
      - Equation numbers garbled in the ar5iv extraction; only section-level references verified.
  Cross-checks:
      - Avdeev-Tarasov three-loop pure SYM DRED beta (agreement); S1e.04_JJP1998 fixes alpha_1.
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / high
  Notes: Lead correct. Answer to brief: the paper explicitly frames the comparison as scheme equivalence, not as a SUSY test.

[S1e.04_JJP1998] I. Jack, D.R.T. Jones, A. Pickering -- The connection between DRED and NSVZ (1998)
  arXiv: hep-ph/9805482   Journal: Phys. Lett. B435 (1998) 61 (from memory)
  Section: 1e N=1 beta functions in DRED
  Claim: The remaining coefficient rho_1 of the three-loop DRED->NSVZ coupling redefinition is fixed (rho_1 = 16/3) through a three-loop computation of the epsilon-scalar mass beta-function in the softly broken theory, completing the NSVZ/DRED connection through O(alpha^4) and thereby determining the four-loop DRED gauge beta-function; agreement with an earlier Pade-approximant prediction. Also introduces DRED' (soft scalar mass beta independent of the epsilon-scalar mass).
  Loop order / theory: redefinition to three loops; DRED gauge beta to four loops (inferred) / general N=1 gauge theories, including soft SUSY breaking
  Scheme: DRED / DRED' with epsilon-scalars acquiring radiative masses (missed by naive spurion formalism).
  Symmetry argument: Relations between soft-breaking beta-functions and the coupling redefinition; consistency of tensor structures T2, T3 used as checks.
  Proved:
      - rho_1 = 16/3 from the epsilon-scalar mass beta (three-loop computation in the broken theory).
  Assumed:
      - Four-loop DRED gauge beta follows from NSVZ + redefinition (i.e. assumes NSVZ holds in some scheme and DRED is a SUSY scheme related to it).
  Checked partially:
      - Consistency of the redefinition with soft scalar mass beta for alpha m^2 Y^2 Y*^2 C(R) terms; T2 and T3 structures give same rho_1.
  Evidence:
      - Main text: determination of rho_1 = 16/3; DRED' definition; Pade comparison.
  Red flags:
      - The four-loop DRED gauge beta is not computed diagrammatically here; it is inferred assuming the NSVZ-DRED redefinition picture (direct check later: S1e.05_HJKMS2006, S1e.06_JJKM2007).
      - Epsilon-scalar masses are a genuine DRED artefact that must be tracked (DRED vs DRED').
  Cross-checks:
      - Completes S1e.03_JJN1996c.
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (extract) / medium
  Notes: Journal ref not shown on the fetched page; PLB 435 (1998) 61 from memory.

[S1e.05_HJKMS2006] R.V. Harlander, D.R.T. Jones, P. Kant, L. Mihaila, M. Steinhauser -- Four-loop beta function and mass anomalous dimension in dimensional reduction (2006)
  arXiv: hep-ph/0610206   Journal: JHEP 0612 (2006) 024 (from memory; not shown on fetched page)
  Section: 1e N=1 beta functions in DRED
  Claim: The four-loop DRED (DR-bar) gauge beta-function and quark mass anomalous dimension of QCD (general group except eta_2, eta_3 parts) are obtained from the four-loop MS-bar results plus three-loop DRED<->MS-bar conversion relations, with epsilon-scalars treated as separate fields carrying evanescent couplings (Yukawa g_e, quartics lambda_r). Specialized to N=1 SYM: beta_e = beta_s through three loops (eq. 15), and the four-loop gauge beta agrees with the Jack-Jones-Pickering SUSY prediction (eq. 21), as does gamma_m from the exact SYM relation (eqs. 22-23).
  Loop order / theory: 4 loops (gauge beta, mass anomalous dimension); evanescent beta_e to 3 loops, beta_eta to 1 loop / QCD with epsilon-scalars; general gauge group; N=1 SYM as special case
  Scheme: DRED with minimal subtraction (DR-bar); epsilon-scalars with independent evanescent couplings alpha_e = g_e^2/4pi and eta_r (eqs. 1-7); tree-level relations lambda_1 = g^2, lambda_2 = lambda_3 = 0 not preserved by renormalization (Sec. 2); epsilon-scalar mass omitted (irrelevant for beta and gamma_m by dimensional arguments).
  Symmetry argument: SUSY test: in N=1 SYM the epsilon-scalar-gaugino Yukawa coupling must equal the gauge coupling; verified through three loops (eq. 15); four-loop gauge beta compared with the NSVZ-derived DRED prediction of S1e.04_JJP1998.
  Proved:
      - beta_e^SYM = beta_s^SYM through three loops (eq. 15): a genuine DRED SUSY identity check at three loops.
      - Four-loop DRED gauge beta (from MS-bar + conversion, independent of NSVZ) equals the JJP prediction for SYM (eq. 21); gamma_m^SYM agrees with gamma_m = pi alpha_s d/d alpha_s (beta_s/alpha_s) (eqs. 22-23).
  Assumed:
      - Equivalence DRED <-> DREG by coupling reparametrization including evanescent couplings (eq. 16).
      - Siegel-type inconsistency/variation of the action giving insertions (Sec. 1, ref. [6]) assumed not to affect the quantities computed.
  Checked partially:
      - Evanescent-coupling equality only through three loops; four-loop check is for gauge beta and gamma_m only.
      - Conversion terms checked for gauge-parameter independence.
  Evidence:
      - Eqs. (1)-(7): Lagrangian with epsilon-scalar evanescent couplings; eqs. (12)-(13): their beta-functions.
      - Eq. (15): beta_e^SYM = beta_s^SYM to three loops; earlier three-loop beta_e of ref. [19] stated incorrect.
      - Eq. (16)-(18): indirect method and three-loop conversion terms.
      - Eq. (21): four-loop SYM gauge beta agreement with Jack-Jones-Pickering; eqs. (22)-(23): gamma_m^SYM.
  Red flags:
      - Four-loop DRED results are not computed with DRED Feynman rules at four loops; they rely on the DRED<->DREG equivalence theorem plus three-loop conversion.
      - Evanescent couplings must be renormalized independently; naive identification led to wrong conclusions in earlier work (Sec. 1/4).
  Cross-checks:
      - Agrees with S1e.04_JJP1998 four-loop prediction; extended to general gauge theories in S1e.06_JJKM2007.
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / high
  Notes: Answer to brief: the paper verifies a DRED SUSY identity (beta_e = beta_s) to three loops and the four-loop gauge beta against NSVZ+redefinition; it makes no general claim of DRED SUSY consistency at four loops.

[S1e.07_HMS2009] R.V. Harlander, L. Mihaila, M. Steinhauser -- The SUSY-QCD beta function to three loops (2009)
  arXiv: 0905.4807   Journal: Eur. Phys. J. C63 (2009) 383 (from memory; not shown on fetched page)
  Section: 1e N=1 beta functions in DRED
  Claim: A direct three-loop component-field DRED computation in SUSY-QCD gives the same beta-function from every alpha_s-type three-point vertex, including the SUSY-fixed quark-squark-gluino vertex; epsilon-scalar couplings track the gauge coupling (Z_s = Z_e through two loops by counting, explicit three-loop checks for several epsilon-scalar vertices); the gluino-mass anomalous dimension obeys gamma_n = (n+1) beta_n through three loops; the result agrees with the NSVZ-based three-loop DRED result of Jack-Jones-North.   Cross-checks:

[S1e.08_Mihaila2013] L. Mihaila -- Precision calculations in supersymmetric theories (2013)
  arXiv: 1310.6178   Journal: Adv. High Energy Phys. 2013 (2013) 607807 (from memory; not shown on fetched page)
  Section: 1e N=1 beta functions in DRED
  Claim: Review of multi-loop DRED calculations up to four loops: DRED is not supersymmetric to all orders; its known inconsistencies (Siegel: 4d epsilon-metric relations; impossibility of splitting 4d space into infinite-dimensional pieces) become active only at higher orders (traces of >= ten gamma matrices, antisymmetrization over five indices); resolution via a quasi-4-dimensional space Q4 = Qd + Q2eps; Fierz identities fail in Q4 and SUSY may break. Epsilon-scalars must be renormalized as separate fields with evanescent couplings; in SUSY theories Z_g = Z_e should hold to all orders. Four-loop SYM gauge beta in DR-bar agrees precisely with the NSVZ-derived result, called a remarkable check of both calculations.
  Loop order / theory: review; statements at 1, 2, 3, 4 loops / SUSY QCD / MSSM / SYM in DRED
  Scheme: DRED (DR-bar) with epsilon-scalars and evanescent couplings; gamma5: HV-type in DRED needs d<4 (problematic), naive anticommuting gamma5 with O(d-4) trace ambiguity (eqs. 3.9-3.11); HV needed for three-loop finite parts with two fermion-triangle subdiagrams.
  Symmetry argument: Supersymmetry identities checked as equalities of renormalization constants (Z_g = Z_e) and agreement with NSVZ + redefinition; QAP-type argument that Fierz violation in Q4 is the source of SUSY breaking (Sec. 3.1).
  Proved: -
  Assumed:

      - HV-gamma5 Adler-Bardeen check to two loops under assumption eq. (3.8).
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2: DRED not supersymmetric to all orders; NSVZ exact relations do not hold for DRED.
      - Sec. 3.1, eq. (3.1): quasi-4-dim space; Fierz failure; gamma5 discussion eqs. (3.8)-(3.11).

    [S2.24_Mihaila2013] L. Mihaila -- Precision calculations in supersymmetric theories (2013)
      arXiv: 1310.6178   Journal: Adv. High Energy Phys. 2013 (review; volume/article no. not verified)
      Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
      Claim: Review of multi-loop DRED calculations: DRED inconsistent in its original form, cured by a formal quasi-4-dimensional space (Sec. 3.1); SUSY-breaking problems become active only at higher orders, e.g. when traces over at least ten gamma matrices occur (Sec. 3.1); epsilon-scalars need separate renormalization, identifying their Z with the gauge one breaks unitarity (Sec. 3.3); in the SUSY limit beta_e^SYM = beta_s^SYM (eqs. 3.47-3.48); four-loop DRED gauge beta of SYM obtained by substitution rules (eq. 3.46) agrees (eq. 3.49) with the NSVZ-based result, with zeta3, zeta4, zeta5 and quartic invariants cancelling; gamma_m^SYM from an exact relation (eqs. 3.50-3.51); gamma5: HV needs d>4 vs DRED d<4; naive anticommuting gamma5 with trace corrections used for 2-3 loop MSSM; HV needed for finite parts of three-loop diagrams with two fermion triangles (Sec. 3.1, eqs. 3.8-3.10).
      Loop order / theory: review: 2-4 loops / SYM, SQCD, MSSM
      Scheme: DRED / DR-bar with epsilon-scalars and evanescent couplings; DRED'.
      Symmetry argument: Equality Z_g = Z_e and beta_e = beta_s in the SUSY limit; agreement with NSVZ after scheme mapping.
      Proved: -
      Assumed:
          - Z_g = Z_e to all orders 'because of SUSY' (Sec. 3.3), stated as expected; per Sec. 3.3.4 explicit checks of the evanescent coupling exist only at two and three loops.
      Checked partially:
          - Four-loop SYM gauge beta in DRED vs NSVZ-based result (eq. 3.49).
      Evidence:
          - Sec. 2, 3.1, 3.3, 3.3.4: eqs. (3.8)-(3.10), (3.46)-(3.51).
      Red flags:
          - No loop order is given up to which DRED is guaranteed SUSY-preserving; 'ten gamma matrices' criterion is the ACV count (S2.04).
          - Sec. 4 (three-loop SUSY-QCD) not accessed (ar5iv truncated).
      Cross-checks:
          - S2.04_ACV1981
          - S2.11_HKMS2006
          - S2.13_ChakrabortyMoch2026
      Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (to Sec. 3.3.4) / medium

[S1e.10_AGKS2016] S.S. Aleshin, I.O. Goriachuk, A.L. Kataev, K.V. Stepanyantz -- The NSVZ scheme for N=1 SQED with N_f flavors, regularized by the dimensional reduction, in the three-loop approximation (2016)
  arXiv: 1610.08034   Journal: Phys. Lett. B764 (2017) 222 (DOI 10.1016/j.physletb.2016.11.041)
  Section: 1e N=1 beta functions in DRED
  Claim: With DRED, the NSVZ relation does not hold for RG functions defined in terms of the bare coupling; for RG functions in terms of the renormalized coupling, boundary conditions on the renormalization constants (more complicated than in the higher-derivative case) define an NSVZ scheme at three loops; the DRED-based and HD-based NSVZ schemes are related by a finite renormalization at this order.
  Loop order / theory: 3 loops (beta), 2 loops (anomalous dimension) / N=1 SQED with N_f flavours
  Scheme: DRED (superspace), renormalized via boundary conditions chosen to give NSVZ; contrasted with higher covariant derivative regularization.
  Symmetry argument: NSVZ as a scheme property: existence of finite renormalizations mapping DR-bar to an NSVZ scheme; DRED loop integrals are not double total derivatives, so the HD mechanism is mimicked only approximately.
  Proved:
      - NSVZ fails for bare-coupling RG functions in DRED at this order; NSVZ scheme for renormalized-coupling RG functions constructed at three loops (abstract).
  Assumed: -
  Checked partially:
      - Only three-loop SQED.
  Evidence:
      - Abstract; S1e.11_Stepanyantz2020 Sec. 1 summarizes: with DR-bar NSVZ fails starting at O(alpha^2, alpha lambda^2, lambda^4) (three-loop beta, two-loop anomalous dimension); a tuned finite renormalization restores it at least through three and four loops.
  Red flags:
      - Failure of NSVZ in DR-bar is a scheme statement, not evidence of SUSY violation by DRED; conversely, restoring NSVZ by finite renormalization is not a SUSY test.
  Cross-checks:
      - S1e.03_JJN1996c (DRED->NSVZ redefinition), S1e.11_Stepanyantz2020.
  Access / confidence: abstract only (plus summary in 2007.11935) / medium
  Notes: Lead correct.

[S1e.11_Stepanyantz2020] K.V. Stepanyantz -- The all-loop perturbative derivation of the NSVZ beta-function and the NSVZ scheme in the non-Abelian case by summing singular contributions (2020)
  arXiv: 2007.11935   Journal: Eur. Phys. J. C80 (2020) 911 (DOI 10.1140/epjc/s10052-020-8416-6; page from memory)
  Section: 1e N=1 beta functions (contrast: manifestly supersymmetric higher-derivative regulator)
  Claim: For general renormalizable N=1 gauge theories regularized by higher covariant derivatives in N=1 superspace (with Pauli-Villars superfields for one-loop divergences), the NSVZ relation holds to all orders for RG functions defined in terms of bare couplings, for any renormalization prescription; for RG functions in renormalized couplings, the HD+MSL prescription (higher derivatives + minimal subtraction of logarithms) is an NSVZ scheme to all orders. The proof sums singular contributions of double-total-derivative integrals (matter, ghost and gauge propagator cuts).
  Loop order / theory: all loops (perturbative proof) / general N=1 supersymmetric gauge theories (non-abelian; abelian case earlier)
  Scheme: Higher covariant derivative regularization: regulator functions R, F of (nablabar^2 nabla^2)/(16 Lambda^2) in background-field N=1 superspace (Sec. 2, eqs. 6-18); PV chiral superfields (three adjoint with mass a_phi Lambda; matter-sector PV in R_PV) with coupling-independent mass ratios.
  Symmetry argument: SUSY manifest (superspace); background gauge invariance manifest (eq. 17); quantum gauge invariance broken by gauge fixing and controlled by Slavnov-Taylor identities (Sec. 3). No continuation of dimension, so no evanescent / Fierz issues.
  Proved:
      - All-loop NSVZ for bare-coupling RG functions (eqs. 5/28, 33) given the stated inputs; HD+MSL is an NSVZ scheme.
  Assumed:
      - All-loop finiteness of triple gauge-ghost vertices, Z_alpha^{-1/2} Z_c Z_V = 1 (eq. 27; proved in earlier work of the programme).
      - No finite constants in Yukawa renormalization (eq. 26).
      - Cancellation among terms with 1/Q^{2n}, n>=2 (argued from well-definedness).
      - One-loop beta taken from earlier calculation.
  Checked partially:
      - Explicit multi-loop checks in the programme (e.g. three-loop Yukawa-dependent terms in 1908.04108, JHEP 10 (2019) 011).
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2, eqs. (6)-(18): regulator and PV fields.
      - Sec. 3: Slavnov-Taylor identities, transversality.
      - Eqs. (26)-(27): scheme conditions; Sec. 5: summation of singularities.
      - Intro: with DR-bar NSVZ fails from O(alpha^2, alpha lambda^2, lambda^4); tuned finite renormalization restores it through three and four loops; bare-coupling NSVZ never holds with DRED.
  Red flags:
      - Higher-derivative + PV regularization is manifestly SUSY and background-gauge invariant but breaks quantum BRST invariance through gauge fixing only; the all-loop proof relies on finiteness of the gauge-ghost vertex proved separately.
      - Different object from DRED: shows that a genuinely SUSY-preserving regulator exists for N=1 beta-functions, and that NSVZ-vs-DRED discrepancies are scheme artefacts.
  Cross-checks:
      - S1e.10_AGKS2016 (DRED vs HD NSVZ schemes related by finite renormalization at three loops); 1908.04108 (double total derivatives, all orders).
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (first 45%) + abstract of 1908.04108 / high
  Notes: Brief listed 1908.04108; the completed all-loop non-abelian NSVZ derivation is 2007.11935 (1908.04108 proves the double-total-derivative structure and only discusses the NSVZ derivation).

[S1e.14_Gracey2021] J.A. Gracey -- Five loop renormalization of the Wess-Zumino model (2021)

[S2.01_Siegel1979] W. Siegel -- Supersymmetric dimensional regularization via dimensional reduction (1979)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Phys. Lett. B 84 (1979) 193-196
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: Proposes regularization by dimensional reduction (DRED): loop momenta and integrals are continued to d<4, while the action is the one obtained by dimensionally reducing the 4-dimensional theory, so all field multiplets keep their 4-dimensional index structure and boson/fermion counting. Claims that gauge invariance, unitarity and global supersymmetry are preserved; sketches application to supergravity.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (claimed, no proof) / globally supersymmetric gauge theories; supergravity (sketched)
  Scheme: DRED: d-dimensional momenta, 4-dimensional field components and Dirac/sigma algebra; in superspace the D-algebra is done in 4 dimensions and the remaining scalar momentum integrals in d dimensions.
  Symmetry argument: Heuristic: dimensional reduction of a supersymmetric theory keeps the supermultiplet structure (equal numbers of bosonic and fermionic components), hence SUSY is expected to survive regularization.
  Proved: -
  Assumed:
      - that 4-dimensional field/spinor algebra can be combined consistently with d-dimensional momenta (shown false in general by the same author, S2.02)
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - INSPIRE abstract (record of PLB 84 (1979) 193): method preserves gauge invariance, unitarity and global SUSY; action obtained by dimensional reduction; supergravity application explored.
  Red flags:
      - No proof of SUSY preservation; the author himself showed the scheme inconsistent one year later (S2.02).
      - Implicit use of 4-dimensional identities (Fierz, epsilon tensors) together with d<4 momenta is the origin of the later inconsistency.
  Cross-checks:
      - S2.02_Siegel1980 (inconsistency)
      - S2.03_CJvN1980 (component formulation)
      - S2.10_Stoeckinger2005 (consistent reformulation)
  Access / confidence: abstract only (INSPIRE) / high
  Notes: Recorded for the definition only; all technical content of later records refers back to this proposal.

[S2.02_Siegel1980] W. Siegel -- Inconsistency of supersymmetric dimensional regularization (1980)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Phys. Lett. B 94 (1980) 37-40
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: Shows that DRED as proposed in S2.01 is mathematically inconsistent and outlines what must be changed for a consistent version.
  Loop order / theory: algebraic (order-independent) statement / any theory regularized by DRED
  Scheme: DRED with 4-dimensional gamma matrices / epsilon tensors and a d-dimensional (d<4) momentum space embedded in the 4-dimensional index space.
  Symmetry argument: Not a symmetry argument: an algebraic contradiction. As reported in S2.10 (Sec. 1): combining 4-dimensional gamma matrices and epsilon tensors with a d-dimensional metric yields 0 = d(d-1)(d-2)(d-3)(d-4); the trigger is the projector relation g^{mu nu} ghat_nu^rho = ghat^{mu rho} (eq. 4 of S2.10) used together with genuinely 4-dimensional epsilon/metric identities. S2.09 (Sec. 3, eqs. 15-19) phrases it as contradictions such as (d+1)(d-2) epsilonhat^{mu nu} = 0 obtained by evaluating epsilon-epsilon contractions in different orders.
  Proved:
      - The combination 'd-dimensional momenta inside a strictly 4-dimensional index space with all 4-dimensional identities available' is contradictory for non-integer d.
  Assumed: -
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - INSPIRE abstract: scheme mathematically inconsistent; required modifications outlined.
      - S2.10 hep-ph/0503129 Sec. 1-2: the identity 0 = D(D-1)(D-2)(D-3)(D-4) (unnumbered), eqs. (3)-(4) of that paper identify the ingredients.
      - S2.09 hep-ph/9707278 Sec. 3, eqs. (15)-(19).
  Red flags:
      - The contradiction implies that a consistent DRED must forbid purely 4-dimensional identities (index counting, 5-index antisymmetrisation, Fierz rearrangements, epsilon-tensor products) inside d-dimensional expressions; but the classical SUSY invariance of the action uses Fierz identities, so a consistent DRED can break SUSY (made explicit in S2.10).
  Cross-checks:
      - S2.10_Stoeckinger2005 (resolution via quasi-4-dimensional space)
      - S2.09_JackJones1997 (view that the problem is technical and avoidable for physical results)
  Access / confidence: secondary: cited in hep-ph/0503129 (Sec. 1-2) and hep-ph/9707278 (Sec. 3); INSPIRE abstract / medium
  Notes: Original text not accessed; the explicit identity is quoted from the two secondary sources, which present it in different but equivalent forms.

  Loop order / theory: 1 and 2 loops (explicit) / SUSY and non-SUSY gauge theories
  Scheme: Component DRED, MS-type subtraction; epsilon-scalars as the 4-d components of the gauge field.
  Symmetry argument: Explicit diagrammatic verification of selected Slavnov-Taylor / Ward identities.
  Proved: -
  Assumed: -

[S2.04_ACV1981] L.V. Avdeev, G.A. Chochia, A.A. Vladimirov -- On the scope of supersymmetric dimensional regularization (1981)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Phys. Lett. B 105 (1981) 272-274
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: Superfield DRED is internally inconsistent; a component-field reformulation removes the ambiguity, but that version violates the supersymmetric Ward identities at higher loop orders. For several models the authors determine the range (domain) in which the method is still valid; this range covers the calculations done up to then.
  Loop order / theory: earliest possible violation of the propagator SUSY Ward identity of pure N=1 SYM: 4 loops (per S2.09); identity holds to 3 loops (per S2.10) / pure N=1 SYM in Wess-Zumino gauge (the case reconstructed in S2.09 Sec. 4); 'several models' in the abstract
  Scheme: Component DRED (ACV formulation), anticommuting gamma5, minimal subtraction.
  Symmetry argument: Quantum-action-principle argument: the SUSY variation of the DRED action is delta L_S = (g/2) f^{abc} epsbar gamma^mu lambda^a lambda^b gamma_mu lambda^c (S2.09 eq. 29), which vanishes in d=4 only by Fierz rearrangement. Inserted into a diagram it contributes through Delta = Tr(A gamma^mu B gamma_mu) + Tr(A gamma^mu)Tr(B gamma_mu) - (-1)^k Tr(A gamma^mu B^R gamma_mu) (S2.09 eq. 36); for five gamma matrices in A and in B this is 48 times the antisymmetrised five-index Kronecker delta (S2.09 eqs. 37-38), with trace 48 d(d-1)(d-2)(d-3)(d-4), zero only for integer d <= 4. Enough gamma matrices to make this survive in the propagator Ward identity need at least four loops.
  Proved:
      - (Within their assumptions) the Fierz-violating insertion cannot spoil the SUSY propagator Ward identity of pure SYM below four loops.
  Assumed:
      - The quantum action principle for component DRED (only argued much later, S2.10 Sec. 3).
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - INSPIRE abstract.
      - S2.09 hep-ph/9707278 Sec. 4, eqs. (27)-(38): reconstruction of the argument; 'at least four loops' statement.
      - S2.10 hep-ph/0503129 Sec. 4.1: ACV assumed the QAP and argued the propagator identity holds to three loops; source of the breaking term Delta_gauge (eq. 35b of S2.10).
  Red flags:
      - No explicit four-loop computation of the breaking; the counting argument itself is not reproduced in either secondary source.
      - The abstract's 'domains of validity' could not be checked against the original text.
  Cross-checks:
      - S2.05_Avdeev1982 (claimed explicit 3-loop breaking of coupling relations, later disputed)
      - S2.13_ChakrabortyMoch2026
      - S2.10_Stoeckinger2005
  Access / confidence: abstract only (INSPIRE) + secondary: hep-ph/9707278 Sec. 4 and hep-ph/0503129 Sec. 4.1 / medium
  Notes: The four-loop statement concerns the propagator Ward identity of pure SYM; it is not a statement about beta functions.

[S2.05_Avdeev1982] L.V. Avdeev -- Noninvariance of regularization by dimensional reduction: an explicit example of supersymmetry breaking (1982)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Phys. Lett. B 117 (1982) 317-320
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: A direct three-loop calculation in component fields is claimed to show that the DRED version of S2.04 breaks the SUSY relations between couplings in N=1, 2 and 4 SYM: the three-loop beta functions extracted from the fermion-fermion-vector vertex and from the fermion-fermion-scalar (Yukawa) vertex differ (per S2.12 v5, for D=4, 6, 10 formulations).

  Assumed: -

[S2.06_AvdeevVladimirov1983] L.V. Avdeev, A.A. Vladimirov -- Dimensional regularization and supersymmetry (1983)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B 219 (1983) 262-276
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: Detailed analysis of supersymmetric dimensional regularization: a manifestly supersymmetric (superfield) version leads to contradictions; removing the contradictions breaks SUSY at higher orders; an explicit three-loop example of SUSY breakdown is analysed at the diagram level; the validity of DR for global SUSY gauge theories is reconsidered.
  Loop order / theory: 3 loops (explicit example); general higher-order statement / globally supersymmetric gauge theories
  Scheme: Superfield DRED versus its consistent component reformulation.
  Symmetry argument: Dichotomy: manifest SUSY (superfield) + DR is inconsistent; consistent component DR is not manifestly SUSY and fails Ward identities at higher orders.
  Proved:
      - The dichotomy at the level of the regularization algebra (consistent with S2.02, S2.10).
  Assumed: -
  Checked partially:
      - The explicit three-loop breaking example is the one disputed by S2.12 and S2.13.
  Evidence:
      - INSPIRE abstract.
      - S2.10 hep-ph/0503129 ref. [15] groups it with S2.04.
      - S2.13 2603.02892 Sec. I: steps not detailed enough to identify the subtlety.
  Red flags:

[S2.07_vanDammeHooft1985] R. van Damme, G. 't Hooft -- Breakdown of unitarity in the dimensional reduction scheme (1985)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Phys. Lett. B 150 (1985) 133-138
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: Beta functions in different schemes should be related by finite coupling redefinitions; in non-SUSY gauge theories DRED fails this test; the culprit is the counterterm structure needed for overlapping divergences, which violates unitarity. SUSY theories (N=2, N=4 gauge theories, any SUSY theory without vector field) are not affected because the epsilon-scalar renormalizes like the scalar, fermion and vector fields.
  Loop order / theory: multi-loop (overlapping divergences, i.e. from two loops) / non-SUSY gauge theories; SUSY theories as stated exceptions
  Scheme: DRED in which the counterterms of the 4-dimensional couplings are reused for the epsilon-scalar couplings (called 'System 4' in S2.08).
  Symmetry argument: Not a SUSY argument; consistency of scheme change by coupling redefinition (unitarity).
  Proved:
      - With epsilon-scalar couplings identified with gauge couplings in non-SUSY theories, DRED is not related to DREG by a coupling redefinition.
  Assumed: -
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - INSPIRE abstract.
      - Response: S2.08 hep-ph/9401349 Sec. 1 and 3 (separate evanescent couplings renormalized so that Green functions with external epsilon-scalars are finite restore equivalence and unitarity).
      - S2.09 hep-ph/9707278 Sec. 1 and 6.
  Red flags:
      - The conclusion is specific to the identification of evanescent with real couplings; it is the origin of the rule that evanescent couplings must be kept separate (S2.11, S2.14).
      - Abstract names N=2, N=4 and vector-free SUSY theories as unaffected; N=1 gauge theories with matter are not named in the abstract.
  Cross-checks:
      - S2.08_JackJonesRoberts1994 (response)
      - S2.11_HKMS2006 (concrete three-loop consequence of identifying alpha_e = alpha_s)
  Access / confidence: abstract only (INSPIRE) + secondary: hep-ph/9401349, hep-ph/9707278 / high
  Notes: 

[S2.08_JackJonesRoberts1994] I. Jack, D.R.T. Jones, K.L. Roberts -- Equivalence of dimensional reduction and dimensional regularisation (1994)
  arXiv: hep-ph/9401349   Journal: Z. Phys. C 63 (1994) 151-160
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: For a general renormalizable theory, DRED (with epsilon-scalars carrying their own evanescent couplings, renormalized so that Green functions with external epsilon-scalars are finite) is equivalent to DREG to all orders: the two are related by an analytic redefinition of all couplings, so S-matrix elements agree. The van Damme-'t Hooft unitarity problem is attributed to their counterterm prescription.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (formal argument); explicit one- and two-loop formulae, three-loop structure only / general renormalizable theory (gauge fields + scalars; fermions said to follow straightforwardly)
  Scheme: DRED realized as DREG plus a multiplet of epsilon-scalars of multiplicity E, with E = epsilon set after expansion ('System 3'); MS.
  Symmetry argument: Not a SUSY argument: renormalization-theory argument (scheme change = coupling redefinition). For SUSY theories, identifying evanescent with real couplings is stated to be consistent because their beta functions coincide (App. A).
  Proved:
      - DRED-DREG equivalence via coupling redefinitions at the level of the formal all-order argument (Sec. 3), with explicit one- and two-loop beta-function differences (App. A).
  Assumed:
      - gamma5 and epsilon^{mu nu rho sigma} issues explicitly excluded (Sec. 1)
      - SUSY of DRED itself is not addressed; equality of evanescent and real beta functions in SUSY theories is taken as input
  Checked partially:
      - Two-loop delta Z^{WW} and delta Z^{psibar psi} compared with earlier calculations (App. A).
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 1: van Damme-'t Hooft problem traced to counterterm prescription; gamma5 not addressed.
      - Sec. 3: evanescent couplings introduced; equivalence by analytic redefinition lambda -> lambda', lambda_E -> lambda'_E.
      - App. A: one- and two-loop formulae; two-loop delta Z^{WW} = g^4 C2(G)^2/[2(16 pi^2)^2 eps].
      - eq. nos. lost in ar5iv conversion (not verified).
  Red flags:
      - The all-order equivalence is a statement about the full theory including evanescent couplings; it does not imply that DRED preserves SUSY.
      - Excludes gamma5/epsilon-tensor traces, which are the source of the higher-loop subtleties (S2.13).
      - Sec. 4 suggests natural values (h = g) are acceptable for S-matrix predictions but not for running only the real-coupling beta functions.
  Cross-checks:
      - S2.07_vanDammeHooft1985
      - S2.11_HKMS2006
      - S2.14_Gnendiger2017 (cites equivalence as indirect proof of unitarity/causality for FDH/DRED)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (equation numbers lost) / high
  Notes: Companion paper Jack, Jones, Roberts, Z. Phys. C 62 (1994) 161 not separately recorded.

[S2.09_JackJones1997] I. Jack, D.R.T. Jones -- Regularisation of supersymmetric theories (1997)
  arXiv: hep-ph/9707278   Journal: contribution to 'Perspectives on Supersymmetry' (ed. G.L. Kane, World Scientific); no journal ref on arXiv
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: Review. DRED is the regulator of choice for SUSY theories; the Siegel-type ambiguities (epsilon tensors, gamma5) are regarded as technical and avoidable for physical results; the one-loop SUSY Ward identity holds in DRED; the Avdeev-Chochia-Vladimirov ambiguity in the propagator Ward identity of pure SYM needs at least four loops; superfield methods do not escape it; DRED beta functions deviate from the NSVZ form from three loops but are related to it by a coupling redefinition; in softly broken SUSY the epsilon-scalar mass enters two-loop scalar-mass beta functions and is removed by the DRED' redefinition; the van Damme-'t Hooft unitarity problem is resolved by separate treatment of epsilon-scalar couplings.
  Loop order / theory: review: 1-loop Ward identity; >= 4-loop onset of the ACV ambiguity; 3-loop DRED/NSVZ deviation; 4-loop DRED beta up to one unknown parameter (status 1997) / N=1 SYM and general N=1 gauge theories; N=2, N=4; softly broken SUSY
  Scheme: DRED (component and superfield), DRED' for soft breaking, comparison with DREG.
  Symmetry argument: (i) Insertion of the Fierz-vanishing variation delta L_S and gamma-trace counting (ACV); (ii) NSVZ vs DRED related by coupling redefinitions; (iii) N=2 one-loop finiteness used to fix the three-loop DRED gauge beta function.
  Proved: -
  Assumed:
      - Sec. 4: the Delta ambiguity is expected not to affect physical results (stated expectation, no proof).
  Checked partially:

  Evidence:
      - Sec. 3, eqs. (15)-(19): Siegel ambiguity, e.g. (d+1)(d-2) epsilonhat^{mu nu} = 0; eq. (25): gamma5 via epsilon tensors, factor epsilon tensors out; eq. (26) Hull-Townsend prescription gives same physics in one case (Allen-Jones NPB 303 (1988) 271).
      - Sec. 4, eqs. (27)-(38): SYM in WZ gauge, delta L_S (29), Delta (36)-(38), Tr Delta = 48 d(d-1)(d-2)(d-3)(d-4); 'at least four loops'.
      - Sec. 6, eqs. (40)-(43): epsilon-scalar mass m~^2, inhomogeneous beta, DRED' (41), top-mass one-loop coefficient 5 (DRED) vs 4 (DREG).
      - Sec. 7, eqs. (46)-(49): NSVZ, deviation at 3 loops (refs Jack-Jones-North NPB 473 (1996) 308, PLB 386 (1996) 138).
  Red flags:
      - Sec. 1: whether a fully satisfactory SUSY regulator exists for gauge theories is called controversial.
      - No loop order is given up to which SUSY is guaranteed; ambiguity estimate rests on ACV's counting argument.
      - Superfield DRED shares the ambiguity (Sec. 4).

[S2.10_Stoeckinger2005] D. Stoeckinger -- Regularization by dimensional reduction: consistency, quantum action principle, and supersymmetry (2005)
  arXiv: hep-ph/0503129   Journal: JHEP 0503 (2005) 076
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: Gives a mathematically consistent formulation of DRED: a quasi-4-dimensional space Q4S = QdS + QepsS (formal dimensions 4, d, eps=4-d; all infinite-dimensional), metric g = ghat + gtilde with only the contraction rules (5a)-(5d); no index counting, no Fierz identities; this avoids Siegel's contradiction. Argues that the quantum action principle (QAP) holds in this DRED as in DREG. Derives the general breaking of the Slavnov-Taylor identity (with constant SUSY and translation ghosts) as an insertion of S(Gamma_cl), eq. (34), whose non-zero pieces (35a)-(35h) are Fierz-vanishing terms (gauge, matter, potential and Yukawa sectors; gauge-fixing part zero). Using gamma-counting rules it shows: the phi-psi propagator identity (37) and the SUSY-transformation identity (38) hold at one and two loops; a new phi^4 identity (39) linking the quartic scalar vertex to gauge and superpotential couplings holds at one loop, its two-loop status is open. Conclusion: no general proof; checked identities hold up to two loops; at higher orders SUSY relations could fail, then requiring counterterms, but much fewer than in DREG.
  Loop order / theory: general structure all orders; identities (37),(38) at 1 and 2 loops; (39) at 1 loop / general N=1 SUSY gauge theory, simple gauge group, chiral matter (ST identity with constant ghosts, conventions of ref. [7])
  Scheme: DRED in Q4S, anticommuting gamma5 (eq. 8), Tr 1 = 4 (eq. 57), traces with gamma5 and >= 4 gamma matrices vanish for non-integer d (footnote 1); alternative 4-dimensional gamma5bar of HVBM type (eqs. 9-11) rejected because it breaks SUSY and gauge invariance at one loop (eq. 36).
  Symmetry argument: Regularized QAP: S(Gamma_DRED) = i [S(Gamma_cl)].Gamma_DRED (eq. 34). S(Gamma_cl) is non-zero only through terms that vanish by 4-dimensional Fierz rearrangement; diagrams with this insertion vanish if the gamma string B in the attached fermion loop/line contains at most 4, 2 or 3 gamma matrices for topologies (a), (b), (c) (eqs. 41a-41d); eq. (42) exhibits a non-vanishing case (topology b with three gamma matrices).
  Proved:
      - Consistency of the Q4S construction (Sec. 2, App. A).
      - General formula (34)-(35) for the breaking of the ST identity in DRED (given the QAP).
      - Identities (37) and (38) at one and two loops; identity (39) at one loop (Sec. 4.3).
  Assumed:
      - QAP in DRED: the DREG proof of Breitenlohner-Maison is said to carry over; combinatorial part not discussed; needs D_ij P_jk = i delta_ik on the regularized level (eq. 30) and vanishing scaleless integrals (Sec. 3).
  Checked partially:
      - Only the three identities (37)-(39); nothing at three loops.
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 1: Siegel identity 0 = D(D-1)(D-2)(D-3)(D-4) (unnumbered).
      - Sec. 2: eqs. (3)-(5d) Q4S rules; App. A explicit construction, eqs. (55)-(57).
      - Sec. 3: QAP eq. (1), (17)-(19), (30), (32)-(34).
      - Sec. 4.1: previous checks (CJvN NPB 167 for gauge WIs to two loops; ACV assumed QAP, propagator identity to three loops).
      - Sec. 4.2: eqs. (35a)-(35h) breaking terms; (36) extra terms with gamma5bar.
      - Sec. 4.3: eqs. (37)-(42); Figs. 2-4.
      - Sec. 5: conclusions (two-loop usability, no general proof).
  Red flags:
      - Anticommuting gamma5 in DRED cannot compute gamma5-odd traces (axial anomaly) correctly; the paper suggests Larin/Trueman-type prescriptions as models (refs [25]).
      - The 4-dimensional gamma5bar (BMHV-like) breaks SUSY already at one loop in DRED.
      - The phi^4 identity (39), the closest analogue of 'quartic coupling = gauge coupling squared', is not established at two loops here.
      - The QAP for DRED is argued by analogy, not proved in full (combinatorics not discussed).
  Cross-checks:
      - S2.02_Siegel1980
      - S2.04_ACV1981
      - S2.21_HollikStoeckinger2005 (two-loop MSSM identity via same technique)
      - S2.22_StoeckingerUnger2018 (three-loop extension)
      - constant-ghost ST identity papers (section 4, other worker)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / high
  Notes: The word 'evanescent' is not used; the breaking terms are described as vanishing identically in four dimensions. They are ghost-number-one insertions built with the constant SUSY ghost and three fermion fields.

  arXiv: 1705.01827   Journal: Eur. Phys. J. C 77 (2017) 471 (DOI 10.1140/epjc/s10052-017-5023-2)
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: Review and comparison of CDR, HV, FDH, DRED (Sec. 2), FDF/SDF (Sec. 3), IREG (Sec. 4), FDR (Sec. 5), FDU (Sec. 6) on simple NLO examples. Sec. 2.1 defines the schemes via the quasi-4-dimensional space QS[4] (QS[d] + QS[n_eps], n_eps = 2 eps), with strictly 4-dimensional S[4] as a subspace, and singular vs regular vector fields (Table 1); epsilon-scalars and evanescent couplings must be separated at multi-loop level in non-SUSY theories (eqs. 2.7-2.8; Sec. 2.2: up to (l-1) loops in an l-loop calculation, two-loop counterterm eq. 2.12).   Scheme: CDR, HV, FDH, DRED, FDF, SDF, IREG, FDR, FDU.

  arXiv: hep-ph/0509298   Journal: Phys. Lett. B 634 (2006) 63-68
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: For the Yukawa-enhanced two-loop MSSM Higgs-mass corrections (O(alpha_t,b alpha_s), O(alpha_t,b^2), O(alpha_t alpha_b), gaugeless limit) DRED preserves the needed SUSY: the relevant ST identity (fifth derivative of S(Gamma) w.r.t. three Higgs components, a Higgsino and the SUSY ghost) holds, so the non-supersymmetric two-loop quartic Higgs counterterm vanishes and multiplicative (symmetric) renormalization is justified.
  Loop order / theory: 2 loops (Yukawa-enhanced, gaugeless) / MSSM (Higgs sector)
  Scheme: DRED of S2.10 with anticommuting gamma5.
  Symmetry argument: ST identity S(Gamma)=0 with constant SUSY ghost (eq. 7); its two-loop breaking rewritten as a single insertion [S(Gamma^(0))] (eq. 18, method of S2.10); gamma-matrix counting (properties after eq. 19) and momentum arguments (eq. 20) make all breaking diagrams vanish.

[S2.22_StoeckingerUnger2018] D. Stoeckinger, J. Unger -- Three-loop MSSM Higgs-boson mass predictions and regularization by dimensional reduction (2018)
  arXiv: 1804.05619   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B 935 (2018) 1 (volume/page not verified)
  Section: 2 DRED consistency / FDH / HV / epsilon-scalars
  Claim: DRED preserves the SUSY needed for the three-loop MSSM Higgs-mass prediction at O(alpha_t,b alpha_s^2, alpha_t,b^2 alpha_s, alpha_t,b^3) in the gaugeless limit: the ST identity linking the quartic Higgs coupling to the electroweak gauge couplings (eqs. 37-38) holds, so the non-SUSY three-loop quartic counterterm vanishes (eq. 41). Two-loop STIs (eqs. 14-36, Table 2) fix the needed two-loop subrenormalization (Higgs/Higgsino-quark/squark Yukawa, squark-Higgs quartics, gluon/epsilon-scalar-Higgs quartics); eq. (35)-(36) give Z_{AhatAhat H2H2} = 0 and Z_{AtildeAtilde H2H2} = 0, the latter (epsilon-scalar) not implied by gauge invariance.
  Loop order / theory: 3 loops (restricted orders) / MSSM, gaugeless limit
  Scheme: DRED (Q4S, anticommuting gamma5), epsilon-scalars renormalized independently; three-loop STI at zero external momenta.
  Symmetry argument: Regularized QAP in DRED: breaking = insertion Delta = S(Gamma_cl+ct), non-zero only through Fierz violation; at three loops Delta^{<=2L} (eq. 40) split into a symmetric-renormalization part and a possible non-SUSY two-loop piece (dropped: needs >= 3 coloured fields); breaking diagrams (Figs. 3-5, classes A-C, Table 3) all satisfy the gamma-count bound of S2.10, so they vanish.
  Proved:
      - The specific three-loop STI (eqs. 37-38) holds in DRED at the stated orders, gaugeless limit, zero external momenta.
  Assumed:
      - QAP in DRED.
      - Non-SUSY two-loop counterterms to y-gluino-squark-quark and four-squark vertices (Fig. 2) argued irrelevant at this order, not shown to vanish.
  Checked partially:
      - Down-type (H1, d_R) cases said to follow in the same way, details not given.
  Evidence:
      - eqs. (14)-(36), Table 2: two-loop STIs.
      - eqs. (37)-(41), Figs. 3-5, Table 3: three-loop analysis.
  Red flags:
      - Restricted: gaugeless limit, specific orders, zero momenta; soft-breaking and EWSB contributions neglected in the two-loop part.
      - Quartic interactions with four coloured fields covered by no analysis cited (open).
      - Epsilon-scalar-Higgs quartic Z_{AtildeAtilde H2H2} = 0 is a SUSY requirement that DREG would violate.
  Cross-checks:

  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / high
  Notes: Highest loop order at which a DRED Slavnov-Taylor identity (as opposed to a beta-function equality) has been verified in a gauge theory with matter, to the extent found here.

====================================================================================================
3   BMHV ('t Hooft-Veltman/Breitenlohner-Maison) and symmetry-restoring counterterms; SUSY applications

[S3.01_tHooftVeltman1972] G. 't Hooft, M.J.G. Veltman -- Regularization and renormalization of gauge fields (1972)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B 44 (1972) 189-213
  Section: 3 BMHV (incl. SUSY applications)
  Claim: Introduces dimensional regularization for gauge theories: separates overlapping divergences, preserves unitarity and causality, allows shifts of integration variables; Ward identities hold throughout when there are no anomalies; makes manifest where anomalies (Bell-Jackiw-Adler) can arise. Contains the non-anticommuting gamma5 (gamma5 anticommutes with the first four gamma matrices and commutes with the others) that is the 'HV' part of BMHV.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (method) / gauge theories (Yang-Mills type, renormalizable theories)
  Scheme: DREG with 't Hooft-Veltman gamma5.
  Symmetry argument: Gauge invariance of the d-dimensional regularized action (vector-like theories); anomalies localized in gamma5-dependent pieces.
  Proved:
      - Unitarity/causality and Ward identities for anomaly-free cases (as stated in the abstract).
  Assumed: -
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - INSPIRE abstract.
  Red flags:
      - For chiral couplings the HV gamma5 breaks gauge invariance of the regularized action by evanescent terms; restoration requires finite counterterms (worked out much later, S3.03 onward).
  Cross-checks:
      - S3.02_BreitenlohnerMaison1977
  Access / confidence: abstract only (INSPIRE) / high
  Notes: 

[S3.02_BreitenlohnerMaison1977] P. Breitenlohner, D. Maison -- Dimensional renormalization and the action principle; Dimensionally renormalized Green's functions for theories with massless particles I, II (1977)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Commun. Math. Phys. 52 (1977) 11-38; 39; 55
  Section: 3 BMHV (incl. SUSY applications)
  Claim: Define dimensional renormalization such that the renormalized (quantum) action principle holds, giving a minimal additive scheme; derive Ward-Takahashi identities and Callan-Symanzik equations from it; prove existence of dimensionally renormalized Green functions to all orders for massless theories without super-renormalizable couplings (and, part II, with interactions of infrared degree >= 4), with the action principle holding as in the massive case if dimensional regularization respects the symmetry that protects masslessness. Together with S3.01 this fixes the consistent (BMHV) treatment of gamma5 in d dimensions.
  Loop order / theory: all orders / general renormalizable QFT (massive and massless)
  Scheme: DREG with minimal subtraction; BMHV gamma5 (gamma5 = 4-dimensional object; {gamma^mu, gamma5} = 2 gammahat^mu gamma5).
  Symmetry argument: Regularized quantum action principle: the variation of the regularized generating functional under a field transformation equals the insertion of the variation of the regularized action, a local (possibly evanescent) operator; Ward identities follow when the variation vanishes.
  Proved:
      - All-order existence and the action principle in dimensional renormalization (abstract-level statements).
  Assumed: -
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - INSPIRE abstracts of the three CMP papers.
      - Cited as the basis of the regularized QAP in S2.10 (ref. [18]) and S3.03 (refs. 3-7).
  Red flags:
      - The action principle gives breakings as insertions of evanescent operators; it does not by itself give their finite four-dimensional remainder, which must be computed (Bonneau identities, S3.03) at each order.
  Cross-checks:
      - S3.01_tHooftVeltman1972
      - S3.03_MartinSanchezRuiz1999
      - S2.10_Stoeckinger2005
  Access / confidence: abstract only (INSPIRE) + secondary: hep-ph/0503129, hep-th/9905076 / high
  Notes: 

[S3.03_MartinSanchezRuiz1999] C.P. Martin, D. Sanchez-Ruiz -- Action principles, restoration of BRS symmetry and the renormalization group equation for chiral non-Abelian gauge theories in dimensional renormalization with a non-anticommuting gamma5 (1999)
  arXiv: hep-th/9905076   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B 572 (2000) 387 (journal ref not shown in the text accessed)
  Section: 3 BMHV (incl. SUSY applications)
  Claim: One-loop renormalization of a general chiral non-Abelian gauge theory (left- and right-handed fermion multiplets, no scalars) in BMHV: the evanescent part of the fermion kinetic term breaks BRS invariance (s_d S_0 = Deltahat, eq. 34); the breaking is computed via the regularized action principles QAP1-QAP4 (eqs. 14-17) and Bonneau identities (eq. 25); the BRS anomaly is obtained for the first time in this framework (essential chiral anomaly, Sec. 3.6) and finite counterterms S_fct^(1) (ansatz eq. 66: (dA)^2, A box A, cubic and quartic gauge terms with d^{abc} and T^{abcd}, fermion kinetic and source terms) restore the ST identity iff d_{L-R}^{abc} = 0; the RGE is worked out in the anomaly-free case.
  Loop order / theory: 1 loop (explicit); all-order consistency of BMHV quoted from S3.02 / chiral non-Abelian gauge theory, simple and then general compact group, no scalars
  Scheme: BMHV DREG, MS for singular parts, finite counterterms fixed algebraically.
  Symmetry argument: Regularized QAP: breaking = N[O].Gamma_ren (eq. 18); its finite four-dimensional part from Bonneau identities (residues of simple poles); cohomological split into removable (b-exact) part and anomaly.
  Proved:
      - At one loop: BRS restorable by finite counterterms iff the gauge anomaly coefficient vanishes; explicit anomaly expression S(Gamma_ren) = (hbar/(4 pi)^2)(2/3) int eps Tr_{L-R}[omega d(dA A - (i/2) A A A)] + O(hbar^2).
  Assumed:
      - Absence of anomaly at higher orders argued from the Adler-Bardeen theorem (expectation, not computed).
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - eqs. (11)-(18), (22)-(25): action principles and Bonneau identities.
      - eqs. (31)-(34): d-dim BRS and breaking.
      - eqs. (64), (66)-(67), Sec. 3.6: breaking, counterterm ansatz, anomaly.
      - eq. (39) discussion: multiplicative renormalization lost at one loop; eq. (40) stated valid to all orders.
  Red flags:
      - S3.08 (footnote 11 and footnote 17) reports disagreements with some ghost/group-factor terms of this paper (eq. 54).
      - Finite counterterms not unique (symmetric finite terms can be added).
  Cross-checks:

[S3.05_HKS1999_SQED] W. Hollik, E. Kraus, D. Stoeckinger -- Renormalization and symmetry conditions in supersymmetric QED (1999)
  arXiv: hep-ph/9907393   Journal: Eur. Phys. J. C 11 (1999) 365
  Section: 3 BMHV (incl. SUSY applications)
  Claim: SQED in Wess-Zumino gauge with constant SUSY and translation ghosts (ST identity eq. 42); explicit one-loop renormalization in DREG with a non-anticommuting gamma5 of 't Hooft-Veltman/BMHV type ({gamma^mu, gamma5} = 2 ghat^{mu nu} gamma_nu gamma5, ghat^mu_mu = D-4, ghat set to 0 only in final results; Sec. 4).   Loop order / theory: 1 loop / N=1 SQED (Wess-Zumino gauge)
  Scheme: DREG with BMHV-type gamma5 (Sec. 4); comparison remark on DRED.
  Symmetry argument: ST identity with constant ghosts gives exact symmetry conditions that fix counterterms even when the regularization breaks SUSY; no anomaly in SQED other than the SUSY extension of the Adler-Bardeen anomaly, so all breakings are scheme breakings.
  Proved:

  Checked partially:

    [S4.05_HollikKrausStoeckinger1999] W. Hollik, E. Kraus, D. Stoeckinger -- Renormalization and symmetry conditions in supersymmetric QED (1999)
      arXiv: hep-ph/9907393   Journal: Eur. Phys. J. C11 (1999) 365-381
      Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts (+ SUSY-restoring counterterms)
      Claim: SQED in Wess-Zumino gauge is renormalized with an arbitrary (non-invariant) regularization by imposing the Slavnov-Taylor identity with constant SUSY and translation ghosts; the ST identity yields simple all-order symmetry conditions on renormalized vertex functions (electron-selectron mass identity, photon-photino relations, photino coupling = gauge coupling, four-scalar couplings), which fix the non-invariant counterterms.       Symmetry argument: ST identity S(Gamma)=0 (eqs. (7), (29), (42)) with constant ghosts: s epsilon = s epsilon-bar = 0, s omega^nu = 2 epsilon sigma^nu epsilon-bar (eqs. (20)-(22)); external sources Y_i for nonlinear transformations (eq. (26)) and a bilinear term Gamma_bil absorbing equation-of-motion terms of the open algebra (eqs. (27)-(28)); gauge fixing with epsilon-dependent compensating terms (eqs. (40)-(41)); only possible anomaly = SUSY extension of Adler-Bardeen, absent in SQED, so all breakings are removable
      Proved:

(70), (73)), photon-photino self-energy relation (eqs. (89)-(93)), gauge constraints (eqs. (94)-(98)), Thomson-limit conditions incl. photino coupling = gauge coupling (eqs. (99)-(103)), four-scalar couplings (eqs. (104)-(109)); collected in Sec. 3.4, eqs. (110)-(134)
          - explicit one-loop DREG breaking: mass counterterms delta m_phi = (alpha/4pi) m [-2B0 - 2/3], delta m_Psi = (alpha/4pi) m [-2B0 + 1], so the SUSY-restoring difference is (5/3)(alpha/4pi) m; photino coupling needs an extra non-symmetric shift -(1/6)(alpha/4pi) to restore charge universality; photon vertices need none (Sec. 4.3)

      Assumed:

      Checked partially:

[S4.01_White1992a] P.L. White -- An analysis of the cohomology structure of super Yang-Mills coupled to matter (1992)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Class. Quant. Grav. 9 (1992) 1663-1682
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts
  Claim: Formulates the Wess-Zumino consistency conditions for N=1 super Yang-Mills coupled to a chiral (Wess-Zumino) multiplet in components, in Wess-Zumino gauge with the auxiliary fields eliminated; the on-shell-closing SUSY+gauge algebra is closed by the Batalin-Vilkovisky construction (antifield-dependent terms) and the resulting local cohomology is computed with spectral sequences. The detailed list of cocycles could not be read (paper not on arXiv).
  Loop order / theory: all orders (cohomological statement; ghost numbers 0 and 1) / N=1 SYM + chiral matter, components, Wess-Zumino gauge, no auxiliary fields
  Scheme: no regulator; regularization-independent algebraic (cohomological) analysis
  Symmetry argument: BV extended BRST differential with constant SUSY ghosts; local cohomology by spectral sequences
  Proved:
      - (per INSPIRE abstract) BV closure of the on-shell SUSY+gauge algebra in WZ gauge and a spectral-sequence computation of the associated cohomology; the exact statements were not verified at equation level
  Assumed:
      - implicit in any cohomological anomaly analysis: a renormalization scheme obeying the quantum action principle, so that breakings of the Slavnov-Taylor identity are local insertions satisfying the consistency condition (not verified in the text)
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - INSPIRE abstract of CQG 9 (1992) 1663: WZ gauge, auxiliary fields eliminated, 1983 BV formalism closes the algebra, cohomology solved via spectral sequences
      - cited as ref. [12] in Maggiore-Piguet-Wolf hep-th/9507045, which treats the same class of models
  Red flags:
      - full text not accessible (not on arXiv); no equation-level verification possible
      - Brandt hep-th/0212070 (Introduction) describes earlier SYM cohomology computations as done in restricted function spaces (power-counting / low ghost number); whether White's analysis is among the works meant there (refs [1,2] of Brandt) was not verified
  Cross-checks:
      - consistent in setting with S4.03_MaggiorePiguetWolf1996 (only the supersymmetric extension of the gauge anomaly in the power-counting-renormalizable sector)
      - complete, unrestricted cohomology later in S4.11_Brandt2003 (hep-th/0212070)

[S4.02_White1992b] P.L. White -- Analysis of the superconformal cohomology structure of N=4 super Yang-Mills (1992)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Class. Quant. Grav. 9 (1992) 413-444
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts
  Claim: Builds, with the Batalin-Vilkovisky method, a nilpotent extended BRST operator that includes the full (on-shell) superconformal symmetry of N=4 SYM through constant ghosts; argues with spectral sequences that its cohomology relevant for anomalies is trivial, so that no symmetry is anomalous, hence no conformal anomaly, hence finiteness to all orders.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (claimed) / N=4 SYM in components, Wess-Zumino-type gauge, superconformal algebra realized with constant ghosts (details not verified)
  Scheme: no regulator; algebraic (cohomological) argument
  Symmetry argument: triviality of the superconformal BV cohomology at ghost number one => all symmetries, including dilatations/special conformal transformations, survive renormalization => vanishing beta function
  Proved:
      - (claimed in the abstract) triviality of the anomaly cohomology of the superconformal BV operator; not verified at equation level
  Assumed:
      - that cohomological triviality suffices to impose the full superconformal Slavnov-Taylor identity order by order, which requires a quantum action principle for x-dependent, nonlinearly realized, on-shell-closing symmetries and normalization conditions compatible with dilatations in a massless theory (how this is handled was not visible)
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - INSPIRE abstract of CQG 9 (1992) 413 (INSPIRE recid 31722): BV construction of the superconformal nilpotent operator, spectral sequences, cohomology trivial, no conformal anomaly, all-order finiteness
      - cited as ref. [9] in hep-th/9507045 and as ref. [3] in hep-th/0605164 (only for conformal invariance / superconformal cohomology)
  Red flags:
      - all-order finiteness from a cohomology statement: in the standard algebraic treatment (Callan-Symanzik equation, supercurrent trace identities; cf. S4.04_Piguet1996 Secs. 7-8) the beta function multiplies the breaking of dilatations by the coupling-constant insertion and the renormalization scale; why this term is excluded by the superconformal consistency conditions is the crux and could not be checked
      - no later paper found that re-derives or audits the result; S4.12_BaulieuBossardSorella2006 cites it without assessment and gives its own (different) algebraic proof
      - not on arXiv; only the abstract was accessible
  Cross-checks:
      - same conclusion (beta = 0 to all orders) from light-cone superspace (Mandelstam 1983; Brink-Lindgren-Nilsson 1983), N=2 extended superspace power counting (Howe-Stelle-Townsend 1984) and S4.12_BaulieuBossardSorella2006; explicit DR/DRED checks of beta = 0 at 3 and 4 loops are in section 1 records
  Access / confidence: abstract only (INSPIRE); secondary: cited in hep-th/9507045, hep-th/0605164 / medium
  Notes: Lead verified: CQG 9 (1992) 413 exists with this title; 46 citing records on INSPIRE, none of those inspected discusses the proof in detail.

[S4.03_MaggiorePiguetWolf1996] N. Maggiore, O. Piguet, S. Wolf -- Algebraic renormalization of N=1 supersymmetric gauge theories (1996)
  arXiv: hep-th/9507045   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B458 (1996) 403-429; Erratum ibid. B469 (1996) 513-514 (arXiv journal-ref field wrongly says Phys. Rev. D52 (1995) 6988)
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts
  Claim: Regulator-free algebraic renormalization of a general N=1 SUSY gauge theory with chiral matter in Wess-Zumino gauge. Gauge BRST, SUSY, translations and R-symmetry are merged into one generalized BRST operator with constant ghosts; the resulting Slavnov-Taylor (SST) identity can be restored order by order, the only possible obstruction being a supersymmetric extension of the non-abelian chiral gauge anomaly, whose coefficient vanishes to all orders if it vanishes at one loop. The physical counterterms are the gauge and Yukawa coupling renormalizations; the rest are field renormalizations. The SUSY generators extracted from the BRST operator close on the Wess-Zumino superalgebra on gauge-invariant operators.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (algebraic induction in hbar) / N=1 SYM + chiral matter in arbitrary representation, components, Wess-Zumino gauge, no auxiliary fields, Landau gauge fixing
  Scheme: no regulator; power counting + locality + quantum action principle (Sec. 3.2, eqs. (3.32)-(3.33)); the authors state that no regularization preserving both BRST and SUSY is known; dimensional schemes are not discussed
  Symmetry argument: generalized BRST operator s with constant SUSY ghost epsilon (commuting spinor), translation ghost xi^mu and R-ghost eta; s^2 = 0 only on-shell, so the classical action Sigma = S_SYM + S_gf + S_ext contains terms quadratic in the external fields (antifields), e.g. -(g^2/2)(epsilon lambda* - ...)^2 and 2 epsilon psi* epsilon-bar psi-bar* (eq. (2.7)); SST identity S(Sigma)=0 (eq. (2.8)); linearized operator B_Sigma nilpotent off-shell as a consequence of S(Sigma)=0 (eq. (2.9)). This is exactly the BV master equation (Sigma,Sigma)=0 of an open algebra with constant ghosts for global symmetries.
  Proved:
      - anomaly cohomology (ghost number 1, B_Sigma modulo d) in the power-counting-renormalizable sector is one-dimensional: the supersymmetric extension Delta_SAB of the non-abelian gauge anomaly, built from d^{ijk} (eq. (3.38)) and an invariant rank-4 tensor D^{ijmk} (eq. (3.39)); filtration computation in App. B, other candidates Delta_I..Delta_IV shown B_Sigma-trivial (eqs. (B.40)-(B.43)); no independent SUSY anomaly
      - quantum SST identity S(Gamma) = r Delta_SAB + O(hbar r) (eq. (3.31)); nonrenormalization: r = 0 to all orders if r vanishes at one loop (Sec. 5, by reduction to ordinary gauge theory at zero global ghosts)
      - stability: invariant counterterms = Z_g dSigma/dg + Yukawa renormalizations (eq. (3.25)) plus B_Sigma-trivial field renormalizations (eq. (3.26))
      - Callan-Symanzik equation with beta_g, beta_abc and anomalous dimensions (eq. (6.4), replaced by (E.2) in the erratum)
  Assumed:
      - existence of a scheme satisfying the quantum action principle for the constant-ghost SST identity (no specific regularization is constructed)
      - the one-loop value of the anomaly coefficient r is taken from the known gauge-anomaly computation, not computed
      - infrared: Landau gauge with massless fields; IR safety of the inductive argument is not addressed in the parts read
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2: field content, generalized BRST s, Table 1 (quantum numbers), eqs. (2.1) on-shell closure, (2.6) s^2 = field equations, (2.7) quadratic external-field terms, (2.8) SST identity, (2.9) B_Sigma
      - Sec. 3: gauge condition (3.2), ghost equation (3.3)-(3.4), global ghost equations for xi and eta (3.5)-(3.6), rigid/translation/R Ward operators (3.16)-(3.18), counterterms (3.25)-(3.26), anomaly (3.31), (3.36)-(3.39)
      - Sec. 5: nonrenormalization of the anomaly coefficient; App. B: cohomology by filtration, result (B.35)-(B.43)
      - Erratum (April 1996): App. A claim that the linear SUSY transformations of bosons are not renormalized is wrong; equality of anomalous dimensions within a multiplet cannot be proven in WZ gauge (they are gauge dependent); boson and fermion field counterterms in (3.30) get independent coefficients; CS equation (6.4) -> (E.2)
  Red flags:
      - the theorem is conditional on a regularization with a quantum action principle; it says nothing about which concrete scheme (DREG, DRED, BMHV) realizes it or which finite counterterms that scheme needs
      - the erratum shows that WZ-gauge field renormalizations of multiplet partners are independent (no 'supersymmetric' relation among wave-function renormalizations) - relevant for any claim that SUSY relates anomalous dimensions of component fields
      - arXiv journal-ref field is incorrect (PRD 52 (1995) 6988); the correct reference is NPB 458 (1996) 403
  Cross-checks:
      - agrees with S4.01_White1992a and S4.11_Brandt2003 on the absence of genuine SUSY anomalies for semisimple gauge groups
      - used as the framework in S4.05-S4.08 (Hollik, Kraus, Stoeckinger et al.)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (including erratum); arXiv abs; INSPIRE for journal data / high
  Notes: The constant-ghost ST identity with quadratic antifield terms is the BV master equation for the open algebra (SUSY closes on gauge transformations + translations only on-shell). Translation ghost xi and R-ghost eta enter through global ghost equations (3.5)-(3.6).

[S4.04_Piguet1996] O. Piguet -- Supersymmetry, supercurrent, and scale invariance (lectures) (1996)
  arXiv: hep-th/9611003   Journal: lecture notes (conference record only)
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts
  Claim: Review of the algebraic renormalization of N=1 SUSY gauge theories in superspace (supersymmetric Landau gauge, not WZ gauge): ST identity, anomaly analysis, supercurrent trace identities, and the algebraic all-order finiteness criterion: if the axial (R) anomaly coefficients vanish and the one-loop beta functions vanish with an isolated solution of the coupling reduction equations, then beta_g = 0 to all orders, only field-amplitude renormalizations remaining infinite.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (algebraic) / N=1 SYM + chiral matter in superspace
  Scheme: no regulator; quantum action principle, BPHZ-type induction, point-split Wick products; DRED not mentioned; IR problem of the massless dimensionless superfield C handled by WZ gauge or a SUSY-breaking regulator mass that is a gauge parameter (Secs. 5.1, 6.1)
  Symmetry argument: manifest (superspace) SUSY + ST identity for gauge BRST; scale breaking expressed through the supercurrent trace identity whose anomaly insertion is expanded in beta functions and anomalous dimensions
  Proved:
      - renormalized supercurrent trace identity with a chiral anomaly insertion S (eqs. (7.18), (7.20)); broken dilatation Ward identity = Callan-Symanzik equation (eqs. (7.21)-(7.22), (6.39))
      - finiteness criterion (Sec. 8, eqs. (8.15)-(8.20)): r = r_0a = 0 and one-loop beta = 0 with isolated reduced solution imply beta_g = 0 to all orders; relation (8.14) ties the anomaly coefficient r to beta functions and anomalous dimensions
      - superpotential nonrenormalization at zero momentum (Sec. 5.3, eqs. (5.22)-(5.24))
  Assumed:
      - absence of SUSY anomalies taken from the literature (Sec. 6.2 remark, refs. [48]-[50] there)
      - the one-loop-to-all-orders vanishing of the supersymmetric Adler-Bardeen gauge anomaly relies on a supersymmetric Bardeen-type theorem that the text says has not been explicitly checked (Sec. 6.3, Remark 1)
      - a scheme satisfying the quantum action principle with manifest SUSY
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 4.2: WZ gauge not preserved by SUSY; Sec. 4.6-4.9: ST identity, general solution, gauge parameters a_k (eq. (4.42))
      - Sec. 6.3, eq. (6.15): unique gauge anomaly candidate (SUSY extension of Adler-Bardeen)
      - Sec. 7: (7.4), (7.14), (7.18), (7.20)-(7.22); Sec. 8: (8.1), (8.14)-(8.20)
  Red flags:
      - superspace formulation with supersymmetric gauge; it does not by itself say anything about component DRED/DREG
      - the all-order nonrenormalization of the supersymmetric gauge-anomaly coefficient is stated as not explicitly checked
  Cross-checks:
      - WZ-gauge constant-ghost version of the same structure: S4.03_MaggiorePiguetWolf1996
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (reference list not reached) / medium
  Notes: Included because the finiteness criterion is the algebraic all-order SUSY protection statement that DR-based finiteness checks (e.g. 3-loop beta in finite N=1 theories) test.

[S4.07_HollikKrausRothRuppSiboldStoeckinger2002] W. Hollik, E. Kraus, M. Roth, C. Rupp, K. Sibold, D. Stoeckinger -- Renormalization of the minimal supersymmetric standard model (2002)
  arXiv: hep-ph/0204350   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B639 (2002) 3-65
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts (+ SUSY-restoring counterterms)
  Claim: All-order algebraic renormalization of the electroweak MSSM: ST identity with constant SUSY and translation ghosts, soft breaking via a chiral spurion combined with BRST doublets, spontaneous breaking via external doublets, local U(1)_Y Ward identity with a functional Gell-Mann-Nishijima relation, IR finiteness in BPHZL, and a complete set of on-shell normalization conditions. Breakings in a given order are local (quantum action principle) and can be removed by uniquely determined non-invariant counterterms, given that the only possible anomaly (supersymmetric Adler-Bardeen) cancels.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (algebraic) / electroweak MSSM (SU(2)xU(1), soft breaking, SSB), components, WZ gauge, on-shell scheme
  Scheme: no specific regulator for UV; quantum action principle (ref. [28] there) for locality of breakings; IR via Lowenstein's BPHZL with auxiliary mass, argued scheme independent; authors state that invariant regularizations of the MSSM probably do not exist (parity violation + SUSY); DRED not discussed in the parts read
  Symmetry argument: S(Gamma)=0 (eqs. (3.1)-(3.3)), constant ghosts epsilon^alpha, xi^mu in the BRS transformations (eq. (2.2)); soft terms coupled to spurion A-hat with shifted theta^2 component and BRST doublet (A, a^alpha = sqrt(2) epsilon^alpha A1, F_A = A2) (eqs. (2.11), (2.15), (2.17)); bilinear external-field terms included in Gamma_ext after eliminating auxiliary fields (Sec. 2.3); local U(1) Ward identity (eq. (3.17)) and Gell-Mann-Nishijima relation (eq. (3.16)); linearized ST operator nilpotent only on a subspace (eq. (3.12))
  Proved:
      - breakings of the ST identity of the form (3.33) can be absorbed by counterterms order by order, given the anomaly assumption
      - invariant counterterms: coupling/mass renormalizations, combined field-parameter renormalizations (eqs. (3.40)-(3.45)); g' not independent (local U(1) Ward identity (3.25)); finite field reparametrizations via demixing matrices (eqs. (3.46)-(3.48)); multiplicative renormalizability
      - IR finiteness of the vertex functional in BPHZL with IR dimensions (Tables 3.3-3.4; conditions (3.70)-(3.71))
  Assumed:
      - explicitly assumed (Sec. 3.3): the only possible anomaly is the supersymmetric Adler-Bardeen anomaly; its coefficient vanishes by the hypercharge assignment (all orders if the local U(1) Ward identity holds)
      - existence of a UV scheme satisfying the quantum action principle; which concrete scheme and which counterterms it needs are left to explicit calculations
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2.2-2.8: soft breaking, external fields, parametrization, gauge fixing, free parameters (eqs. (2.26a-g), (2.90)-(2.95))
      - Sec. 3.1, 3.3, 3.4, 3.6: ST and Ward identities, anomaly assumption, invariant counterterms, IR finiteness; Sec. 4: on-shell normalization conditions, open question on M_A0 pole vs mass definition (Sec. 4.3.1)
  Red flags:
      - absence of SUSY anomalies is an assumption in this paper (cohomology not computed here; covered for semisimple groups by S4.03, S4.11 but the MSSM has an abelian factor, for which S4.11 lists extra candidate cohomology)
      - nilpotency of the linearized ST operator only on a subspace (abelian factor)
      - statement 'symmetry can be restored in the given order' is conditional; no explicit regulator
  Cross-checks:
      - explicit one-loop counterterms: S4.05, S4.06, S4.08; DRED 2-loop MSSM Higgs-mass checks in section 2 records (Hollik-Stoeckinger hep-ph/0509298, Stoeckinger hep-ph/0503129)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (Sec. 4.3 and conclusions not reached); INSPIRE for journal data / high
  Notes: This is the constant-ghost ST identity (= BV master equation with constant ghosts and spurion BRST doublets) for a realistic chiral SUSY model; its anomaly input for the abelian sector is assumed.

[S4.09_Kraus2002] E. Kraus -- An anomalous breaking of supersymmetry in supersymmetric gauge theories with local coupling (+ companion: Calculating the anomalous supersymmetry breaking in super-Yang-Mills theories with local coupling) (2002)
  arXiv: hep-th/0107239; companion hep-ph/0110323   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B620 (2002) 55-83; companion Phys. Rev. D65 (2002) 105003
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts
  Claim: When the SYM gauge coupling is promoted to an external real superfield G = (eta + eta-bar)^(-1/2) (local coupling), the Wess-Zumino-gauge ST identity with constant SUSY ghosts acquires a one-loop anomalous breaking: the BRS variation of a monomial containing ln g times the YM Lagrangian (eqs. (31)-(32)), coefficient r_eta^(1) = C(G)/(8 pi^2) (eq. (38)), which vanishes for constant coupling, is gauge and scheme independent, and cannot be removed by a local counterterm - only shifted between the ST identity and the eta - eta-bar identity, or absorbed into a coupling-dependent modification F(g^2) of the SUSY transformation of g^2 (eqs. (47)-(50), (61)-(62)). It generates the two-loop gauge beta function from one-loop data; the NSVZ form corresponds to the choice F = 1/(1 - r_eta g^2) (eqs. (65), (104)-(106)), which the paper says is not distinguished.
  Loop order / theory: anomaly first at 1 loop; consequences for beta at 2 loops and a closed all-order form depending on normalization / N=1 SYM (plus axial vector and charged multiplets for multiplicative renormalizability, eqs. (7)-(10)), WZ gauge, local coupling
  Scheme: algebraic (quantum action principle); BPHZL and DREG with MS stated to satisfy the eta - eta-bar identity by construction (eq. (41)); companion paper computes the coefficient from convergent one-loop integrals (fermion triangle with axial-current insertion, bosonic graphs with topological-current insertion) in Feynman gauge with a background gauge, divergent parts fixed by transversality; agrees with a DREG computation by Bos (ref. [20] there); DRED not discussed
  Symmetry argument: ST identity S(Gamma_cl) = 0 (eq. (26)) and eta - eta-bar identity (eq. (27)); anomaly coefficient scheme/gauge independence via BRS-varying gauge parameter (eqs. (51)-(52)) and RG argument (eqs. (53)-(57))
  Proved:
      - existence and uniqueness (up to shifting) of the one-loop anomalous breaking of SUSY in WZ gauge with local coupling; non-removability by local counterterms (Sec. 3, 4)
      - two-loop beta function fixed by one-loop beta, r_eta^(1), the Adler-Bardeen coefficient r^(1) = -C(G)/(16 pi^2) (eq. (71)) and gamma^(1): eq. (104)
      - explicit one-loop value r_eta^(1) = C(G)/(8 pi^2) from convergent integrals (companion, Sec. 4, eqs. (27), (79), (80))
  Assumed:
      - quantum action principle for the extended (local-coupling) ST identity
      - the NSVZ form is a normalization choice, not derived (paper's own statement)
  Checked partially:
      - companion: Feynman-gauge simplifications attributed to apparent accidental cancellations; gauge independence argued algebraically
  Evidence:
      - hep-th/0107239: eq. (5) local coupling; (26)-(27) identities; (31)-(32) anomaly; (38) coefficient; (47)-(50) shifting; (61)-(62) modified SUSY transformation; (65), (104)-(106) beta function forms; (70)-(71) Adler-Bardeen anomaly

  Red flags:
      - shows that a constant-ghost ST identity can carry a genuine (non-removable) anomaly once couplings are local external fields, although no SUSY anomaly exists at constant coupling - a BV treatment with background couplings must include it
      - the closed NSVZ-type formula is normalization dependent
  Cross-checks:

      - relation to holomorphic vs canonical coupling (Arkani-Hamed-Murayama rescaling anomaly) mentioned as not apparent (hep-ph/0110323 Sec. 5)

  Notes: Lead corrected: the anomaly is not Pontryagin-like; it is the BRS variation of a ln g x Lagrangian monomial. Relevant to any BV/QME analysis with x-dependent couplings or with the coupling in front of the kinetic term.

[S4.10_KrausRuppSibold2003] E. Kraus, C. Rupp, K. Sibold -- Supersymmetric Yang-Mills theories with local coupling: the supersymmetric gauge (2003)
  arXiv: hep-th/0212064   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B661 (2003) 83-98
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts
  Claim: With manifest superspace SUSY and a space-time dependent complex coupling, the local-coupling anomaly appears in two identities (non-renormalization of the topological term; relation between gauge-coupling and complex-supercoupling renormalization); only one can be maintained.   Loop order / theory: anomaly at 1 loop; all-order statements on the structure of beta / pure N=1 SYM in superspace, local complex coupling
  Scheme: assumes consistent SUSY-invariant schemes (BPHZ/BPHZL or Wilsonian), no specific regulator; soft gauge-breaking masses for off-shell IR (eqs. (19)-(20)), ST identity up to soft terms

  Assumed:

      - existence of SUSY-invariant schemes
