[S4.11_Brandt2003] F. Brandt -- Extended BRST cohomology, consistent deformations and anomalies of four-dimensional supersymmetric gauge theories (2003)
  arXiv: hep-th/0212070   Journal: JHEP 0304 (2003) 035
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts
  Claim: Complete computation, without restrictions on mass dimension or ghost number, of the local cohomology of the extended BRST differential that includes rigid N=1 SUSY and Poincare transformations through constant ghosts, for SYM + chiral matter (+ linear multiplets). For semisimple gauge groups without linear multiplets the candidate anomalies are exhausted by supersymmetric versions of the non-abelian chiral anomalies and the first-order deformations are standard superspace integrals; abelian factors and linear multiplets give additional deformations and candidate anomalies.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (cohomological; first-order deformations / lowest-order anomalies) / N=1 SYM + chiral multiplets (+ linear multiplets with 2-form potentials), components, on-shell (WZ-gauge-type) formulation; Sec. 9 also with auxiliary fields and with linearly realized SUSY
  Scheme: no regulator; classical local BRST cohomology
  Symmetry argument: extended BRST differential s_ext with constant ghosts c^mu (translations), c^{mu nu} (Lorentz), commuting spinor ghosts xi^alpha, xi-bar (SUSY, ghost number 1); antifield-dependent terms quadratic in antifields in L_ext (eq. (2.24)) encode on-shell closure; s_ext^2 = 0 on fields, antifields and constant ghosts
  Proved:
      - H^{1,4} (candidate anomalies): for semisimple gauge group without linear multiplets only representative (7.8), a supersymmetric generalization of the non-abelian chiral anomaly (Sec. 7, comments (g), (m)); no further SUSY anomaly candidate
      - additional candidates only with abelian factors / linear multiplets: (7.9)-(7.11) (abelian factors under which matter is uncharged), (7.12)-(7.13) (at least two abelian factors), (7.14), (7.15) (supersymmetrized abelian chiral anomalies)
      - H^{0,4} (counterterms / deformations): representatives (7.1)-(7.6), generic invariants (7.6) infinitely many of arbitrary dimension (comment (f))
      - with auxiliary fields every representative of H^4 can be made antifield independent (comment (c)); antifield dependence otherwise reflects characteristic cohomology
  Assumed:
      - the step from cohomology to renormalized perturbation theory (quantum action principle, a regularization) is outside the paper; anomalies are classified only to lowest nontrivial order
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2, eqs. (2.8)-(2.21): s_ext on fields and constant ghosts; (2.22) c-hat^mu = c^mu - x^nu c_nu^mu; (2.23)-(2.24) L_ext with quadratic antifield terms
      - Secs. 4-5: change of variables and gauge covariant algebra, eqs. (5.1)-(5.2); Sec. 6(e) supersymmetry algebra cohomology; Lemmas 6.1-6.3 extra invariants for abelian factors (6.22)-(6.25)
      - Sec. 7: counterterms (7.1)-(7.6), anomalies (7.8)-(7.15)
  Red flags:
      - classical cohomology only; which finite counterterms a concrete scheme (DREG/DRED/BMHV) needs is not addressed
      - abelian factors (e.g. U(1) factors with neutral matter, as in U(N) theories) enlarge both the deformation and the anomaly cohomology
  Cross-checks:
      - agrees with S4.03_MaggiorePiguetWolf1996 and S4.01_White1992a in the renormalizable semisimple case and extends them
      - Brandt's later 'Supersymmetry algebra cohomology I-IV' (0911.2118 and sequels) systematize the constant-ghost (SUSY algebra) part; not separately recorded
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (reference list not reached); arXiv abs / high
  Notes: Most complete classical statement that rigid N=1 SUSY has no genuine anomaly beyond the supersymmetrized chiral gauge anomalies (semisimple case). The constant-ghost extended BRST differential here is the classical part of the BV master equation the user wants.

[S4.12_BaulieuBossardSorella2006] L. Baulieu, G. Bossard, S.P. Sorella -- Finiteness properties of the N=4 super-Yang-Mills theory in supersymmetric gauge (2006)
  arXiv: hep-th/0605164   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B753 (2006) 252-272
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts
  Claim: Using shadow fields and a 9-generator twisted subalgebra of N=4 SUSY that closes off-shell (with auxiliary fields), the authors claim regularization-independent renormalizability of N=4 SYM in components, an algebraic proof that the beta function vanishes to all orders given its one-loop vanishing, and UV finiteness of the 1/2-BPS operators.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (algebraic; input: one-loop beta = 0) / N=4 SYM, simple gauge group, zero instanton number, twisted variables (SU(2)+ x diag(SU(2)- x SU(2)_R) x U(1))
  Scheme: no regulator; algebraic renormalization with power counting; supersymmetric (Q-exact) gauge fixing, shadow-Landau gauge alpha = 0, linear gauges
  Symmetry argument: two Slavnov-Taylor identities, for the gauge BRST s (with shadow field c and ghost Omega) and for the twisted supercharge Q, plus ghost/antighost Ward identities; off-shell closure of the 9-parameter subalgebra (eq. (7)), decoupling condition (eq. (25)); beta = 0 from a descent/Chern-Simons cocycle argument (eqs. (63)-(65), (70)) giving beta^(n) proportional to beta^(1)
  Proved:
      - (within the stated hypotheses) stability: most general counterterm eqs. (59)-(60); only g renormalizes; SUSY parameters not renormalized in the chosen gauge
      - absence of the Adler-Bardeen-type anomaly via identification of the cocycle (eqs. (22)-(23)) and claimed emptiness of H^1 for the twisted supersymmetry
      - all-order beta = 0 given one-loop beta = 0 (eq. (70)); 1/2-BPS operators finite (eqs. (48), (62))
  Assumed:
      - Ward identities can be imposed order by order (a scheme with quantum action principle exists); finite set of sources suffices
      - one-loop vanishing of beta taken as 'well-established' input
      - simple gauge group, zero instanton sector, linear gauges
      - stability established 'by inspection' through power counting; some expansions done by brute force (authors' caveats)
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2.1-2.2: twisted fields, auxiliary fields H_I, T_mu, Q-exact gauge fermion; eq. (7) closure on gauge + translation
      - Sec. 3.1 anomalies (eqs. (22)-(23)); 3.2 stability; 3.3 Callan-Symanzik
      - Sec. 5: protected and 1/2-BPS operators (eqs. (48), (62)); Sec. 6: cocycles, descent, Chern-Simons formula, beta cancellation (eqs. (63)-(70), (87))
  Red flags:
      - only 9 (or fewer) of the 16 supercharges are realized in the ST identities; full superconformal symmetry is not part of the quantum identities
      - physical observables defined as the cohomology of s rather than Q; authors state this redefinition is what makes the approach work
      - regularization-independence is a claim about the algebraic structure; no concrete scheme is shown to satisfy the identities, and the needed finite counterterms in DR/DRED are not discussed
      - companion paper cited as in preparation; the proof is called an improved version of an earlier one
  Cross-checks:
      - cites S4.02_White1992b without assessment; other finiteness proofs (light-cone, Howe-Stelle-Townsend) cited only
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / medium
  Notes: The relevant structure for a BV/QME formulation: twisted scalar supercharge realized off-shell, constant SUSY parameters treated as ghost-number-0 commuting parameters (per text), Q-exact gauge fixing.

[S4.13_RigidSUSYAnomalyDebate2019] G. Katsianis, I. Papadimitriou, K. Skenderis, M. Taylor; I. Papadimitriou; S.M. Kuzenko, A. Schwimmer, S. Theisen; A. Bzowski, G. Festuccia, V. Prochazka -- Debate on rigid supersymmetry anomalies in N=1 theories with anomalous R-symmetry (Anomalous supersymmetry; Supersymmetry anomalies in N=1 conformal supergravity; ... new minimal supergravity; Comments on anomalies in supersymmetric theories; Consistency of supersymmetric 't Hooft anomalies) (2019)
  arXiv: 1902.06715; 1902.06717; 1904.00347; 1909.07084; 2011.09978 (also 2011.09506)   Journal: PRL (2019) [1902.06715]; JHEP 04 (2019) 040 [1902.06717]; JHEP 09 (2019) 039 [1904.00347]; J. Phys. A (2020), DOI 10.1088/1751-8121/ab64a8 [1909.07084]; JHEP 02 (2021) 225 [2011.09978]
  Section: 4 Algebraic renormalization of SUSY gauge theories with constant ghosts
  Claim: Katsianis-Papadimitriou-Skenderis-Taylor and Papadimitriou: Wess-Zumino consistency conditions in the presence of background (conformal or new-minimal) supergravity imply that rigid Q-supersymmetry is anomalous whenever the R-symmetry (or a flavour symmetry) is anomalous; supported by a one-loop four-point function of two supercurrents and two R-currents in the free Wess-Zumino model. Kuzenko-Schwimmer-Theisen: superspace and WZ-gauge anomaly cohomologies are in one-to-one correspondence; apparent Q-SUSY violations come from an explicit superspace counterterm (a scheme choice) and are removable. Bzowski-Festuccia-Prochazka: give an explicit local counterterm removing the SUSY anomaly at the price of enlarging the current multiplet, and a manifestly SUSY Pauli-Villars treatment of supercurrent correlators.
  Loop order / theory: 1 (anomaly cocycles; free-field one-loop correlators) / 4d N=1 (super)conformal and R-multiplet theories coupled to background supergravity
  Scheme: background-field cohomology (Wess-Zumino consistency conditions); explicit one-loop in free WZ model; Pauli-Villars (BFP)
  Symmetry argument: consistency conditions of the combined Weyl/R/SUSY/diffeo transformations of background fields vs choice of local counterterms and current multiplets
  Proved:
      - (KPST/Papadimitriou) a solution of the WZ consistency conditions with nonzero SUSY variation tied to the R-anomaly in the chosen background multiplet
      - (KST) correspondence between superspace and WZ-gauge anomaly cohomologies; removability by counterterm (per abstract)
      - (BFP) explicit counterterm removing the SUSY anomaly with an enlarged current multiplet (per abstract)
  Assumed:
      - which background supergravity multiplet (and hence which current multiplet) the field theory must couple to
  Checked partially:
      - only abstracts read; equation-level verification not done
  Evidence:
      - abstracts of 1902.06715, 1902.06717, 1904.00347, 1909.07084, 2011.09978 (arXiv/INSPIRE/search snippets)
  Red flags:
      - the outcome is a statement about rigid SUSY with background fields; for constant couplings in flat space (the setting of S4.03, S4.11) no SUSY anomaly arises in any of these works
      - the dispute shows that 'SUSY anomaly' depends on which current multiplet and which local counterterms are allowed - the same ambiguity a BV/QME treatment with background fields must fix
  Cross-checks:
      - consistent with S4.09_Kraus2002: anomalies of SUSY appear only once couplings/backgrounds are made local
  Access / confidence: abstract only / medium
  Notes: Optional context record. Lead corrected: the KST paper (1909.07084) has authors Kuzenko, Schwimmer, Theisen (no Tartaglino-Mazzucchelli); Bzowski-Festuccia-Prochazka is 2011.09978.

[S5.01_KoernerKreimerSchilcher1992] J.G. Koerner, D. Kreimer, K. Schilcher (and D. Kreimer 1990) -- A practicable gamma5-scheme in dimensional regularization (KKS); The gamma5-problem and anomalies - a Clifford algebra approach (Kreimer 1990) (1992)
  arXiv: none (pre-arXiv)   Journal: Z. Phys. C54 (1992) 503-512 (KKS); Phys. Lett. B237 (1990) 59 (Kreimer)
  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5
  Claim: Keep an anticommuting gamma5 in D dimensions and give up cyclicity of the trace: gamma5-odd traces are evaluated with a non-cyclic linear functional ('Trace') that agrees with the 4d trace at D=4; the result then depends on where the trace is started (the 'reading point'), and the ABJ anomaly appears as the non-cyclic output of the scheme. KKS turn this into a practical prescription and show (per Kreimer 1994) that starting the reading at different points in graphs contributing to the same process can violate gauge invariance in the SM.
  Loop order / theory: one-loop anomaly and low-order applications (details not seen) / QED/QCD/electroweak SM fermion loops
  Scheme: DREG with anticommuting gamma5, non-cyclic trace functional, reading-point prescription; epsilon tensor projected on the 4d index set
  Symmetry argument: Ward identities of vector vertices kept by anticommutation; the anomaly localized at the reading point (axial vertex)
  Proved: -
  Assumed:
      - a consistent rule for choosing reading points in every graph and its counterterm graphs (formalized only in Kreimer 1994, S5.02)
  Checked partially:
      - one-loop anomaly reproduced; equivalence of reading points of types i_+ and i_I found in the 1-loop anomaly analysis (as reported in S5.02)
  Evidence:
      - Kreimer hep-ph/9401354 refs. [4], [6] and Sec. 2: AAA = (1/3) AVV obtained by Bose-symmetrizing over reading points; gauge-invariance violation in the SM if reading starts at different points in graphs of the same process (citing [6] = KKS)
  Red flags:
      - not on arXiv; content known only through Kreimer hep-ph/9401354 and later citations
      - the prescription is incomplete without a rule for multi-loop graphs with subdivergences, several gamma5-odd loops, or anomalous subgraphs; later works (S5.07_Chen2023, S5.11, S5.12) show residual ambiguities at high loop order
  Cross-checks:
      - formalized in S5.02_Kreimer1994; critique/alternatives in S5.03_Jegerlehner2001; modern variant in S5.07_Chen2023
  Access / confidence: secondary: cited in hep-ph/9401354 (full text read) / medium
  Notes: Lead verified via INSPIRE: 'A practicable gamma(5) scheme in dimensional regularization', J.G. Koerner, D. Kreimer, K. Schilcher, Z. Phys. C54 (1992) 503-512 (Kreimer's ref. [6] lists the authors in a different order); Kreimer PLB 237 (1990) 59.

[S5.02_Kreimer1994] D. Kreimer -- The role of gamma5 in dimensional regularization (1994)
  arXiv: hep-ph/9401354   Journal: preprint UTAS-PHYS-94-01; INSPIRE lists no journal publication
  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5
  Claim: Defines the reading-point prescription for the anticommuting-gamma5, non-cyclic-trace scheme to all loop orders and argues (i) consistency with the forest formula, (ii) no modification of BRST identities at any loop order in anomaly-free theories (in contrast with BM schemes), (iii) a short proof of the Adler-Bardeen theorem.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (claimed, power-counting arguments) / renormalizable anomaly-free gauge theories with chiral fermions (SU(N) and with abelian factors, e.g. U(1)xSU(2)xSU(3))
  Scheme: DREG, gamma5 anticommuting with all gamma^mu; trace replaced by a linear non-cyclic functional Tr that projects gamma5-odd strings on the 4d form epsilon^{0123} (App. A, eqs. (9)-(18)); epsilon tensor defined as antisymmetrized product of Kronecker deltas on the index set (0,1,2,3) (eq. (14)); non-cyclicity e.g. Tr(gamma5 gamma^{mu1..mu6}) - Tr(gamma^{mu6} gamma5 gamma^{mu1..mu5}) g_{mu1 mu6} = 8(D-4) epsilon (App. A)
  Symmetry argument: reading points restricted to types i_+ / i_I at the axial (A) vertices, outside maximal forests of the forest formula (Sec. 2, App. C); Bose-symmetrized sum over all axial couplings to the loop; Furry's theorem kept by the symmetric choice (i_I + i_+)/2 (eq. (2)); BRST identities from improved power counting: total-derivative argument (eq. (3)) and colour-trace antisymmetry arguments (eqs. (4)-(5)); Adler-Bardeen from screening of the A vertex by subdivergences (Sec. 4)
  Proved:
      - (argued) reading points inside maximal forests spoil subtraction of subdivergences; explicit two-loop illustration (Fig. 3)
      - (argued) gamma5-odd closed loops in anomaly-free theories acquire improved power counting so that no BRST-violating counterterms arise (Sec. 3, eqs. (3)-(5))
  Assumed:
      - anomaly-free representation (d_abc = 0 or cancellation over the representation)
      - existence of a reading point outside all subdivergences; the author shows (Fig. 9) a two-point function with overlapping (non-UV) singularities where no such point exists and an anomalous term 'is likely to appear', e.g. IR-divergent phase-space integrations
      - renormalizable theory; not applicable as is to non-renormalizable theories or IR regularization
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Abstract and Sec. 1 (claims); Sec. 2 (reading prescription, eq. (2), Figs. 1-8); Sec. 3 (BRST identities, eqs. (3)-(5), Figs. 10-16); Sec. 4 (Adler-Bardeen); Sec. 5 (conclusions: BM and new scheme claimed fully equivalent for physical amplitudes; Fierz generalization eq. (6); toy homology Delta^2 = 0, eqs. (7)-(8)); App. A (definition, eqs. (9)-(20)); App. B (forest formula, eqs. (21)-(23)); App. C (reading points)
      - refs: [6] Koerner-Schilcher-Kreimer Z. Phys. C54 (1992) 503; [4] Kreimer PLB 237 (1990) 59; [10] Larin PLB 303 (1993) 113
  Red flags:
      - arguments are proofs by power counting and diagrammatic reasoning, not an algebraic (QAP-based) proof; existence of an admissible reading point is not guaranteed for overlapping singularities (Fig. 9)
      - no rule given for products of several gamma5-odd loops or for anomalous subgraphs inside larger divergent graphs beyond the screening argument; later work finds additional counterterms needed for the singlet axial current at O(alpha_s^2) (S5.07_Chen2023)
      - the epsilon tensor restricted to (0,1,2,3) inside D-dim integrals; noncyclic effects are O(D-4) but multiply poles
  Cross-checks:
      - S5.07_Chen2023 finds that the scheme does not give the bare ABJ equation automatically at O(alpha_s^2) (contradicting the expectation of an automatic Adler-Bardeen proof)
      - used (as 'reading prescriptions') in the 4-loop SM strong-coupling beta function: S5.11, S5.12
  Access / confidence: full text via PDF (arXiv PDF fetched by WebFetch, read pages 1-21) / high
  Notes: Definition of the 'reading point' (where Trace evaluation starts) and the standard Bose-symmetric/Furry-symmetric prescription come from this paper.

[S5.03_Jegerlehner2001] F. Jegerlehner -- Facts of life with gamma5 (2001)
  arXiv: hep-th/0005255   Journal: Eur. Phys. J. C18 (2001) 673-679
  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5
  Claim: In D = 4 - 2 epsilon an anticommuting gamma5 with a cyclic trace is incompatible with the nonzero 4d trace Tr(gamma^mu gamma^nu gamma^rho gamma^sigma gamma5); one must choose between the trace condition and the Ward-Takahashi identities. Keeping a non-vanishing anticommutator makes the fermion propagator effectively 4-dimensional and fermion loops are then not regularized. Recommends for practice: strictly anticommuting gamma5 (NDR) for everything except hard anomalies, and treat gamma5-odd closed loops (VVA-type) as intrinsically 4-dimensional, fixed by Bose symmetry and vector-current conservation (Adler's approach), relying on Adler-Bardeen for higher orders.
  Loop order / theory: general; examples at 1 loop; NDR use claimed at 1-2 loops and beyond / electroweak SM
  Scheme: discussion of HV/BM, NDR, 'chirally improved HV' (AC(mu) != 0) and 'quasi self-chiral' (AC(mu) = 0) schemes
  Symmetry argument: gauge invariance (WT identities) prioritized over the trace condition; anomaly cancellation in the SM by quark-lepton duality
  Proved:
      - inconsistency argument for cyclic trace + anticommuting gamma5 + nonzero epsilon trace in D != 4 (eqs. (1)-(5))
      - non-regularization argument when AC(mu) != 0 (Sec. 3, eq. (31))
  Assumed:
      - that treating gamma5-odd loops as 4-dimensional with Bose symmetry and vector current conservation (eq. (45)) is sufficient at higher orders, via the Adler-Bardeen theorem
  Checked partially:
      - App.: one-loop SM self energies and vertices independent of AC terms
  Evidence:
      - Secs. 1-4 and App. (as listed); eqs. (1)-(5), (31), (45)
  Red flags:
      - does not analyse the non-cyclic (reading-point) trace formally; KKS/Kreimer cited only in reference lists
      - no explicit statement about 3-loop and higher SM diagrams with gamma5-odd traces (where the 4d treatment of subloops embedded in divergent graphs is precisely the issue)
  Cross-checks:
      - S5.02_Kreimer1994 (alternative: non-cyclic trace); S5.04_Larin1993 (HV + finite renormalization)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / medium
  Notes: Journal data verified on INSPIRE.

[S5.04_Larin1993] S.A. Larin -- The renormalization of the axial anomaly in dimensional regularization (1993)

  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5
  Claim: Practical multiloop gamma5 prescription: 't Hooft-Veltman gamma5 = i(1/4!) epsilon gamma gamma gamma gamma with the epsilon tensor kept outside the R-operation, symmetrized axial current J5 = i(1/3!) epsilon_{mu nu1 nu2 nu3} psi-bar gamma^nu1 gamma^nu2 gamma^nu3 psi; products of two epsilon tensors rewritten as D-dimensional metric determinants. [S5.05_AhmedChenCzakon2021] T. Ahmed, L. Chen, M. Czakon -- Renormalization of the flavor-singlet axial-vector current and its anomaly in dimensional regularization (2021)
  arXiv: 2101.09479   Journal: JHEP 05 (2021) 087
  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5

[S5.07_Chen2023] L. Chen -- An observation on Feynman diagrams with axial anomalous subgraphs in dimensional regularization with an anticommuting gamma5 (2023)
  arXiv: 2304.13814   Journal: JHEP 11 (2023) 030
  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5
  Claim: Reformulates Kreimer's anticommuting-gamma5 scheme with standard cyclic traces and a constructively defined gamma5-hat = -(i/4!) epsilon gamma gamma gamma gamma inserted at the boundary of the maximal 1PI non-singlet-type subgraph, averaging the two insertion points (eq. (1)) and, for closed chains with an odd number >= 3 of gamma5 vertices, over the vertices (eq. (3)). (22); gauge dependent at O(alpha_s^3)), so the bare ABJ equation does not hold automatically and the scheme does not give a constructive Adler-Bardeen proof.

  Checked partially:

  Evidence:

  Red flags:

      - epsilon-tensor products contracted in D dims: contraction order matters without the 4d Schouten identity; resolved only by a prescription (App. A.2)

  Cross-checks:

[S5.11_BednyakovPikelner2016] A.V. Bednyakov, A.F. Pikelner -- Four-loop strong coupling beta-function in the Standard Model (2016)
  arXiv: 1508.02680   Journal: Phys. Lett. B762 (2016) 151-156
  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5
  Claim: Four-loop beta function of g_s in the SM with g_s, y_t, lambda (electroweak gauge couplings neglected). The gamma5 ambiguity arises from 72 diagrams with two fermion traces containing gamma5 from Goldstone (chi, phi+-) Yukawa vertices, at order a_s^2 a_t^2 T_F^2. With anticommuting gamma5 and the trace cut at a 'reading point', three classes give different divergent results: A (both traces start/end at external vertices), B (only one), C (both at internal vertices); the divergent ambiguity is a_s^2 a_t^2 T_F^2/epsilon (X1 + X2 zeta3) (eq. (8)) with A giving (4/3 + 8 zeta3) (eq. (9)), B twice, C three times; in beta_3 it appears as R [16/3 + 32 zeta3] with 1 <= R <= 3 (eqs. (14), (18)). The authors adopt R = 3 (prescription C).
  Loop order / theory: 4 / SM, gaugeless limit (g1 = g2 = 0)
  Scheme: DREG, MS-bar, anticommuting gamma5 with non-cyclic traces cut at a reading point; cross-check with infrared rearrangement and BMHV algebra applied after tensor integration
  Symmetry argument: transversality of the finite gluon self-energy (cutting at external vertices breaks it), charge-conjugation consistency (eqs. (6)-(7), (12)-(13)), a Fierz-identity check (eq. (15)) and the IRR/BMHV recalculation independent of the cut point - all supporting R = 3
  Proved:
      - explicit 4-loop result up to the R-dependent term; xi independence; reproduction of pure QCD and gluino-model results
  Assumed:
      - R = 3 adopted on the basis of transversality/C-symmetry/Fierz arguments, not from a regularization proven consistent for chiral theories
  Checked partially:

  Red flags:

  Notes: This is the concrete 'reading point' ambiguity in the SM at four loops: R in {1,2,3}, coefficient R(16/3 + 32 zeta3) T_F^2 a_s^2 a_t^2.

  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5
  Claim: Osborn's equation d_I A-tilde = T_IJ beta^J (eq. (1.7)) relates the gamma5-ambiguous coefficients of the general 4-loop gauge beta function (g^(4)_199..202) to 3-loop Yukawa coefficients (y^(3)_304..308) that are taken as unambiguous: g199 = y304/6, g200 = y305/6, g201 = y306/6, g202 = y307/3, y307 = y308 (eq. (3.4)); with y304 = -24, y305 = -12, y306 = y307 = y308 = 8 - 24 zeta3 (eq. (4.1)) this gives g199 = -4, g200 = -2, g201 = 4/3 - 4 zeta3, g202 = 8/3 - 8 zeta3 (eq. (4.2)). For the SM strong coupling this yields (16 + 96 zeta3) T_F^2 a_s^2 a_t^2 (eq. (4.3)), i.e. R = 3 (eq. (4.4)): the trace must be cut at internal vertices. Presented as the first proof that it must be so.
  Loop order / theory: 4-loop gauge / 3-loop Yukawa / general 4d gauge-Yukawa theory; SM
  Scheme: MS-bar; the 3-loop Yukawa inputs from explicit SM computations (semi-naive gamma5); WCC assumed to hold for the 4d beta functions after epsilon -> 0 'for all perturbative renormalization schemes'
  Symmetry argument: Weyl consistency conditions; ABJ anomaly cancellation for other gamma5-odd structures (gauge and scalar betas spared at 3 loops)
  Proved:
      - given the WCC and the 3-loop Yukawa inputs: the 4-loop gauge gamma5-odd coefficients (eq. (4.2)) and R = 3 (eq. (4.4))
  Assumed:
      - validity of the WCC (local RG of Jack-Osborn) in the scheme actually used for the chiral SM, i.e. with an anticommuting/reading-point gamma5 that is not a consistent regularization of chiral gauge theories; not discussed
      - the 3-loop Yukawa gamma5-odd coefficients are unambiguous (single-pole argument of the semi-naive treatment)
      - no lower-order beta insertions contribute to the relevant A-tilde tensors (primitive, topologically symmetric graphs: cubes, Moebius ladders)
      - other diagrams vanish by anomaly cancellation
  Checked partially:
      - agreement with the independent explicit choice R = 3 of S5.11 (transversality arguments)
  Evidence:
      - eqs. (1.1)-(1.7), (3.1)-(3.4), (4.1)-(4.4)
  Red flags:
      - the argument fixes the coefficients by consistency, not by a regularization proven to be consistent; if the WCC derivation itself were scheme-sensitive in the gamma5 sector, the conclusion would be circular
      - the classification of relevant topologies at higher orders is stated to be incomplete; gamma5 may enter more complicated conditions there
  Cross-checks:
      - S5.11_BednyakovPikelner2016 (R = 3); S5.12_Zoller2016 (originally R = 1); used in S5.19 and S5.20, S5.21
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / high
  Notes: Lead verified.

[S5.22_JackOsbornSteudtner2024] I. Jack, H. Osborn, T. Steudtner -- Explorations in scalar fermion theories: beta-functions, supersymmetry and fixed points (2024)
  arXiv: 2301.10903   Journal: JHEP 02 (2024) 038
  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5
  Claim: General scalar-fermion beta functions and anomalous dimensions to three loops (MS-bar form, no new diagrammatic computation), constrained diagram by diagram by N=1 (4d Wess-Zumino, four supercharges) and formal N=1/2 (two supercharges, 3d-type) SUSY relations and by a-function consistency conditions; applied to epsilon-expansion fixed points. States that DR breaks SUSY but for N=1 the anomalous contributions should be removable by a scheme change, and that problems with extending SUSY away from integer dimensions do not arise up to three loops; in general d various SUSY Ward identities fail because they rely on Fierz identities relating graphs with different numbers of fermion loops.

[S5.23_Chen2025g5anchor] L. Chen -- A procedure g5anchor to anchor gamma5 in Feynman diagrams for the Standard Model (2025)
  arXiv: 2409.08099   Journal: JHEP 05 (2025) 109
  Section: 5 Standard Model >= 3 loops and gamma5
  Claim: Gives an algorithm that, for each closed fermion chain with an odd number of not-yet-defined gamma5 in a diagram, returns 'anchor points' (pairs of ordered fermion propagators at the I-leg head and O-leg tail of the maximal 1PI open-line subgraph) where gamma5 is anticommuted to and replaced by the 4d form gamma5-hat (eq. (2)), averaging the two placements (eq. (4)); traces are then cyclic and integrals done in conventional DR. Identifies limitations of the KKS/Kreimer reading-point schemes: no automatic ABJ/Adler-Bardeen for external axial anomalies; conditions implicit in Kreimer's scheme fail in the full SM at sufficiently high orders; gamma5-odd loops in multi-boson subgraphs with external scalars can become overall divergent, and for some 4-loop diagrams no qualified anchor choice seems to exist (Secs. 2.2, 2.4.1).
  Loop order / theory: proposed for SM calculations at least to two loops (JHEP version; arXiv v1 said three loops); survey of diagrams to three loops / SM
  Scheme: DREG with anticommuting gamma5 + anchored 4d gamma5-hat in gamma5-odd closed chains; D-dim metrics in contracted Levi-Civita pairs (Sec. 2.3.2); 't Hooft-type gauge condition (eq. (12))
  Symmetry argument: anticommutativity preserves WT identities away from anchors; Case-A gamma5-odd diagrams up to three loops argued to have no overall UV divergence (Sec. 2.3.1); anomaly cancellation
  Proved: -
  Assumed:
      - the 't Hooft-type gauge condition can be imposed to all orders (footnote 14: no systematic demonstration known)
      - sufficiency of a single D-dim metric in Levi-Civita contractions for Case-A diagrams (argued, not proven)
  Checked partially:
      - analytic arguments (traces, power counting, anomaly cancellation); a 3-loop Yukawa-type diagram (Fig. 6) with 1/epsilon poles not established to O(epsilon^0)
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 1 (limitations of KKS/Kreimer), Sec. 2.1 (procedure, eqs. (2), (4)), Sec. 2.2 (overall-divergent gamma5-odd loops at 4 loops), Sec. 2.3.1-2.3.2, Sec. 2.4 (Case-BB open), footnotes 14-15
  Red flags:
      - not proven to preserve BRST/gauge invariance nor shown equivalent to BMHV + symmetry-restoring counterterms
      - open: Case-BB diagrams (gamma5-odd loops inside overall-divergent 1PI amplitudes) - exactly the 4-loop beta-function situation
      - claimed validity range changed between arXiv v1 (three loops) and JHEP (two loops)
  Cross-checks:
      - continues S5.07_Chen2023 and S5.02_Kreimer1994; contrasts with the BMHV program (section 3 records: Stoeckinger-Weisswange 2312.11291 abelian 3 loops; Kuehler-Stoeckinger 2504.06080 SU(2) 2 loops)
  Access / confidence: full text via arxiv.org/html (first ~55 percent read) / medium
  Notes: Status as of 2026-11 (from this survey): the 4-loop SM gauge-beta gamma5-odd terms are fixed by WCC (S5.17, S5.19-S5.21), not by a regularization proven consistent; no independent BMHV computation of these 4-loop terms was found; reading-point/anchor schemes are proposed to two/three loops only; WCC-type full determination at 5-4-3 is stated to be open (S5.21).

6   Supporting literature for the synthesis: BV/QAP in DR, conformal symmetry in d != 4, all-order protection

[S6.01_Barnich1998] G. Barnich -- On the quantum Batalin-Vilkovisky formalism and the renormalization of non linear symmetries (1998)
  arXiv: hep-th/9807210   Journal: JHEP 9903 (1999) 010
  Section: 6a BV master equation / quantum action principle in DR and regularization-free BV
  Claim: Builds an extended antifield formalism containing all observables, with an extended master equation. If an invariant regularization exists, subtracted and effective actions satisfy the extended master equation to all orders independently of power counting. For dimensional regularization the evanescent breaking of the regularized master equation is coupled to a global fermionic source rho*, and the operator Delta_d = (tau/(-i hbar)) d^L/d rho* takes the role of the BV Laplacian; the formal BV Delta term vanishes in DR because delta(0)=0.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (structural); explicit bookkeeping of one- and two-loop anomalies / general Lagrangian gauge theories with non-linear (local or global) symmetries
  Scheme: Dimensional regularization with the conventions of the paper's ref. [32]; regulator parameter tau; breaking theta_tau = (1/2tau)(S_tau,S_tau) + (1/tau) Delta_c S_tau coupled to global source rho* (Sec. 2.1); renormalized quantum action principle taken from the cited literature.
  Symmetry argument: Quantum action principle applied to the regularized extended master equation: (1/2)(W,W) + Delta_c W - i hbar Delta_d W = A (eq. 131), consistency condition (W,A) + Delta_c A - i hbar Delta_d A = 0 (eq. 132); anomalies are BRST cocycles at ghost number 1.
  Proved:
      - With an invariant regularization (theta_tau = 0) the subtracted and effective actions obey the extended master equation (Sec. 2.2, eqs. 72-73).
      - In DR the would-be BV Laplacian term drops out (delta(0)=0); Delta_d is nilpotent, anticommutes with Delta_c and is a graded derivation of the antibracket (Sec. 2.8, eqs. 129-132).
      - One-loop divergences are cocycles at ghost number 0, the one-loop anomaly a cocycle at ghost number 1 (Theorem 6, Sec. 2.4, eqs. 85-89); two-loop anomaly is local and obeys a consistency condition (Sec. 2.6, eqs. 109-115).
  Assumed:
      - Renormalized quantum action principle in dimensional renormalization (taken from refs. [30-34] of the paper).
      - Knowledge of the relevant local BRST cohomology.
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2.1: theta_tau definition; eqs. (68)-(71) regularized master equation with source rho* and its QAP form.
      - Sec. 2.2: invariant case, eqs. (72)-(73).
      - Sec. 2.4-2.6: one- and two-loop anomalies, eqs. (85)-(115).
      - Sec. 2.8: quantum BV Delta operator in DR, eqs. (129)-(132); Delta_d defined after eq. (130).
      - Footnote 3 (Introduction): evanescent breaking terms of the regularized master equation are what make Delta non-trivial in dimensional renormalization.
  Red flags:
      - Delta_d exists only before the regulator is removed and with rho* kept non-zero; the formal BV Delta has no place in the final renormalized theory (Sec. 2.8).
      - No supersymmetric application.
  Cross-checks:
      - S3.02_BreitenlohnerMaison1977 (quantum action principle in DR)
      - S6.02_DPT1996 (BPHZ version)
      - S6.04_FredenhagenRejzner2011 (regularization-free version)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / high
  Notes: Gives the precise dictionary needed for a DR-based QME: in DR the entire 'quantum' part of the QME is the evanescent breaking insertion; this is the object that a BV computation of DRED or BMHV breaking has to evaluate.

[S6.02_DPT1996] F. De Jonghe, J. Paris, W. Troost -- The BPHZ renormalised BV master equation and two-loop anomalies in chiral gravities (1996)
  arXiv: hep-th/9603012   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B476 (1996) 559
  Section: 6a BV master equation / quantum action principle in DR and regularization-free BV
  Claim: Using Zimmermann normal products and BPHZ, the BV master equation is turned into a local quantum operator equation valid to all orders; anomalies are computed with it at one and two loops for chiral W3 gravity; the anomaly-computation method is usable outside BV.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (structure); explicit one and two loops / chiral W3 gravity (2d)
  Scheme: BPHZ subtraction with Zimmermann normal products (no regulator); quantum action principle of Lowenstein-Zimmermann type.
  Symmetry argument: Renormalized master equation as a normal-product operator identity; anomaly = local insertion obeying the Wess-Zumino consistency condition, whatever loop order it first appears at.
  Proved:
      - Local all-order form of the renormalized BV master equation in BPHZ (abstract).
      - Explicit two-loop anomalies in chiral W3 gravity (abstract).
  Assumed: -
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Abstract only; section/eq. no. not verified.
  Red flags:
      - Not a supersymmetric or four-dimensional gauge theory example.
  Cross-checks:
      - S6.01_Barnich1998
  Access / confidence: abstract only / medium
  Notes: Shows that a regulator-free (BPHZ) renormalized QME exists as a local operator statement; relevant as the scheme-independent benchmark against which DR-induced breakings are measured.

[S6.03_Hollands2007] S. Hollands -- Renormalized Quantum Yang-Mills Fields in Curved Spacetime (2007)
  arXiv: 0705.3340   Journal: Rev. Math. Phys. 20 (2008) 1033
  Section: 6a BV master equation / quantum action principle in DR and regularization-free BV
  Claim: Constructs perturbative quantum Yang-Mills theory on globally hyperbolic spacetimes: the algebra of gauge-invariant observables is the cohomology of the interacting BRST charge of an auxiliary gauge-fixed theory with ghosts and antifields; the key step is a hierarchy of renormalized Ward identities ensuring conservation of the interacting BRST current and nilpotency of the interacting BRST charge; OPE closes on gauge-invariant operators and RG flow stays in that space.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (formal power series) / pure Yang-Mills (no matter) on curved spacetime
  Scheme: Renormalization of local covariant time-ordered products (abstract does not name the scheme; the construction is of Epstein-Glaser type, not dimensional regularization).
  Symmetry argument: Renormalized (anomalous) Ward identities for the BRST current; anomalies controlled by BRST cohomology.
  Proved:
      - Ward identities at the renormalized level guaranteeing conserved BRST current and nilpotent BRST charge (abstract).
  Assumed: -
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Abstract only; section/eq. no. not verified.
  Red flags:
      - No matter, no supersymmetry; details of how potential anomalies are removed were not read here.
  Cross-checks:
      - S6.04_FredenhagenRejzner2011
  Access / confidence: abstract only / medium
  Notes: Rigorous regularization-independent existence result for a renormalized BRST/BV structure in 4d gauge theory; a supersymmetric analogue (constant SUSY ghosts) was not found in this survey.

[S6.04_FredenhagenRejzner2011] K. Fredenhagen, K. Rejzner -- Batalin-Vilkovisky formalism in perturbative algebraic quantum field theory (2011)
  arXiv: 1110.5232   Journal: Commun. Math. Phys. (DOI 10.1007/s00220-012-1601-1; volume/page not verified)
  Section: 6a BV master equation / quantum action principle in DR and regularization-free BV

  Loop order / theory: all orders (formal) / general perturbative gauge theories in the pAQFT framework
  Scheme: Epstein-Glaser renormalization of time-ordered products (no regulator).
  Symmetry argument: Renormalized QME expressed through the quantum BV operator; failure = anomaly of the anomalous Master Ward Identity.

[S6.05_Anselmi2014AB] D. Anselmi -- Adler-Bardeen theorem and manifest anomaly cancellation to all orders in gauge theories (2014)
  arXiv: 1402.6453   Journal: Eur. Phys. J. C74 (2014) 3083
  Section: 6a BV master equation / quantum action principle in DR and regularization-free BV
  Claim: In renormalizable, perturbatively unitary gauge theories with matter there is a subtraction scheme in which gauge anomalies cancel manifestly to all orders when they vanish at one loop (the scheme with no finite local subtractions from two loops on). The proof uses the BV formalism with a combined dimensional + higher-derivative regularization and no renormalization-group argument, hence covers conformal and finite theories.
  Loop order / theory: all orders / non-abelian gauge theory with left-handed fermions (worked model); right-handed fermions, scalars, photons in Sec. 8 (not read)
  Scheme: 'DHD' regularization: spacetime R^4 x R^(-eps), hatted evanescent components (Sec. 2); right-handed partners psi_R coupled through a matrix varsigma_IJ to regulate chiral propagators in all directions (eqs. 2.5-2.7); gauge-invariant higher-derivative factors (D^2/Lambda^2)^8 on F^2 and (D^2/Lambda^2)^3 on fermion kinetic terms, modified gauge fixing (eqs. 3.1-3.3); limits: eps -> 0 at fixed Lambda, then Lambda -> infinity (Sec. 3.1); gamma5 = i gamma0 gamma1 gamma2 gamma3 with continued Dirac algebra, no named gamma5 scheme.
  Symmetry argument: BV antibracket, master equation with gauge fermion (eqs. 2.2-2.4); anomaly functional A = (Gamma,Gamma) = <(S,S)>_S (eq. 2.11); regularized violation (S_r0,S_r0) = O(eps) (eq. 2.9) and DHD version (eqs. 3.5, 3.10, 4.2); one-loop anomaly split into non-evanescent, divergent-evanescent and evanescent parts (eqs. 4.3-4.4, 5.1); higher-derivative theory has only one-loop divergences (eqs. 3.11, 4.1).
  Proved:
      - Counterterms confined to one loop in the HD-regularized theory; the subtracted anomaly functional is convergent (Secs. 3-5).
  Assumed:
      - No analytically evanescent coefficients in the regularized action (Sec. 2.2).
      - No products of two or more fermion bilinears in the relevant functionals, to avoid Fierz ambiguities (Secs. 2.2, 2.6).
      - Perturbative unitarity and renormalizability.
  Checked partially:
      - The higher-loop cancellation step (Sec. 6) and the Lambda -> infinity limit (Sec. 7) were not read here.
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2: eqs. (2.2)-(2.19) BV setup, chiral regularization, evanescent terms S_ev.
      - Sec. 3: eqs. (3.1)-(3.11) DHD regularization and limit prescription.
      - Secs. 4-5: eqs. (4.1)-(5.1) one-loop anomaly decomposition.
  Red flags:
      - The Fierz restriction excludes exactly the structures on which supersymmetry invariance depends (Yukawa and gaugino Fierz identities); supersymmetry is not treated.
      - Existence statement: the scheme is characterized implicitly (no finite subtractions beyond one loop), not constructed explicitly for practical computations.
  Cross-checks:
      - S6.06_Anselmi2014chiralDR
      - S3.08_BIMS2020 and later BMHV works (explicit finite restoring counterterms instead of an existence proof)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (Secs. 1-5 only) / medium
  Notes: Closest existing template for an all-order BV/QME argument combined with DR; a supersymmetric version would have to lift the Fierz restriction, which is where DRED breaks (S2.10).

[S6.06_Anselmi2014chiralDR] D. Anselmi -- Weighted power counting and chiral dimensional regularization (2014)
  arXiv: 1405.3110   Journal: Phys. Rev. D89 (2014) 125024
  Section: 6a BV master equation / quantum action principle in DR and regularization-free BV
  Claim: Proposes a dimensional regularization for chiral and parity-violating theories in which spinors, vectors and tensors keep their physical-dimension components (standard gamma matrices), while propagators are regularized by evanescent higher-derivative kinetic terms (Majorana-type for chiral fermions); with weighted power counting the scheme is claimed consistent to all orders.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (claim) / chiral gauge theories (examples: renormalizability, Adler-Bardeen theorem)
  Scheme: 'Chiral dimensional regularization': physical-dimension index algebra, evanescent higher-derivative kinetic terms in the -eps directions.
  Symmetry argument: Weighted power counting controls the evanescent sector; gauge symmetry handled in BV (per the companion papers).
  Proved:

  Assumed: -
  Checked partially: -

[S6.10_BraunManashov2013] V. M. Braun, A. N. Manashov -- Evolution equations beyond one loop from conformal symmetry (2013)
  arXiv: 1306.5644   Journal: Eur. Phys. J. C73 (2013) 2544
  Section: 6b conformal symmetry in d != 4 (breaking and restoration in DR)
  Claim: At the critical coupling of a theory in non-integer dimension (O(n) phi^4 in d=4-2eps, su(n) phi^3 in d=6-2eps) the theory is taken to be exactly conformal; the collinear conformal generators acquire coupling-dependent corrections, and commutation relations with the evolution kernel determine the non-conformal part of the l-loop kernel from the (l-1)-loop correction to the special conformal generator; MS kernels are eps-independent, so results carry over to integer dimension.
  Loop order / theory: method to all orders; explicit checks at two/three loops in scalar theories / O(n) phi^4 (d=4-2eps), su(n) phi^3 (d=6-2eps); gauge theories announced for later work
  Scheme: Dimensional regularization at the Wilson-Fisher critical point u*, MS(-bar); kernels eps-independent in minimal subtraction (Sec. 2.2).
  Symmetry argument: Exact conformal invariance of the critical theory in d dimensions; special conformal Ward identity derived from the finite renormalized generating functional.
  Proved:
      - Generator corrections: S_- uncorrected (eqs. 44-46); Delta S_0 = -eps + (1/2) H(u*) (eqs. 39, 51); Delta S_+ = (z1+z2)(-eps + (1/2) u* H^(1)) + O(eps^2) at one loop (eqs. 39, 52-62, 80).
      - Consistency relations [S_+^(0), H(u*)] = [H(u*), Delta S_+] and [Delta S_+, S_-] = 2 Delta S_0 (eqs. 88-91).
      - A naive all-order guess for S_+ (eq. 81) fails; explicit extra term (1/4) u*^2 [H^(2), z1+z2] (eq. 83).
  Assumed:
      - The critical theory in non-integer d is exactly conformally invariant (Secs. 1, 2.3).
      - eps-singular terms of the Ward identity cancel because the renormalized generating functional is finite (Sec. 3).
      - Transfer to integer d via eps-independence of MS kernels (Secs. 1, 2.2).
  Checked partially:
      - Only scalar theories in this paper.
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 2.1 eqs. (2)-(4), (12)-(13): models and critical points.
      - Sec. 2.3 eq. (39); Sec. 3 eqs. (44)-(62), (80)-(83); Sec. 4 eqs. (88)-(91); eq. (103) eps-u* relation.
  Red flags:
      - Non-integer-d theories are non-unitary, so 'scale implies conformal' is not available; conformality is assumed (motivates S6.11).
      - Does not apply directly to N=4 SYM: with beta_4 = 0 the d-dimensional beta function -2eps a has only the trivial zero, so no interacting critical point exists in d = 4-2eps.
  Cross-checks:
      - S6.11_BMMS2018
      - S6.13_BelitskyMueller1998
      - S6.14_BKM2005
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv (first 100k characters; Secs. 1-4) / high
  Notes: Provides the precise form of 'conformal symmetry broken by eps and restored at the critical point': generator deformations of order eps and of order the kernel.

[S6.11_BMMS2018] V. M. Braun, A. N. Manashov, S. Moch, M. Strohmaier -- Conformal symmetry of QCD in d dimensions (2018)
  arXiv: 1810.04993   Journal: journal not verified (DESY 18-175)
  Section: 6b conformal symmetry in d != 4 (breaking and restoration in DR)
  Claim: Gives a perturbative argument that QCD in d = 4-2eps at the critical coupling a* is conformal in the gauge-invariant sector: the inhomogeneous term in the conformal variation of a renormalized gauge-invariant operator is itself gauge-invariant up to BRST-exact and EOM terms that drop out of correlators.
  Loop order / theory: all orders (perturbative argument) / QCD in d = 4-2eps at the critical point
  Scheme: MS in d = 4-2eps (eq. 5); multiplicative renormalizability assumed (Sec. 2); Landau gauge used for scale invariance of all Green functions (Sec. 5).
  Symmetry argument: Scale and conformal Ward identities: the bulk term carries beta(a) and vanishes at a* (eq. 24); products of the Lagrangian with renormalized operators decompose into gauge-invariant, BRST and EOM pieces (eqs. 26-28, 35; Joglekar-Lee); renormalized transformation law delta_K O_j = (delta_jj' K_Dj + 2 x^mu gamma_jj') O_j' + O^mu_j (eq. 44).
  Proved:
      - Vanishing of bulk contributions at beta(a*)=0 (eq. 24); dilatation identity F_j = - sum gamma_jj' O_j' (eq. 42); gauge invariance of the inhomogeneous term up to BRST/EOM terms (Secs. 4-5).
  Assumed:
      - Multiplicative renormalizability; existence of a*; diagonalizability of the anomalous-dimension matrix (fn. 2 warns it can fail with fermions).
      - Joglekar-Lee structure of gauge-invariant operator mixing.
  Checked partially: -
  Evidence:
      - Sec. 1 eqs. (1)-(3); Sec. 2 eqs. (4), (9)-(14), (21)-(23); Sec. 3 eqs. (16)-(25); Sec. 4 eqs. (26)-(35); Sec. 5 eqs. (38)-(44).
  Red flags:
      - Gauge fixing and ghosts break conformal symmetry already in d=4; only gauge-invariant correlators transform covariantly.
      - The authors flag that F Ftilde = dK shows inhomogeneous terms that are not gauge invariant can arise.
      - Evanescent operators, the d-dimensional energy-momentum tensor and gamma5 are not discussed.
  Cross-checks:
      - S6.10_BraunManashov2013
      - S6.12_BMMS2021 (gamma5 in Larin's scheme)
  Access / confidence: full text via ar5iv / high
  Notes: The BRST-exact/EOM bookkeeping here is exactly what a BV formulation organizes (antifield-dependent terms); a BV proof would replace the Joglekar-Lee input.

[S6.14_BKM2005] A. V. Belitsky, G. P. Korchemsky, D. Mueller -- Integrability of two-loop dilatation operator in gauge theories (2005)
  arXiv: hep-th/0509121   Journal: Nucl. Phys. B735 (2006) 17
  Section: 6b conformal symmetry in d != 4 (breaking and restoration in DR)
  Claim: Computes one- and two-loop dilatation operators in the non-compact SL(2) sector (three quark or gaugino fields of equal helicity with light-cone derivatives) of QCD and of N=1,2,4 SYM; two-loop spectra are integrable in planar SYM, and with extended supersymmetry the N-dependence factorizes into a c-number; conformal symmetry is violated from two loops on.
  Loop order / theory: 2 / QCD (SU(3)) and N=1,2,4 SYM (SU(Nc)), SL(2) sector
  Scheme: Computed in parallel in DREG (MS-bar) and DRED (DR-bar) (Sec. 4.1), light-like axial gauge with principal-value prescription (eq. 4.3); conversion eqs. (5.48)-(5.53).
  Symmetry argument: In d = 4-2eps the coupling runs with beta_eps(g^2) = -2eps + beta(g^2) (eq. 4.6), so conformal symmetry is broken for eps != 0 even for N=4; the breaking enters the dilatation operator from two loops; at L=3, V_DR^(1) = V^(1) - (1/6) V^(0) - (3/4)(4-N) 1l (eq. 5.54), the c-number term vanishing for N=4.
  Proved:
      - Two-loop dilatation operators in both schemes; DR-bar and MS-bar operators differ by a c-number/normalization at two loops (Sec. 5.4.1, eqs. 5.48-5.54).
  Assumed:

  Checked partially:
      - Sec. 6.1.3 (conformal Ward identity discussion for N=4) only partly read.
  Evidence:

      - Sec. 4.1, eq. (4.6).
      - Sec. 5.4.1, eqs. (5.48)-(5.54).
  Red flags:

  Cross-checks:
      - S6.13_BelitskyMueller1998

  Notes: For N=4 the only conformal-breaking parameter in d dimensions is the explicit -2eps (no critical point), exactly the term a d-dimensional BV conformal Ward identity has to carry.
